A functionality-preserving review of the whole repository at that commit: the 55 TypeScript files under `src/`
(11,149 lines), `src/style.css` (2,508 lines), `index.html`, the 53 unit test files (13,216 lines with their
helper), the fixtures, the integration and Chromatic tests, four story files and their helper, three shell
scripts, eight workflows and the configuration. The shared `@brain-bbqs/*` packages were read where the app
calls them and are not under review. No changes have been applied, and nothing from the repository was executed
for this review: the baseline is CI's last run on this commit (see Method & limits).

Confidence ratings are about the safety of a proposed change, not about whether the finding is real.

## Summary

This codebase is in good health, and most of what follows is residue and repetition, not disrepair. On every
point a tool can decide, the sweep came back clean: no orphan module, no export without a user, no unused local,
parameter or import, no class in the stylesheet that the source does not name, no lint, type or coverage
suppression and no `TODO`. CI's last run on this commit passed 959 unit tests in 53 files at 99.9% statement
coverage, beside an integration suite and two visual suites. Several things are built with care. The `avcC`
record is walked with a bounds check before every length read (`src/lib/chromaFormat.ts:44-63`). The ffmpeg core
is downloaded once and shared, and a failed download is forgotten so that the next attempt fetches it again
(`src/lib/ffmpegEngine.ts:65-81`). A cached measurement is keyed by the whole built command, so anything added to
the command builder later enters the key without the cache being told (`src/lib/matrixCache.ts:429-433`), and a
storage failure degrades the cache to no cache (`src/lib/matrixCache.ts:171-205`). The `src` link parameter is
accepted only as http or https (`src/lib/appUrl.ts:53-62`). Loading a comparison registers each input before
anything can throw and swaps the new set in only once every stretch has opened (`src/ui/abPanel.ts:65-105`). The
seek table is defined once for the page and the document (`src/lib/seekReport.ts:8-20`), and the ids in
`index.html` are checked against the element lookups in both directions (`tests/unit/elements.test.ts:15-17`).

The dominant theme is **residue of fast feature turnover**. The repository is three months and 69 commits old;
features were added, renamed and retired quickly, and each pass left something behind. The largest single item is
DC-1: the Full Analysis document builds 23 prose blocks and strips every one of them before anything is shown,
and about 206 lines of `src/` exist only for those blocks. Around it sit members that nothing reads (DC-2),
parameters no caller passes (DC-3), guards that cannot fire (DC-4), style rules that match nothing (DC-7),
fallbacks for the single-segment model that sample windows replaced (CON-2), names for tabs and engines that no
longer exist (CL-4), and 87 tabled comments, docstrings and one test title that do not describe the code beside
them, most because they describe an earlier design or were never true (CL-1 to CL-3).

The second theme is that **the page and the exported document are two hand-written copies**. The same facts are
listed once for the page's cards and once for the document, and the sweep's output enumeration is written three
times (DUP-1); 58 class selectors are styled in both stylesheets with nothing holding them equal (DUP-2). The
copies have already drifted: the document's Compare Quality table ignores the frame-rate axis (NV-10), and
several figures and labels differ between the two (NV-18).

The third theme is **a test suite that is large, green and less protective than its coverage figure suggests**.
`tests/` is outside every type check, and five places in it would not compile (NV-30). Scaffolding is copied
between test files, with drift (DUP-7), and the fixture that mirrors the command builder's defaults has fallen
behind them (CON-9). CL-6 and CL-7 list 39 places where a test's body cannot fail for what its title names, among
them the two tests that sit on the pool defects below. No coverage threshold holds the 99.9% (NV-35).

Smaller themes: helpers that exist in the shared packages or a file away are still written out by hand (DUP-4,
DUP-5); the workflows repeat their setup and pin Node 20 while four locked packages declare engines that exclude
it (DUP-10, NV-31); two of the project's own rules have slipped, with em-dashes on 239 lines (CL-9) and five
version bumps that have no changelog heading (NV-32); and the manual test template describes tabs the app no
longer has (CL-10).

**Needs verification holds what a maintainer should look at first**, because it is behaviour and not tidiness.
Run state is where the defects cluster. A retried square can be dropped without a word by the ffmpeg pool on a
machine with three or more CPUs (NV-1). The stop flag outlives the run it stopped, so the next square that needs
encoding answers "Stopped." (NV-3). A Run press refused because the other tab is busy leaves its button disabled
after that run ends (NV-4). Flipping the Educational switch rebuilds every panel under a live run (NV-6). The
A/B window's error banner can never be seen (NV-9). None of these was reproduced in a browser: each is a reading
of the code, confirmed by at least two independent readings, with the quickest confirmation stated in the item.

under the lower one on a tie. No finding, row or step is rated Low.

## Findings

### Dead code

#### DC-1: Every prose block built for the Full Analysis document is stripped before anything is shown

- **Location**: `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:452-456` (the filter), `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:510` (its one call), and the 23
  blocks at `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:116, 119, 130-131, 154, 156, 172, 181, 186, 188, 207, 222-223, 233, 259, 265, 275, 302, 329-334, 357, 375, 413-421`.
- **Issue**: `stripEducationalBlocks` drops every block of kind prose, whatever the Educational switch says
  (`.map((sec) => ({ ...sec, blocks: sec.blocks.filter((b) => b.kind !== "prose") }))`), and the one list it
  returns is what the preview, the HTML file, print, the Markdown file and the Markdown copy are all rendered
  from (`src/ui/analysisTab.ts:512, 532, 536-551`). `buildAnalysisSections` is module-private with no other
  caller. So the 23 prose block constructions (on 22 lines; line 375 holds two) run on every visit to the tab
  wherever their branch applies, and none reaches any output. This is the stated design since #25 (d3a7c25):
  the docstring above the filter says `That distinction is unconditional`, and `CHANGELOG.md:183` says
  `Full Analysis always leaves them out regardless`. About 206 lines of `src` exist only for these blocks
  (about 81 in `src/ui/analysisTab.ts`, 125 in `src/lib`, tabled below), plus the tests that pin them, and #35
  (fa82244) later added a comment and a docstring that read as if the prose were shown
  (`src/ui/analysisTab.ts:117-118, 250`).

<!-- prettier-ignore -->
| Exists only for the stripped blocks | Where | Tests that go with it |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Both renderer arms, `htmlToPlainText` (called only by the Markdown arm) and the `teachBox` import | `src/lib/analysisDoc.ts:15, 40-41, 73-90, 105-106` | `tests/unit/analysisDoc.test.ts:4, 33, 65-73, 89, 150` |
| The prose member of `AnalysisBlock` (its docstring counts six kinds) | `src/lib/types.ts:307-313, 321-322` | none |
| Five explainers that no page shows: `ATOM_STRUCTURE_TEACH`, `ENCODED_SEGMENT_NOTE`, `SIZE_SAVINGS_INTRO`, `sizeEstimateTeach`, the private `windowDifficultySentence`; one type import | `src/lib/explainers.ts:22, 500-505, 662-716` | `tests/unit/explainers.test.ts:10, 13, 148-205` |
| `SizeEstimate.basis` and `windowDifficulty`, read only by `sizeEstimateTeach` and its private helper (`src/lib/explainers.ts:676, 704`) | `src/lib/sizeEstimate.ts:27-32, 60, 79-84, 170, 198, 201, 214` | `tests/unit/sizeEstimate.test.ts:49, 58, 66, 83, 89, 105, 120, 134, 136` |
| The default of `teachBox`'s `mark`: the prose arm is the only one of 16 calls in `src` that passes none | `src/lib/dom.ts:163` | `tests/unit/dom.test.ts:179-181` |
| The document's value for `containerExplainer`'s `atomMapHref` (one production value is left, at `src/ui/inspectTab.ts:108`) and one of the two uses of `ATOM_MAP_TITLE` | `src/lib/explainers.ts:52-57`, `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:119, 250-251` | none; `tests/unit/explainers.test.ts:49-63` pass their own href |
| `--teach`, the `.teach` rules and `.teach code, .doc code` in the document stylesheet, and the `.teach` its docstring lists | `src/lib/analysisDoc.ts:205, 219, 326-341` | none |

- **Proposed change**: Decide the last bullet of NV-10 first. Two of the 23 blocks are captions written inline
  rather than explainer copy, the command's lead-in (`src/ui/analysisTab.ts:329-334`) and the matrix table's
  legend (`src/ui/analysisTab.ts:413-421`), and for those the other reading is that the stripping, not the
  prose, is the mistake: step 1 deletes the only copy of both strings. For the other 21 the stripping is the
  stated design, so a maintainer who doubts it as well has a product decision to take before step 1. Steps 1
  to 4 change no output at all. The steps are ordered so that each can compile by itself (INFERRED,
  not compiled):
  1. In `src/ui/analysisTab.ts`, delete the 23 constructions and what only they use: 21 of the imports at
     `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:24-46`, the `describeContainer` and `slugify` imports, and the `info` local (line 115).
  2. In one commit, delete `stripEducationalBlocks` (rewording the header at `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:5-7`, which
     points at it), both `case "prose":` arms, `htmlToPlainText`, the `teachBox` import and the union member,
     with the tests tabled for them, and take the document's prose blocks out of the list in the header comment
     at `src/lib/explainers.ts:15-16`. The member cannot go alone: the filter and the arms compare against it.
  3. Delete the five explainers and the type import, with their tests.
  4. Optional: remove `basis` and `windowDifficulty` with their nine assertions; make `mark` required, rewording
     the sentence about its default at `src/lib/dom.ts:157-159`; reword the two docstrings about the document's
     Atom Map link, keeping the `atomMapHref` parameter.
  5. Optional, and the one step that changes a byte of any output: delete the `.teach` CSS. The style block
     inlined into the preview and into each downloaded HTML file gets 17 lines shorter and draws the same. The
     rest of the document stylesheet is DC-7.
- **Confidence it's safe**: **High** for steps 1 to 3, **Medium** for step 4; step 5 draws the same (INFERRED)
  but changes exported bytes, hence optional. OBSERVED: a search for `buildAnalysisSections` returns its
  definition and line 510 only; the two renderers have no other caller in `src` or `stories`; the only other
  producer of a prose block is the fixture at `tests/unit/analysisDoc.test.ts:33`. Tried, to break it: a section
  made only of prose, which the second filter (`src/ui/analysisTab.ts:455`) would drop (none: every builder
  keeps a kv, badge, code, table or figure block); a side effect in a removed builder (all are pure string
  functions); a dependence on the switch (the only builders that read it are `infoIcon` and `teachBox`, at
  `src/lib/dom.ts:84, 165`, and neither runs for the document). The filter keeps order, so both renderers get
  the same list block for block. `noUnusedLocals` in the shared `tsconfig.base.json` makes `npm run typecheck`
  flag an import left behind. The existing pins of the stripping (`tests/unit/analysisTab.test.ts:396, 481`;
  Markdown only, nothing asserts the HTML) assert absence, so they pass either way and cannot catch a mistake
  here. Step 4 is Medium because five tests use `basis` to show which branch ran (each sits beside an
  `originalSegmentBytes` assertion that still pins it) and both fields are needed again if the prose returns.
  Step 5 rests on reading that the document cannot contain a `.teach` or `code` element: the shared `h()` sets
  text only, and the only non-empty `innerHTML` writes in `src` are `src/lib/dom.ts:93, 172` and the scratch
  element at `src/lib/analysisDoc.ts:80`. Nothing was rendered.
- **Verification**: after each step,
  `npm run typecheck && npm run lint && npm test && npm run build && npm run test:integration`. The test count
  should fall by seven over steps 2 and 3 (two `htmlToPlainText` tests, five `sizeEstimateTeach` tests) and by
  one more if step 4 makes `mark` required. Then load one file and compare both downloads before and after:
  apart from the generated time and the version stamp, the Markdown must be identical at every step, and the
  HTML identical until step 5, where only the style block may differ.

#### DC-2: Result fields, state and type members that nothing reads

- **Issue**: Each row is a member that is declared but that no production code reads or passes, that is read
  only inside the function that builds it, or (`gopOverride`) that never holds anything but its initial null; the
  last row is a type wider than any value assigned to it. A reader of the type has to rediscover that each time,
  some are computed for every estimate or kept for every frame of the loaded file, and tests pin values no user
  sees. The cleanup in #39 (913aa8f) removed members of this kind from `SampleInfo`, `SizeEstimate` and the
  mp4box declarations; the rows below are ones it left. Each was established by a whole-word search of `src`,
  `tests` and `stories`. `tests/` and `stories/` are outside the TypeScript project
  (`configs/tsconfig.json:6-14`), so a fixture key left behind after a removal passes silently: the test lines
  to edit are named in each row.

- **Proposed change**: As tabled, one row at a time; the rows are independent. Two neighbours are elsewhere:
  `AppState.file`, which mirrors the source's file, is in CON-1, and `resetFfmpeg` is DC-5.
- **Confidence it's safe**: **High** for every row but `is_sync` (**Medium**, a small rewrite, not a deletion).
  OBSERVED: the searches find no read of these members beyond the lines in the table. `src` contains no
  `JSON.stringify` and enumerates none of these objects, and the one stored record of an encode holds bytes,
  seconds and elapsed time only (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:422-426`), so nothing saved or exported changes shape.
  All of `src` is type-checked, so a read of one that this review missed fails `npm run typecheck`. `computeGop`
  returns the same number for every state the app can build: 13 assignments write `cli` fields
  (`src/ui/cliControls.ts:88-95`, `src/ui/encodeTab.ts:152-194`), none this one, and the one spread copy
  (`src/lib/qualityMatrix.ts:255`) carries the same null. The `hasBFrames` rewrite applies the same predicate to
  the same numbers. Removing `gopOverride` deletes two tests of a branch the app cannot reach, so the 959 tests
  of CI's last run on this commit become 957. INFERRED, not compiled or linted: that a `supportsRange` local
  assigned inside the `try` passes the lint rule `@typescript-eslint/no-unnecessary-condition`, and that
  `src/ui/fileLoading.ts:77` compiles unchanged against the narrowed `file`.
- **Verification**: `npm run typecheck && npm run lint && npm test`, then
  `git grep -nw -e segmentSavedBytes -e gopOverride -e bufferSizeDB -e keepMdatData`, which must print nothing.

  that cannot change a result. Several are left from callers that were removed, and some are kept alive only by
  a unit test of the unused arm, so the signatures promise more than the app does. Established by listing every
  call of each function in `src`, `tests` and `stories`.

#### DC-5: `resetFfmpeg` has no caller outside its own test

- **Location**: `src/lib/ffmpegEngine.ts:224-226`; the comments at `src/lib/ffmpegEngine.ts:62` and
  `src/ui/compareTab.ts:316`; the test lines `tests/unit/ffmpegEngine.test.ts:7, 115, 145, 371`.
- **Issue**: `export function resetFfmpeg(): void {` only forwards to `defaultFfmpegWorker.reset();`. A search of
  the whole tree for the name returns the definition, two comments that point at it, and four lines of its own
  test file. Its last production call, in the crash handler, was removed in #22 (d951965): since then a failed
  core calls its own `reset` (`src/lib/ffmpegEngine.ts:157`) and Stop resets each core in use
  (`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:487`). It is the one export in `src` with neither a production importer nor a use
  inside its own module; the other exports that only tests import are still called by their modules, and
  Not changed counts them.
- **Proposed change**: Delete the function. In the test, import `defaultFfmpegWorker` in its place and call its
  `reset()` at the three sites (at line 371 on the freshly imported module). Point the two comments at the
  worker's own `reset`. CON-3 inlines three more of the facade's forwarders; this deletion can go first or with
  it.
- **Confidence it's safe**: **High**. OBSERVED: no file in `src` imports it, and the only `vi.mock` of the module
  (`tests/unit/inBrowserEncode.test.ts:18`) does not list it, so the app cannot change. The test edit is outside
  the type check, so vitest is the detector: a call site left behind fails when it runs.
- **Verification**: `npm run typecheck && npx vitest run --config configs/vitest.config.ts tests/unit/ffmpegEngine.test.ts`,
  then `git grep -n resetFfmpeg`, which must print nothing.

  introduced it), so none was ever styled or queried. A reader who meets one goes looking for the rule or
  the test hook it implies and finds neither. Established by searching every tracked file and the published
  CSS, JavaScript and HTML fragments of the shared `@brain-bbqs/ui` package.

  four rows without a mark, where no element can match or nothing names the token: the four `infoIcon`
  call sites and every list in the sources were read (the one list built element by element,
  `src/ui/inspectToc.ts:28`, goes into the Inspect tab's contents `nav`); `git log -S` finds an SVG in no
  revision of `src/ui/matrixPanel.ts`; `item.wide` and `span 2` occur only on `src/lib/analysisDoc.ts:325`
  across all tracked files; `original` is never used as a class outside the one assignment. INFERRED for
  the rest, most of them by one of four arguments: a more specific rule sets the same property on every
  element the row reaches (`h1`, `.log-console`, `pre.cmd`); the value is already the one in force (the
  group head, `box-sizing`, both `break-inside` rows); the selector addresses a state no element can be in
  (the first-child reset); or a later rule of equal specificity always applies as well (the `90px`, whose
  600px query is contained in the 1560px one). The creators behind those arguments: three `h1` elements,
  two on the page and one inside the preview iframe, which has its own sheet; one page creator each for
  `pre.cmd` and `.log-console`, and none in a story. The `h1` row holds while the shared shell keeps
  `margin` and `text-align` on its header `h1` rule (its `styles/shell.css`, lines 13 to 17;
  `package-lock.json:215-216` pins 0.3.0 and `package.json:23` allows later 0.3 releases). The drop-zone
  row rests on flex layout, not on selector matching. The two Medium rows: the `.fold-note` declarations
  would still style a note if a caller passed one, which only `tests/unit/dom.test.ts:101` does, and
  dropping `original` is not a one-token edit because it is the value of a required positional parameter. A
  mistake would show as a visual change. The snapshots cover both `h1` elements, the collapsed drop zone,
  the body padding at the 390 px viewport and the savings bars; they do not cover the matrix table, the
  command block, the console, the fold bar or the document. No test reads the document's inlined stylesheet
  text beyond `tests/unit/analysisDoc.test.ts:112`.
- **Verification**: `npm run build` and `npm run format:check`, then `npm run test:chromatic` and the
  snapshot diffs of the two Chromatic workflows, which should be empty. By eye, for the panels no snapshot
  draws: the Reencode tab (command block and output console) and the Compare tab (a sweep over two
  resolutions for the block titles, and the selected square's command), and for the `.fold-note` row the
  bar of the seeking test's table fold on the Inspect tab. For the document rows,
  `npx vitest run --config configs/vitest.config.ts tests/unit/analysisDoc.test.ts` and a look at the Full
  Analysis preview and its print preview.

#### DC-8: Twelve icon files that nothing references

- **Location**: `src/assets/encoding-helper-favicon-48.png`, `src/assets/encoding-helper-favicon-64.png`,
  `src/assets/encoding-helper-icon-48.png`, `src/assets/encoding-helper-icon-64.png`,
  `src/assets/encoding-helper-icon-128.png`, `src/assets/encoding-helper-icon-180.png`,
  `src/assets/encoding-helper-icon-192.png`, `src/assets/encoding-helper-icon-256.png`,
  `src/assets/encoding-helper-icon-512.png`, `src/assets/encoding-helper-maskable-192.png`,
  `src/assets/encoding-helper-maskable-512.png`, `src/assets/encoding-helper-maskable.svg`
- **Issue**: Twelve of the 23 files in `src/assets` are referenced by nothing. A search for each base name
  over every tracked file returns no line, against one or two for each of the other eleven. `index.html:7-12`
  links the six icon files the page declares (`encoding-helper-favicon.svg`,
  `encoding-helper-favicon-32.png`, `encoding-helper-favicon-16.png`, `favicon.ico`,
  `encoding-helper-favicon-180.png` and `encoding-helper-favicon-mono.svg`). The twelve arrived in one
  commit (c4a522c, "Add files via upload") as part of a full icon set of nineteen files: favicon PNGs at 48
  and 64 px, app icons from 48 to 512 px, two maskable icons and their SVG source, the kind a web app
  manifest lists. The repository has never had a web app manifest: `git log --oneline -S webmanifest` and
  `git log --oneline -S 'rel="manifest"'` find no commit. The eleven PNGs total 168,074 bytes and the SVG
  774; they cost clone size and a reader's attention.
- **Proposed change**: Delete the twelve files. If an installable-app manifest is planned, keep them and say
  so in a line of the README instead. The files are under `src/`, so the deletion needs the version bump the
  project rule asks for (the version check runs on `src/**`, `.github/workflows/version-check.yml:7-11`).
- **Confidence it's safe**: **High**. OBSERVED: no reference by name anywhere in the tracked tree, the
  lockfile and the changelog included, and none in the content of any commit. Nothing picks the folder up
  wholesale: there is no glob import, no `publicDir` or Storybook `staticDirs` setting
  (`configs/storybook/main.ts:3-5` passes only `packageJson`) and no copy step in `configs/`, `scripts/`
  or the workflows. No test or story names the files. INFERRED: Vite emits only assets that are imported
  or linked from `index.html`, so these files are not in the build today and deleting them changes
  nothing that is built or served; no build was run for this review. If the claim were wrong, the build's
  asset list would shrink or a browser run would report a missing icon.
- **Verification**: `npm run build` (the list of emitted assets is unchanged), then
  `npm run test:integration` and `npm run test:chromatic`.

  exist: a reader of `sweepOf` assumes some test sweeps more than one stretch, and no test in that file
  does. Established by counting the calls of each helper, searching `src`, `tests` and `stories` for every
  reader of each assigned value, and reading the hook each reset duplicates. Tests that cannot fail are a

  and its history. Nothing was executed, and two rows depend on tool behaviour that could only be inferred.

### Duplication

#### DUP-1: The page and the exported document state the same facts from two hand-written lists

- **Issue**: The Full Analysis document rebuilds in `src/ui/analysisTab.ts` the fact lists that the Inspect,
  Compare and Reencode panels already build in their own modules: the same labels and the same value
  expressions, typed twice, although both sides end in one element builder (`gridItem`, which the document
  reaches through `src/lib/analysisDoc.ts:37`). The document's builders say what they are
  (`src/ui/analysisTab.ts:161` begins `Mirrors the Inspect tab's card`), but nothing ties a copy to its
  source, so a row changed on one side stops matching the other without any signal. That has already
  happened: d951965 (#22) added a factor to the page's "Change" row and fa82244 (#35) added a "Chroma" row
  to the page, and the document's lists got neither (the rows that differ today are in NV-18); the
  document's copy of the sweep loop also lacks the frame-rate axis that the page's copy has (NV-10). The
  table is a label-by-label comparison of every pair. Part of this is already solved the right way:
  `overviewTitle`, `flattenMetadataTags`, `describeSampledStretches` and everything in
  `src/lib/seekReport.ts` are single definitions that both sides use, and the header of that file gives
  the reason: `the page and the document cannot drift apart on a column header or a decimal`.

- **Proposed change**: For each row marked yes, export one function from the page module that owns the card
  and build both renderings from what it returns: rows of label and value, plus the page's optional `sm`
  and `info` hints (seven of those rows use one or both), which the document ignores because it applies its
  own length rule (`src/lib/analysisDoc.ts:29-31`). The page maps the rows to `gridItem` calls, the document
  to a `kv` block. The badges become a tone and a text; the bitrate card gets one function that says which
  of the three cases applies, so the two `if` ladders cannot diverge; the titles become constants exported
  from `src/ui/inspectTab.ts` and `src/ui/seekTab.ts` (not from `src/ui/analysisTab.ts`: it imports both
  modules at `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:77-78`, so an import back would close a cycle). For the sweep, export
  one function from `src/lib/qualityMatrix.ts` that lists the outputs for given resolutions, kernels and
  rates, and call it from `buildMatrixCombos` and `renderMatrixTable` with each site's own arrays; the
  document can call it with `[null]` for the rates and print exactly what it prints today, which leaves
  the missing axis as one visible argument for NV-10 to fill. Leave every row marked NV-18 alone: sharing
  it would change what one side prints. Leave the TSV export alone as well: its column list
  (`src/ui/seekTab.ts:188-201`) runs parallel to the table's (`src/lib/seekReport.ts:9-20`) but is a
  different format on purpose (units in the header, blanks for missing values, three decimals), pinned at
  `tests/unit/seekTab.test.ts:350-358`. The sweep row head is a separate, optional step:
  `comboCrf(quality)` on the page gives the same text for every grid the app can hold, but the expectation
  `"high (CRF ?)"` at `tests/unit/matrixPanel.test.ts:109` would have to read CRF 18 instead, which changes
  what that test pins. Line 97 of the headline strip is in DUP-4. The edits are under `src/`, so the
  version-bump rule applies.
- **Confidence it's safe**: **Medium**. OBSERVED: for every row marked yes the labels, the value expressions
  and their order were compared character by character and are equal. `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:74-78` already
  imports the page modules, so exporting from them adds no import cycle. The sweep helper keeps each
  caller's arrays and the same nesting (rate, resolution, kernel); `git grep -n "slice(0, 1)" -- src tests stories`
  returns exactly the three lines being merged, and the rule is pinned for the builder at
  `tests/unit/qualityMatrix.test.ts:75-78` and for the page at `tests/unit/matrixPanel.test.ts:63-103`. The
  document's sweep test (`tests/unit/analysisTab.test.ts:516-531`) ticks a single kernel, so it pins the two
  output rows but would pass without the rule. The atom block class strings are pinned in order at
  `tests/unit/atomsTab.test.ts:171-172` and `tests/unit/atomsTab.test.ts:195-197`. It is Medium, not High,
  because the suite would not catch every slip. No test or story names "Quietest Window" or
  "Peak ÷ Average"; the overview's "File Size" label is matched only inside "Original File Size"
  (`tests/unit/savingsPanel.test.ts:92`); "Overall Bitrate" is named by a test on the page side only
  (`tests/unit/inspectTab.test.ts:486`) and "MIME Type" on the document side only
  (`tests/unit/analysisTab.test.ts:622`). CL-7 lists the rows that run without a value assertion. Several
  modules change at once, and the output is a file users keep. A shared row type that dropped `sm` or
  `info` would change the page, not the document.
- **Verification**: `npm run typecheck && npm run lint`, then
  `npx vitest run --config configs/vitest.config.ts tests/unit/inspectTab.test.ts tests/unit/seekTab.test.ts tests/unit/atomsTab.test.ts tests/unit/analysisTab.test.ts tests/unit/analysisDoc.test.ts tests/unit/qualityMatrix.test.ts tests/unit/matrixPanel.test.ts tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts tests/unit/abPanel.test.ts`
  and `npm run test:integration`. For the rows no test pins, download the Markdown of one demo file before
  and after: only the line that begins `_Generated` should differ. Pinning those rows first (CL-7) removes
  the reason for Medium.

#### DUP-2: The page stylesheet and the document stylesheet share 58 selectors with nothing holding them equal

- **Location**: `src/style.css` (the page) and `src/lib/analysisDoc.ts:209-469` (the document's sheet,
  inlined into every exported file)
- **Issue**: The document reuses the page's class names so that one set of section builders serves both
  (`src/lib/analysisDoc.ts:204-206`), so every rule for those classes exists twice: 58 class selectors are
  defined in both sheets. With each light palette resolved, 31 carry equal declarations, 11 are equal where
  they overlap and have extras on one side or both, and 16 differ in a shared property (four of the 58 are
  `.teach` selectors that DC-1's optional last step deletes from the document, after which the figures are
  54, 29, 11 and 14). Nine of the 16
  differ only in `font-family`, `font-size`, `letter-spacing`, `margin`, `gap`, `border-radius` or
  `display`. The other seven differ in a colour, with no comment saying why: the info badge (`#e8effe` at
  `src/lib/analysisDoc.ts:352` against `var(--accent-tint)` at `src/style.css:711`), the axes of the two
  charts (`src/lib/analysisDoc.ts:445` against `src/style.css:1080`), their axis titles
  (`src/lib/analysisDoc.ts:447` against `src/style.css:1088`), the bold text of a teach box
  (`src/lib/analysisDoc.ts:335` against `src/style.css:739`; never seen, since the document strips its
  teach boxes at `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:452-456`) and the command block, through the recessed surface
  `--sunken` (`src/lib/analysisDoc.ts:218` against `src/style.css:29`). They are not drift:
  `git show cfaf80f:src/lib/analysisDoc.ts`, the commit that created the sheet (#17), has all five values,
  and the page had today's values then. The cost: the one promise the code makes,
  `re-step it there and here together` for the atom-map palette (`src/lib/analysisDoc.ts:378`), is kept by
  hand, and no test compares the two sheets.
- **Proposed change**: Keep two sheets and add a guard: a unit test that reads both as text (the document's
  is the content of the `<style>` element written at `src/lib/analysisDoc.ts:179-181`), resolves each light
  palette and asserts that the selectors meant to match carry equal declarations, with the intended
  differences listed in the test. Start with the 31 that are equal today, which include the four atom-map
  family rules. One partial imported by both sheets is not proposed, because it is not
  behaviour-preserving: an import must come first (`src/style.css:4-6`), which moves `.badge`
  (`src/style.css:695`) ahead of `.info` (`src/style.css:609`) and flips the equal-specificity tie that
  keeps a badge that also has the class `info` an inline block; see NV-37. Whether those colours should
  match the page changes the exported file and is a maintainer's decision.
- **Confidence it's safe**: **Medium**. A test changes no shipped byte. The risk is in the test: it has to
  record which of the 27 unequal selectors are intended (the colours above are undecided), and comparing CSS
  as text can fail on a harmless reformat. OBSERVED: the inventory was computed by script (top-level rules,
  grouped selectors split, custom properties resolved against each sheet's `:root`; `*`, `:root`, `a` and
  `body` left out) and recomputed with a second, separately written script: 58, 31, 11 and 16 both times.
  Nothing under `tests`, `stories` or `configs` reads either sheet beyond the import at
  `configs/storybook/preview.ts:1` and the check that a `<style>` element exists
  (`tests/unit/analysisDoc.test.ts:112`). INFERRED: the cascade argument against the partial; nothing was
  rendered.
- **Verification**: `npm test` (the new test should pass on the tree as it is), then `npm run lint` and
  `npm run format:check`.

#### DUP-3: Repeats inside `src/style.css`: the dark palette twice and three disclosure bars

- **Location**: `src/style.css:84-136` and `src/style.css:138-187` (the dark palette);
  `src/style.css:1316-1334`, `src/style.css:1376-1394` and `src/style.css:1840-1857` (the three bars)
- **Issue**: (1) The dark palette is written once for the system preference (inside
  `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark)`) and once for the header toggle (`:root[data-theme="dark"]`): 48
  custom properties in each block, identical in name, value and order. CSS cannot share a declaration block
  between a media query and an attribute selector, so the repeat is deliberate, and the comment at
  `src/style.css:22-23` asks for the two blocks to stay identical. Nothing checks it, and a colour changed
  in one block only gives a dark theme that depends on how it was switched on. (2) The fold, the output
  console and the sweep settings repeat three wrapper declarations (`src/style.css:1318-1320`,
  `src/style.css:1378-1380`, `src/style.css:1842-1844`) and six summary declarations (`display`,
  `align-items`, `gap`, `color`, `cursor`, `list-style`) under the same two-line comment, although their
  caret is already grouped (`src/style.css:1335-1361`). The copies are not quite equal: two summaries set
  `user-select: none` (`src/style.css:1330`, `src/style.css:1390`); the sweep bar, the oldest of the three
  (28bc126, #21), never had it. The playhead gradient written for both slider thumbs
  (`src/style.css:1664-1671`, `src/style.css:1679-1686`) is the usual vendor pair and needs no change.
- **Proposed change**: (1) A unit test that reads `src/style.css` as text, extracts the custom properties of
  the two dark blocks and asserts that the lists are equal; `tests/unit/main.smoke.test.ts:15` already
  reads a repository file this way. (2) Move the three wrapper and the six summary declarations, with one
  copy of the comment, into grouped rules beside the caret rules. Keep each bar's padding, font size,
  margins and `user-select` as they are: giving the sweep bar `user-select: none` would change behaviour
  and is not proposed.
- **Confidence it's safe**: **Medium**. The test changes no shipped byte, and a script comparing the two
  blocks finds 48 equal declarations, so it should start green. For the regrouping, OBSERVED: besides the
  rules being merged, the only rules in `src/style.css` that reach the three wrappers or their summaries
  are at `src/style.css:1336-1361` and `src/style.css:1867-1870`, and on the elements themselves they set
  none of the nine properties except the hover colour, which is more specific than the base colour
  wherever that sits; the two shared sheets the file imports (`src/style.css:5-6`) have no rule for
  `details`, `summary` or the three class names. INFERRED: with unchanged selectors and no competing rule,
  moving a declaration cannot change what applies. No story and no Chromatic snapshot draws any of the
  bars, so a slip would go unseen.
- **Verification**: (1) `npm test`. (2) `npm run build` and `npm run format:check`, then by eye in both
  themes: the "Sampled timestamps" fold after a seeking test, the output console after a sample run, and
  the "Settings to sweep" bar, closed and open.

#### DUP-4: Inline copies of helpers that already exist

- **Issue**: Each row is a few lines that spell out by hand what an exported helper, or a sibling a file
  away, already does. Every copy was compared with its counterpart for value and order and is equal today,
  so the cost is the usual one: the next change lands in one place only. The preset list shows the risk,
  since the Reencode tab's copy is a plain string array that nothing checks against the type (the select's
  value is cast back at `src/ui/encodeTab.ts:162`). Confidence is given per row.

| `src/ui/encodeTab.ts:97`: the nine x264 presets as a string literal | `MATRIX_PRESETS` (`src/lib/qualityMatrix.ts:47-57`): the same nine in the same order, typed against the union at `src/lib/types.ts:105-106` | pass `MATRIX_PRESETS` as the options | High |
| `fmtRate(...)` followed by " fps" at `src/lib/qualityMatrix.ts:139`, `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:97`, `src/ui/compareTab.ts:697`, `src/ui/matrixPanel.ts:219` | `describeFrameRate` (`src/lib/format.ts:37-39`) | call it (every site is already behind a guard); the four `fmtRate` imports then go | High |
| Keyframe snap at `src/ui/segmentRun.ts:148-151` and `src/ui/samplePicker.ts:80-83` | each other: both return the keyframe at or before a time, or the time itself | one pure helper beside `nearestKeyframeAtOrBefore` in `src/lib/mp4boxParser.ts` | High |
| Ruler edge class at `src/ui/abPanel.ts:239` and `src/ui/samplePicker.ts:68-69` | each other: a nested and a split form of the same choice | one helper in `src/lib/sampleTimeline.ts`, which both files import | High |
| Sum of blob sizes at `src/ui/compareTab.ts:662`, `src/ui/compareTab.ts:668`, `src/ui/abPanel.ts:103` and `src/ui/segmentRun.ts:413` | `heldBytes` (`src/lib/qualityMatrix.ts:292-294`); and the call on `src/ui/compareTab.ts:657` already returns the same sum as `bytes`, which is dropped | keep the call's result and assign its `bytes`; export the helper for the two `??` fallbacks | High |
| `e instanceof DOMException` with the name `AbortError`, the same line at `src/ui/fileLoading.ts:156` and `src/lib/save.ts:25` | each other | optional: one local predicate, not the shared `isInterruption` | High |
| `src/lib/educational.ts:16-23` and `src/lib/educational.ts:42-47`: `localStorage` in hand-written try and catch | `readStorageItem` and `writeStorageItem` of `@brain-bbqs/utils` | call them, with a no-op error handler on the write | High |

- **Proposed change**: Apply the Change column; the edits are under `src/`, so the version-bump rule applies.
  Notes on the rows that need care:
  - Blob sum. Only `src/ui/segmentRun.ts:413` and `src/ui/compareTab.ts:662` run in the app; the other two
    (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:668`, `src/ui/abPanel.ts:103`) sit behind a `??` whose left side the app always sets
    (DC-4 lists them, with the helper's own call at `src/lib/qualityMatrix.ts:298`, as arms that cannot fire).
    The helper must not move to
    `src/lib/sizeEstimate.ts`: that module imports `state` (`src/lib/sizeEstimate.ts:24`), and
    `src/lib/state.ts:98` reads constants of `src/lib/qualityMatrix.ts` while it loads
    (`src/lib/state.ts:42`), so an import of `sizeEstimate` by `qualityMatrix` closes a cycle that fails

    `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:60` ahead of `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:70-72`). `src/lib/qualityMatrix.ts` itself
    or the import-free `src/lib/format.ts` are safe homes. `src/ui/abPanel.ts` is not: two test files

    not a candidate for `resetProgressFill`: the tab already holds the fill element, and the helper would
    hand back a nullable one.
  - AbortError. `isInterruption` (the shared package's `interrupt.js`, lines 22 to 29) also accepts its own
    error class, any object with that name, and a second name; it means that the visitor pressed Stop,
    which is a different idea from closing a file dialog.
  - Keyframe snap. Not exported from `src/ui/segmentRun.ts`: `tests/unit/samplePicker.test.ts:13` mocks
    only mediabunny, and that import would bring the ffmpeg modules into the picker's tests.
  - Presets. Deriving the union from one `as const` list is a wider edit and is not proposed.
- **Confidence it's safe**: **High** for every row; two steps are INFERRED. OBSERVED, row by row: the
  helper returns `null` where the inline lookup has `undefined`, the guard treats both alike, no test
  replaces `src/lib/state.ts`, and `tests/unit/encodeTab.test.ts:59-64` expects an empty panel for a file
  with no video track, which both forms give. `fieldSelect` only iterates its options
  (`src/ui/formControls.ts:30-36`); `git grep veryslow -- src stories` finds the three lists and no fourth;
  `MATRIX_PRESETS` is never mutated. `describeFrameRate` is `fmtRate` plus " fps" for a non-null rate
  (`src/lib/format.ts:38`), and a leftover import fails the type check (`noUnusedLocals`, line 15 of the
  shared config's `tsconfig.base.json`). The two snaps choose the same branch, and the two class forms give
  the same string because a position cannot be both under 2 and over 98. `src/ui/segmentRun.ts:410` throws
  when a blob is missing, so the `bytes` returned on line 413 is the plain sum over the same array, and no
  test that fakes the encoder selects a sweep square. The storage wrappers and the helpers were compared
  case by case (absent key, "true", "false", other text, a throwing read, a throwing write): same result,
  same stored strings; `localStorage` occurs in code only on `src/lib/educational.ts:18` and
  `src/lib/educational.ts:43`, and no test mocks `@brain-bbqs/utils`. `section()` builds the same `div`,
  class and `h2`. INFERRED: the seek field gains one attribute, a step of 1, which is the default step of a

  (`tests/unit/seekTab.test.ts:75`, `tests/unit/inspectTab.test.ts:251`,
  `tests/integration/smoke.spec.ts:160`). The module-order failure is read off the import lines and the
  rules of module evaluation, not run; neither `tsc` nor the lint configuration detects import cycles.
- **Verification**: `npm run typecheck && npm run lint`, then
  `npx vitest run --config configs/vitest.config.ts tests/unit/encodeTab.test.ts tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts tests/unit/matrixPanel.test.ts tests/unit/qualityMatrix.test.ts tests/unit/format.test.ts tests/unit/samplePicker.test.ts tests/unit/segmentRun.test.ts tests/unit/abPanel.test.ts tests/unit/analysisTab.test.ts tests/unit/educational.test.ts tests/unit/seekTab.test.ts tests/unit/inspectTab.test.ts tests/unit/demosPage.test.ts tests/unit/save.test.ts tests/unit/fileLoading.test.ts tests/unit/main.smoke.test.ts`,

#### DUP-5: UI wiring written more than once

- **Issue**: Seven pieces of control wiring are written two to six times: a click handler copied between the
  two encode tabs, listeners that differ in one key, a helper rebuilt beside itself, predicates spelled out
  at each use. Each row was compared line by line. None is a defect on its own, but two rows show what the
  copies cost: the label head (row 3) has differed from the helper it copies since it was written (28bc126,
  #21), and the Run handler (row 1) carries the defect of NV-4 in both copies, so its fix would have to be
  made twice.

  nothing is awaited between lines 316 and 367, and the test for a track of unknown size expects four zeros
  and an aspect ratio of `0 / 0` (`tests/unit/abPanel.test.ts:721-722`), which the named values give. Row 5:
  each group was checked against the status union (`src/lib/types.ts:169`); the negated forms are exact
  complements. Row 7: `loadSource` reads only `kind`, `file`, `url` and `name` from the request
  (`src/ui/fileLoading.ts:72-77`, `src/ui/fileLoading.ts:116`), and a missing name and an undefined one
  both reach `ChunkedSource.fromUrl` as `undefined`, which `tests/unit/fileLoading.test.ts:182` and
  `tests/unit/fileLoading.test.ts:287` already assert. Row 3 is Medium because it changes the DOM of one
  field and rests on a reading of the stylesheet: a bare label carries a 4px bottom margin, and inside
  `.field-head` the head carries the 4px and the label none (`src/style.css:462-482`), so the spacing
  should be the same (INFERRED, not rendered); no test queries the Compare tab's `.field-head`, and
  `tests/unit/formControls.test.ts:41` pins the helper's form without a wrapper. Row 6 is Medium because
  the helpers' homes are constrained by which modules the tests mock, which is easy to get wrong for a
  small gain.
- **Verification**: `npm run typecheck && npm run lint`, then
  `npx vitest run --config configs/vitest.config.ts tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts tests/unit/encodeTab.test.ts tests/unit/segmentRun.test.ts tests/unit/formControls.test.ts tests/unit/abPanel.test.ts tests/unit/matrixPanel.test.ts tests/unit/qualityMatrix.test.ts tests/unit/analysisTab.test.ts tests/unit/inBrowserEncode.test.ts tests/unit/fileLoading.test.ts tests/unit/demosPage.test.ts tests/unit/main.loaded.test.ts`.
  For row 3, `npm run build` and a look at the open "Settings to sweep" fold: the "Quality levels" label
  should sit where it did.

#### DUP-6: The two SVG charts carry the same axis scaffold

- **Location**: `src/ui/bitrateChart.ts:130-176` (constants at `src/ui/bitrateChart.ts:13-23`) and
  `src/ui/seekTab.ts:232-277` (constants at `src/ui/seekTab.ts:212-219`)
- **Issue**: The bitrate chart and the seek scatter build the same scaffold in the same order: for each step
  two grid lines and two tick labels, then two axis lines and two axis titles. Read attribute by attribute,
  the keys are the same in the same order and the value expressions are the same once `PLOT_BOTTOM` is read
  as `MT + plotH` and `PLOT_RIGHT` as `ML + plotW` (compare `src/ui/bitrateChart.ts:135-136` with
  `src/ui/seekTab.ts:236-237`). Only the height (260 and 280), the left margin (64 and 56), the two tick
  formatters and the two titles differ. The scatter came first; 54e1c6e (#16) added the bitrate chart with
  the same scaffold. The stylesheet rules of the two charts are already grouped
  (`src/style.css:1074-1089`); the builders are not.
- **Proposed change**: One function that appends the scaffold for a given geometry, two tick formatters and
  two titles, exported from `src/ui/bitrateChart.ts` or a small chart module and called by both builders.
  Keep the element order, the attribute key order and the arithmetic as they are.
- **Confidence it's safe**: **Medium**. OBSERVED: the two scaffolds were compared attribute by attribute, and
  every coordinate in both is a whole number (the plot areas are 520 by 204 and 528 by 224, cut into four
  steps), so regrouping the arithmetic cannot move a value. The shared `svgEl` helper of `@brain-bbqs/ui`
  sets attributes in the order of the object's keys (its `dom.js`, lines 19 to 26), so with the order kept
  the markup, which the exported document embeds, should stay byte-identical (INFERRED, not serialised
  here). It is Medium because the tests would not notice a slip. They pin the tick texts and their
  interleaved order, the titles and the scatter's point positions (`tests/unit/bitrateChart.test.ts:71-75`,
  `tests/unit/seekTab.test.ts:394-413`), but no test reads a grid line, an axis line, a tick position, a
  font size or the title's `transform`, and no Chromatic snapshot draws the scatter: the only one with a
  file loaded stops at the Inspect tab before any seeking test (`tests/chromatic/app.chromatic.test.ts:19-24`).
- **Verification**: `npm run typecheck && npm run lint`, then
  `npx vitest run --config configs/vitest.config.ts tests/unit/bitrateChart.test.ts tests/unit/seekTab.test.ts tests/unit/inspectTab.test.ts tests/unit/analysisTab.test.ts`,
  `npm run test:integration` and `npm run test:chromatic` (the bitrate chart only). For certainty, compare
  the serialised markup of one chart of each kind before and after.

#### DUP-7: Test scaffolding copied between test files

- **Issue**: Twenty-one families of test scaffolding exist in more than one place: helpers copied whole
  from one test file into the next, fixtures written out by hand beside a builder that already exists, and
  a few fakes that are near twins of one another. A change to one copy (a base path, a default, how the
  fake core behaves) has to be found and repeated in the others, and nothing reports a miss. The rows marked
  byte-identical were established by hashing the cited ranges, the others by reading both sides. Nearly all
  the second copies arrived with #44 and #45 on 2026-09-05, two days after #39 gathered the shared unit-test
  fixtures into `tests/fixtures` (the question that raises is NV-38). The shared `@brain-bbqs/test-utils`
  0.3.0 that the lockfile pins is a home for two of the families only: its Vitest entry exports twenty
  functions, and `pickFiles` and `installObserverStub` are the only ones that match a repeat listed here.

| the fake mediabunny `Input` | `tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts:78-88`, `tests/unit/segmentRun.test.ts:91-103` | compareTab's is segmentRun's with the duration fixed at 1.9 and an empty `dispose` | only together with the fake core (same two files, same hoisted state) | Medium |
| `loadClip` | `tests/unit/segmentRun.test.ts:111-119`, `tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts:167-176` | same docstring, same clip; they differ in who sets the tracks and duration, a name parameter, and the real bytes one of them needs | only as part of that harness; alone, leave | Medium |
| a promise held open | `heldEncode` at `tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts:198-205`, `deferred` at `tests/unit/encodeTab.test.ts:311-326` | one idea in two shapes: resolve only (named `release`), against resolve and reject | one generic `deferred` under `tests/unit/helpers`; four lines of `tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts` change with it: the two calls that create the promise (lines 1004 and 1119) and the two that release it (lines 1030 and 1126) | High |

- **Proposed change**: Work down the table. The first fifteen rows are mechanical (create or extend the
  module, import it, delete the local copies), except that the pool reset line belongs to point 5 below.
  Five points the table has no room for. (1) The inline block at `tests/unit/matrixCache.test.ts:335-348`
  becomes one awaited call of `seed` with the store name and the record it writes on line 341; the
  hand-built table at `tests/unit/sizeEstimate.test.ts:323-329` is what `evenSamples` builds for 100 frames
  over 100 seconds, given the size and keyframe rules of lines 324 and 328 as its two functions, `cts` and
  `dts` apart. (2) `pickFiles` replaces the stub at the first and third site only. At the second
  (`tests/unit/fileLoading.test.ts:386-389`) the direct call of the handler is the one thing that would
  fail if the app stopped installing it (calling a missing handler throws, a dispatched event is silent),
  and that test asserts nothing else that would notice; leave it until the test checks something of its
  own. (3) The shared observer stub needs an explicit restore in `afterEach`: it is installed as a plain
  property definition, which the `vi.unstubAllGlobals()` at `tests/unit/analysisTab.test.ts:105` does not
  track (INFERRED for vitest's side). The two assertions become reads of the instance's `observed` and
  `disconnected`, the second a boolean where the test now counts one call (a spy on the instance's method
  keeps the count, if it matters). The shared stub can also fire the callback, which the hand-rolled class
  drops, so the same test could then pin the re-fit as well. (4) Left alone on purpose: the two
  `fragmentedFile` helpers (`tests/unit/atomLayout.test.ts:19-27`, `tests/unit/atomsTab.test.ts:22-28`)
  are different functions; `fileOf` (`tests/unit/chunkedSource.test.ts:17-25`) fakes a `File` because
  `readChunk` is the code under test there; the sample literal at `tests/unit/fileLoading.test.ts:23` sits
  in a hoisted mock factory; the fake core at `tests/unit/ffmpegEngine.test.ts:43-81` is the engine's own
  double (injected errors, a built count, a file set, a progress handler) and
  `tests/unit/ffmpegPool.test.ts:6` is a one-line stub; the integration mock's data set
  (`tests/integration/demoArchive.ts:31-129`) is pinned by the Playwright assertions and the Chromatic
  snapshots; the two helpers that read a store straight out of IndexedDB
  (`tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts:249-263`, `tests/unit/matrixCache.test.ts:64-78`) have one shape over
  different databases and are too slight to share. (5) The fake core, the fake `Input`, `loadClip` and the
  pool reset are one decision: a shared harness for `tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts` and
  `tests/unit/segmentRun.test.ts`, or nothing.
- **Confidence it's safe**: **High** for 17 rows, **Medium** for 4 (last column). Tests only: `tests/` is
  outside the type check (`configs/tsconfig.json:6-14`), and the shared Vitest config collects only files
  under `tests/unit` whose names end in `.test.ts` and leaves all of `tests/` out of coverage, so a helper
  module is neither run as a test nor counted. OBSERVED: `setUrl`, `flush`, a generic `deferred` and the
  grid lookup import nothing, so no `vi.mock` or `vi.hoisted` call in the files that use them can reach
  them. The `result` and `cutInputs` fixtures need type imports only, which are erased, so the mediabunny
  mock at `tests/unit/seekTab.test.ts:21` and the `segmentRun` mock at `tests/unit/encodeTab.test.ts:28-33`
  are untouched. Every copy of `flush` looks `setTimeout` up when it is called, as an imported one would, so
  the files that fake timers see the same timer. The upgrade handler that `seed` adds cannot fire at the
  inline site: the cache has already opened the database at version 1 and created both stores
  (`tests/unit/matrixCache.test.ts:333-334`, `src/lib/matrixCache.ts:177-183`). The sample builders write 0
  into `cts` and `dts`; `src/lib/sizeEstimate.ts:105-106`, `src/lib/sizeEstimate.ts:127-128` and
  `src/lib/sizeEstimate.ts:154` read only `ctsSec` and `size`, and `src/ui/seekTab.ts:33` only the array's
  length, so no assertion sees the difference. The describe at `tests/unit/ffmpegEngine.test.ts:113-168`
  asserts on none of the nine further fields `resetCore` clears, and the four error fields among them, which
  the fake reads, hold null until a later test gives one a value (`tests/unit/ffmpegEngine.test.ts:277`). No
  test relies on what either grid lookup returns on a miss: the callers of one all assert an exact text, and
  the other would throw. INFERRED: that jsdom runs a handler assigned to `onchange`
  (`src/ui/fileLoading.ts:161`) when `pickFiles` dispatches the event. The Medium rows are not mechanical.
  The state the fake core reads has to exist when the hoisted mock factory runs, so it must move out of each
  file's `vi.hoisted` into the helper, and compareTab would gain the other fake's `writeFile`, which
  detaches the buffer it is given (harmless by reading: the engine's only `writeFile` passes a copy,
  `src/lib/ffmpegEngine.ts:188`). For the archive only the stub function can be shared: merging the data
  sets would change what the demosPage tests assert.
- **Verification**: `npm run lint`, `npm run format:check` and `npm test`. Lint matters here: once `result`
  and `cutInputs` are imported, the `SeekResult` type import at `tests/unit/seekTab.test.ts:4` and
  `tests/unit/seekReport.test.ts:4` and `RunInputs` at `tests/unit/encodeTab.test.ts:8` are unused, and lint
  reports them until they are removed. No row adds or removes a test, so the totals should stay where CI's
  last run on this commit has them: 53 test files, 959 tests.

#### DUP-8: Tests that repeat other tests

- **Issue**: The eight rows below are tests, or single assertions, that state what another test already
  pins, mostly because a suite for a helper was added after a tab-level or older test had covered the same
  ground. A repeat costs run time and reading time, and it hides where a behaviour is really pinned: in
  several rows (the third column says which) one side of the apparent duplicate carries the only assertion
  of its kind, so deleting it as a repeat can lose a pin that no coverage figure reports. Established by
  reading each pair line by line and by searching the test tree for other assertions on the same value.

  clamp row is Medium because it is safe only in the stated order and nothing automatic notices a slip:
  without the `"0"` case, statement and branch coverage do not move (the line still runs and `Math.max` is
  not a branch), no coverage threshold is configured, and CI stays green. The test count drops by one for
  each test deleted, six at most (two in metadataTagKb, one each in ffmpegEngine, sizeEstimate, compareTab
  and inspectTab).
