| `src/lib/atomLayout.ts:162` | `.sort((a, b) => a.from - b.from);` on siblings that `place` (`src/lib/atomLayout.ts:93-105`) already emits in axis order | Drop the sort | High |
| `src/lib/chromaFormat.ts:62,68` | `?? null` behind a lookup masked with `& 0x03` into a table that has all four entries (`src/lib/chromaFormat.ts:26-31`) | Drop, or keep as a guard: low value either way | High |
| `src/lib/demoArchive.ts:126-128` | `first` is assigned on the first line, copied into `next` on the third and never read again | Initialise `next` with the URL | High |
| `src/lib/dom.ts:99` | A bare 360 in `box.left + 360 > window.innerWidth`, beside a popover capped at `max-width: min(340px, 70vw)` (`src/style.css:646`) | Name the constant and point its comment at that rule | High |
| `src/lib/explainers.ts:213` | The string ends `over a network. ";`: a trailing space, there since the string was added in #35 | Remove the space | High (the popover's markup loses one unrendered space) |
| `src/lib/qualityMatrix.ts:137` | `describeSettings` spells out `${settings.quality} (CRF ${settings.crf})`, which is what `describeQuality` (`src/lib/qualityMatrix.ts:143-145`) returns for every quality but `custom` | Build it on `describeQuality(settings)` | High (no caller can pass `custom`) |
| `src/lib/qualityMatrix.ts:320` | Eviction orders held cells by `(b.bytes ?? 0) - (a.bytes ?? 0)` but subtracts `heldCellBytes(cell)` (`src/lib/qualityMatrix.ts:324`) | Order by `heldCellBytes` too | High |
| `src/lib/state.ts:94` | `scaler: "lanczos",` restates `DEFAULT_SCALER` (`src/lib/cliCommand.ts:39`) | Import the constant | High |

- **Proposed change**: Apply row by row; the rows are independent. The eviction row keeps the
  `?? heldBytes(cell.blobs)` fallback at `src/lib/qualityMatrix.ts:298` and the test written for it
  (`tests/unit/qualityMatrix.test.ts:209-218`): removing that arm is not a clean deletion, since the type
  still needs a fallback and the test was added on purpose.
- **Confidence it's safe**: **High** for every row. OBSERVED, for the rows that are not self-evident. The
  sort: `place` is the only producer of sibling lists, every `layoutAtoms` argument in `src` and in the tests
  is a `placeAtoms` result (`src/ui/atomsTab.ts:85,118,166-167`), and clipping with `Math.max` and filtering
  both keep order, so a stable sort has nothing to move. The chroma lookups: the index is masked to 0 to 3
  after the bounds checks, and CI's last run on this commit lists both lines as uncovered branches.
  `describeSettings`: its four callers (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:445,567`, `src/ui/matrixPanel.ts:80,98`) pass a
  `MatrixCombo`, whose quality excludes `custom` (`src/lib/types.ts:142,162`), and the tests use `high`.
  Eviction: a cell's `bytes` and `blobs` are written by `makeMatrixCells` (both null,
  `src/lib/qualityMatrix.ts:225,228`), `markMeasured` (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:467-470`), `selectMatrixCell`
  (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:661-662`, consecutive statements) and eviction itself, so every held cell has
  `bytes`, the two orderings are equal, and the multi-cell tests set `bytes`
  (`tests/unit/qualityMatrix.test.ts:220-234`). `DEFAULT_SCALER`: `src/lib/cliCommand.ts:4` imports only
  types and `src/lib/state.ts:4-10` already depends on that module through `src/lib/qualityMatrix.ts`, so no
  import cycle appears. `sink`: `drawPreview` first runs at `src/ui/samplePicker.ts:176`, after the
  declaration either way, and a type-only import is erased, so mediabunny stays lazily loaded. `h` and
  `button`: in the shared ui package's `dom.js` (lines 4 to 17) `h` is `createElement` plus `className`, and
  `button` adds only the type. INFERRED: the trailing space is not rendered (`.info-pop` has
  `white-space: normal`, `src/style.css:660`); no test reads that string.
- **Verification**: `npm run typecheck && npm run lint && npm run format:check && npm test`, then
  `npm run test:integration` for the atom map and the document preview in a real browser.

#### CL-6: Tests that do not pin what their titles name: the Reencode, Compare and A/B suites

- **Issue**: CI's last run on this commit reports 99.9% statement coverage, but coverage counts a line as
  soon as a test executes it, whatever the test goes on to assert. The 16 rows below, and the 23 in CL-7,
  are places where a test's body cannot fail for what its title names: in most the line the title is about
  can be removed or inverted and the test still passes, in a few the named case is never reached. Where a
  row names a change to the source, that change was traced by hand through the tests the row cites; every
  row has had two independent readings and nothing was run. The middle column also says what the test does
  pin. The cost is a regression in exactly the named behaviour arriving under a green suite, and the last
  two rows sit on the defects described in NV-1 and NV-2.

<!-- prettier-ignore -->
| Test | What its body can and cannot show | Smallest strengthening | Safe? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `tests/unit/encodeTab.test.ts:77` `offers a run of the built command over one fixed stretch` | Its two absence checks (`tests/unit/encodeTab.test.ts:81-82`) look for `#sampleDuration` and `#sampleSegments`, ids no source file emits in any commit of this history: a pickaxe search over all refs finds them only in these two lines (93231de). That commit squashes pull request #23, whose message records fixed duration and segment fields being put on this tab and then taken off, so the ids may date from a commit that was not kept. Today the helper's one call under `src/` is `sampleFields("et")` (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:92`), so the fields could return to this tab under any other prefix and both checks would pass. | Query what the helper always emits instead: its inputs' classes `sample-duration` and `sample-segments` (`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:71-72`), inside the panel. If CON-4's optional step later drops those classes, move the two checks to whatever then identifies the fields. | High |
| `tests/unit/encodeTab.test.ts:125` `leaves the Compare Quality tab's duration and segments alone` | Its one action is typing into the Compare tab's own Duration field; it reads that back and repeats the width check of the track test (`tests/unit/encodeTab.test.ts:90,134,137`). The band's width comes from a fixed length and is redrawn only by `sync()` (`src/ui/samplePicker.ts:39,133,175`), so this is the reverse of the title: a Reencode tab that overwrote `encodeTest.duration` when rendered would pass, and Segments is never read. | Retitle to what it pins (this tab keeps its three-second stretch whatever the sweep's Duration says). - **Proposed change**: Apply the last column one test file at a time; every edit is under `tests/`. A
  retitle or a comment edit leaves the suite as strict as it is and a strengthening makes it stricter; a
  deletion, offered in three rows as an alternative, removes a test whose checks a neighbouring test
  repeats or nearly repeats. Before keeping a strengthened test, make the source change its row names on
  a scratch branch and see the new test fail, then drop that change: a test that still passes under its
  own mutation has not closed the gap. Where a row offers a retitle as the alternative, the retitle is the
  no-risk choice. The two pool rows change titles and one comment, or remove the tests, and nothing else;
  what the pool should do is NV-1 and NV-2, and the last clause of the docstring at
  `src/lib/ffmpegPool.ts:52-54` wants rewording once NV-1 is decided.

  assertions whose outcome on today's code follows from the lines cited (the class query and the fake
  core's output); **Medium** for every other new or rewritten expectation, because none has been run.
  Nothing under `src/` changes, so the only ways to be wrong are a new test that fails on today's code or
  one that still passes under its mutation. OBSERVED by reading: each mutation in the table was traced
  by hand through the tests its row cites (all six `drainWithPool` bodies finish in one pass of the outer
  loop; every Play press in `tests/unit/abPanel.test.ts` is at slider 0 or 1000), and the searches behind
  the first row (the two ids over the whole history, the helper's callers over `src/`) returned only what

  parked at 990 the label reads "0.00s" with the special case and "0.99s" without, a stale loop doubles
  the frame requests, and the reordered preview ends at width 2 with the token check and width 1 without.
  Where another test was found to pin the same line, its row names it (the output delete, the spare CPU,
  the default qualities and resolutions); the two pins in the third row rest on one reading.
- **Verification**:
  `npx vitest run --config configs/vitest.config.ts tests/unit/encodeTab.test.ts tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts tests/unit/abPanel.test.ts tests/unit/segmentRun.test.ts tests/unit/samplePicker.test.ts tests/unit/ffmpegEngine.test.ts tests/unit/ffmpegPool.test.ts`,
  then `npm run lint`, `npm run format:check` and `npm test`. The test count moves only by the cases added
  or deleted. If the second row is settled by deleting its test, the `renderCompareTab` import at
  `tests/unit/encodeTab.test.ts:35` goes with it, or lint reports it unused (INFERRED: the shared ESLint
  configuration applies the recommended TypeScript rules to every file). The mutation check in the
  proposed change is the real verification of each strengthened row: today's test stays green under it
  and the new one must not.

#### CL-8: Test idioms: a helper exists in the file and a test spells it out again

- **Issue**: In eight test files a test writes out by hand what a helper in the same file already does, or
  the same few lines recur with no helper at all. Each case is local to one file. The cost is reading time
  and, where the hand-written version differs slightly from the helper (a missing separator, an event that
  does not bubble), the time to work out that the difference does not matter. Established by reading each
  site against its helper and by searching each file for the repeated expression.

#### CL-9: Em-dashes against the project's own rule

- **Location**: 239 lines outside `CHANGELOG.md`: 172 in TypeScript under `src` (95 in `src/lib`, 73 in
  `src/ui`, 4 in `src/main.ts`), 34 in `src/style.css`, 19 under `tests`, 4 in `stories`, 9 in `scripts` and
  `.github/workflows/version-check.yml:28`.
- **Issue**: `CLAUDE.md:14` reads `- Limit use of em-dashes in all text` and has since #3 (0180ed8), so
  nearly every one of these lines was written under it. This review counts 214 of the 239 as comments (157
  in TypeScript under `src`, 34 in the stylesheet, 14 in tests, 9 in scripts). The other 25 are 15 lines
  inside string literals in `src`, 4 story names, the workflow echo, 4 test lines that pin visible
  strings (three assertions and the lookup at `tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts:226`) and 1 test input
  (`tests/unit/analysisDoc.test.ts:61`). Thirteen of those 15 lines reach a user;
  the other two sit in prose that the Full Analysis document strips before anything is shown
  (`src/lib/explainers.ts:664`, `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:419`; see DC-1). None of the eight commits from #58
  to the reviewed one adds an em-dash, so what is left is a one-time clean-up.
- **Proposed change**: One commit that touches nothing else: in comments, replace each em-dash with a colon,
  a comma, parentheses or a full stop. That covers 209 lines. Five comment lines are left out because they
  are also program output: `scripts/generate-demos.sh:4,16,27,29` and `scripts/purge-sample-blob.sh:14` lie
  inside the header ranges those scripts print for `-h` (`scripts/generate-demos.sh:67`,
  `scripts/purge-sample-blob.sh:50`), so rewording them changes the help text. The 13 visible lines with the
  4 test lines that pin them, the 4 story names (Chromatic snapshot names) and the workflow echo are a
  wording decision for the maintainers (NV-39), and the five help-text lines belong to the same decision.
  The two stripped lines go with DC-1, the test input stays, and the four lines in `CHANGELOG.md` are
  history.
- **Confidence it's safe**: **High**. OBSERVED: the per-directory counts are `git grep -c` results for
  U+2014. Each of the 239 lines was then classified with comment and string boundaries tracked across lines,
  and the 25 non-comment lines were read one by one (in `src`: ten in template literals, five in quoted
  strings; no line that looks like a comment lies inside a template literal). A comment edit cannot change
  what the bundle, the stylesheet's rules or a test does; the one way it can be observed, a script printing
  its own header, is the exception above (read in the two scripts, which were not run).
- **Verification**: `npm run format:check && npm run lint && npm test`. Afterwards
  `git grep -c $'\u2014' -- src tests stories scripts .github` should add up to 30 lines: the 25 non-comment
  lines and the five help-text lines.

#### CL-10: The manual test template describes an app that no longer exists

- **Location**: `docs/user_tests/template.md:10-49`
- **Issue**: The checklist was added in #41 (49ced87), after #23 folded six tabs into three and #28 replaced
  the bundled sample, and it asks for things the app could not do even then.
  `docs/user_tests/template.md:12` reads `Loading a sample video from the sample picker works`: the page
  offers Choose File, Browse Demo Files and Load from URL (`index.html:96-98`), and `src/ui/samplePicker.ts:1`
  is another control (it picks which seconds of the loaded file a run encodes). Of the five tab headings only
  "Inspect tab" is a tab's label: "Analysis tab" (`docs/user_tests/template.md:23`) is the Full Analysis
  document behind a button, "Seek tab" (`docs/user_tests/template.md:34`) became a section of Inspect in #23,
  and the Compare and Encode tabs are labelled Compare Quality and Reencode with FFmpeg
  (`index.html:128-161`). `docs/user_tests/template.md:18-20` describe an atom tree with selection and a
  table of contents that `matches the atom tree`; the app draws an atom map whose blocks show a readout on
  hover and zoom on click (`src/ui/atomsTab.ts:263-280`), and the contents list comes from the section
  headings (`src/ui/inspectToc.ts:21`). `docs/user_tests/template.md:30` reads
  `Two files can be loaded side-by-side (A/B panel)`: the A/B window compares the loaded file with an encode
  of itself (`src/ui/abPanel.ts:1-2`), as it has since #1 (d1ab1a5).
  `docs/user_tests/template.md:37` ties a segment run to the seeking test, but the segment run belongs to
  the two encoding tabs (`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:1-4`). On the demos page (`docs/user_tests/template.md:48-49`)
  nothing plays, and the only download is a link per file (`src/ui/demosPage.ts:163-168`). Three filled-in
  records tick the two-files item (`docs/user_tests/erik.md:30`, `docs/user_tests/rahul.md:32`,
  `docs/user_tests/brock.md:30`), so the template also collects answers that cannot
  mean what they say.
- **Proposed change**: Rewrite the checklist against what the page has: the three load buttons
  (`index.html:96-98`), the three tabs and the Full Analysis button (`index.html:128-161`), and the demos
  page (open a demo in the app, download one file). Keep what already holds: the bitrate chart and the codec
  and container details can be checked in the Full Analysis document, while the chroma figure is on Inspect
  only (`src/ui/inspectTab.ts:157`). Leave the three filled-in records as they are.
- **Confidence it's safe**: **High**. Documentation only. OBSERVED:
  `git grep -n -E "user_tests|template\.md" -- ':!docs'` prints nothing, and nothing bundles `docs/`.
  Prettier checks Markdown, so the rewrite has to stay formatted.
- **Verification**: `npm run format:check`

#### CL-11: Restated shared defaults and ignore entries for things the repository does not have

- **Issue**: Five settings repeat a value that the shared `@brain-bbqs/config` package or the tool itself
  already supplies, one ignore list lacks the directory the demo generator writes to by default, and two
  ignore lists carry entries for files the repository does not have. The restated values are harmless today
  and an explicit pin is a defensible choice, so those rows are optional. The missing ignore entry is the row
  worth doing: one `git add -A` after a local generator run would stage every generated video. The defaults
  were read in the published 0.2.1 package, the version `package-lock.json:153-154` locks.

<!-- prettier-ignore -->
| Location | What is there | Why it is redundant, or what is missing | Change |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `configs/eslint.config.js:9` | `complexity: 20,` | The shared config's `eslint.js` (line 42) defaults the cap to 20, and its README (line 34) says the apps other than bbqs-uploader "take the default 20" | Optional: drop, or keep with a comment saying the pin is deliberate |
| `configs/vitest.config.ts:7` | `environment: "jsdom",` | The shared config's `vitest.js` (line 40) defaults to jsdom. The migration commit (#53, 616f82f) lists the jsdom environment among the things it kept | Optional: keeping it is the recorded choice |
| `.pre-commit-config.yaml:11-12` | `default_install_hook_types:` with the single entry `pre-commit` | That is pre-commit's own default (INFERRED from memory of its documentation, which was not consulted for this review; nothing in the repository shows it) | Optional: drop |
| `tests/unit/main.changelog-link.test.ts:1`, `tests/unit/main.loaded.test.ts:1`, `tests/unit/main.preload-failure.test.ts:1`, `tests/unit/main.smoke.test.ts:1` | `// @vitest-environment jsdom` | Four of the 53 unit test files, all added in #58; the other 49 run under jsdom from the config alone (for example `tests/unit/elements.test.ts`) | Optional: remove the four lines |
| `package.json:9,16` | `tsc --noEmit` in the `build` and `typecheck` scripts | The shared `tsconfig.base.json` (line 13) sets `noEmit`, as the project's own tsconfig did from #3 until #53 moved it there; `tsconfig.json:2` reaches it through `configs/tsconfig.json:2` | Optional: drop the flag when `package.json` next changes |
| `.gitignore:11`, `.gitignore:14` | The generated-media entries are `scripts/data/` and `tests/fixtures/.generated/` | `demo-out`, the default directory of the generator and the uploader (`scripts/generate-demos.sh:43`, `scripts/upload-demos.sh:28`), is not ignored | Add `demo-out/` |

- **Proposed change**: Add `demo-out/` to `.gitignore`. For the optional rows, either drop the restated
  values together with other config tidying or keep them as explicit pins; editing `package.json` for the two
  flags alone is not worth the version bump it triggers. Too slight to change on their own: `reports/`
  (`.gitignore:4`), which no tool configured here writes, and the `*.jpg`, `*.jpeg`, `*.gif` and `*.mp4`
  entries of the codespell skip list (`configs/.codespellrc:2`), which match no tracked file (no such image
  was ever committed, and no `.mp4` has been tracked since #28).
- **Confidence it's safe**: **High**. OBSERVED: the two config values equal the defaults in the locked
  package, and the `^0.2.0` range (`package.json:31`) admits only 0.2.x, so lint and test behaviour is the
  same with or without them. CI calls the two npm scripts by name (`.github/workflows/lint.yml:32`,
  `.github/workflows/deploy.yml:33`). The new `.gitignore` entry changes only what `git status` and tools that
  follow that file see: there is no `.prettierignore`, and Prettier reads `.gitignore` by default (INFERRED).
  No tracked path lies under `demo-out`, and the upload path copies out of that directory with `cp` and names
  it to the artifact action by path (`scripts/upload-demos.sh:56-65`,
  `.github/workflows/upload-demos.yml:32-41`), neither of which goes through git (INFERRED for the action).
  Edits under `configs/` and to `package.json` need the version bump
  (`.github/workflows/version-check.yml:8-11`); the pre-commit, test and ignore rows do not.
- **Verification**: `npm run lint && npm test` (still 53 files and 959 tests, as in CI's last run on this
  commit), `npm run typecheck`, `npm run build`, `npm run format:check`.

#### CL-12: Smaller repository texts and settings

- **Issue**: Four small places where a repository text or setting covers more than this repository has or
  needs: two rules in `CLAUDE.md` are worded for things the project does not have, the footer pasted into
  `index.html` carries, besides the app's own additions, one redundant attribute on two images that the
  shared fragment does not have, and one CI job installs Playwright's browsers without naming one, for a
  suite that runs in Chromium only. None affects the app. Each was compared with the tree, the history and
  the published shared packages.

<!-- prettier-ignore -->
| Location | What is there | What is wrong | Change | Safe? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `CLAUDE.md:6` | The version-bump rule covers any file under `src/` "(except `tests/`)" | `src/` has never held tests (they were under `tests/unit` in #3, which wrote the clause), and the check filters on plain `src/**` (`.github/workflows/version-check.yml:8`) | Drop the parenthesis | High |
| `CLAUDE.md:9` | "Never mention `?test` live test injections (or any other debug-only URL override)" | The app reads four URL parameters (`src/lib/appUrl.ts:31-34`) and no `?test`; the string occurs nowhere else in the tree. The clause arrived in #58; the parameter belongs to sibling apps (the shared test-utils README boots a page with it) | Keep only the general clause, unless the wording is shared across the BBQS apps on purpose | High |
| `index.html:231-237` | `aria-hidden="true"` on the two Talmo Lab logo images | The shared `page-footer.html` fragment (lines 46 and 47) has neither; both images have an empty `alt` inside a link that carries an `aria-label` | Drop the two attributes, or leave them: very low value | High |
| `.github/workflows/chromatic-playwright.yml:64-65` | `run: npx playwright install --with-deps`: no browser named, no cache | The suite has one Chromium project (the shared config's `playwright.js`, lines 73 to 83); the integration job names `chromium` and caches it (`.github/workflows/test.yml:39-52`) | Name `chromium` | Medium |

- **Proposed change**: As per row, all low priority. `CLAUDE.md` is the maintainers' own instruction file,
  so the first two rows are flagged for them to decide, not presumed. For the CI row there is a variant that
  leaves the runner's installed system packages as they are: split the step into
  `npx playwright install-deps` with no argument, then `npx playwright install chromium` (from a single
  reading; not run).
- **Confidence it's safe**: **High** for the first three rows, **Medium** for the fourth. OBSERVED:
  `CLAUDE.md` is text that nothing executes. Nothing in `tests`, `stories` or `src/style.css` selects the two
  logo images by `aria-hidden`, the attributes date from #13, and `index.html` is outside the version check's
  path filter. INFERRED: an image with an empty `alt` inside a labelled link is already outside the
  accessibility tree, so assistive technology sees no change (no browser was run). For the CI job, both
  install lines date from #5 (5932b31), so the two workflows have differed since they were written: this is
  not drift. That an install with no browser argument fetches every default browser, and with `--with-deps`
  their system packages, is INFERRED from Playwright's command line as read in a copy one minor version newer
  than the locked 1.62.1 (`package-lock.json:2471-2472`). By the same reading, naming `chromium` removes the
  Firefox and WebKit system libraries from a job whose own comments treat the runner's libraries as
  pixel-sensitive (`.github/workflows/chromatic-playwright.yml:14-15`), which is why that row is Medium.
- **Verification**: `npm run format:check`,
  `npx vitest run --config configs/vitest.config.ts tests/unit/elements.test.ts tests/unit/main.smoke.test.ts`,
  `npm run test:integration` and `npm run test:chromatic` for the footer. Nothing to run for `CLAUDE.md`. For
  the CI row there is no local command: on the pull request, read the log of the "Install Playwright
  browsers" step and confirm that the Chromatic (Playwright) build reports no visual changes.

## Needs verification

Nothing in this section is proposed as a quality change. Each item either changes something a user, an exported
file or CI can observe, or needs a maintainer's judgment. Defects and behaviour questions come first, the most
consequential first, then decisions for maintainers, then smaller questions. Each item says how it was
established. None of them was reproduced in a browser: nothing from the repository was executed for this review, so "inferred" marks
every part that rests on browser, library or platform behaviour, and each item ends with the quickest way to
confirm it.

### Defects and behaviour questions

<a name="nv-1"></a>**NV-1: `drainWithPool` never runs an item appended after a lane has run off the end of the queue.**
`src/lib/ffmpegPool.ts:66-81`. Each lane takes its index before testing it (`const index = next++;` then
`if (index >= queue.length) return;`, `src/lib/ffmpegPool.ts:74-75`), so every lane that finds the queue
exhausted still moves `next` one further past the end, and an item appended afterwards sits at an index
that is already spent: one appended item is lost for each lane that has left. The outer loop's
`while (next < queue.length)` (`src/lib/ffmpegPool.ts:67`) is false for the same reason. In the app,
clicking a failed (or skipped) square during a sweep appends it to the live queue
(`src/ui/compareTab.ts:440-447`), and a sweep with two or more squares to encode, on a machine reporting
three or more CPUs, runs two to four lanes (`src/lib/ffmpegPool.ts:37-40`). A square clicked once any lane
has left (from the moment the first of the last squares finishes; a square that fails in that final batch
opens the window itself) is logged as "Queued a retry of ...", reads "queued", is never encoded, and ends
"skipped" (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:578-580`): its face says "run it", its failure text is gone, the note
counts one skipped, the button reads "Run 1 unmeasured" and the ranking star is withheld. The docstring
(`src/lib/ffmpegPool.ts:52-54`) and `CHANGELOG.md:250` promise the opposite, and the tests cannot see it:
both pool tests for appends use one worker and push from inside `work`
(`tests/unit/ffmpegPool.test.ts:78-102`, see CL-6), and the app's test of the feature runs on one lane
(`tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts:279-280`, `tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts:1000-1039`). Fixing it is not a
quality change: retries dropped today would run, changing final statuses, the note and the button label.
Question for a maintainer: should a lane test the bound before it claims an index, with the docstring
brought into line? To confirm: add a two-worker case to `tests/unit/ffmpegPool.test.ts` that holds one item
and pushes a third after the other lane has finished (by reading, two of the three run today), or run the
app's test with `hardwareConcurrency` mocked to 4 (by reading, it stops at two encode attempts where it
expects three). Confirmed by two independent readings; a hand-copied model of the loop (a model, not a run
of the repository's code) ran the appended item with one lane and dropped it with two and with four.

<a name="nv-2"></a>**NV-2: The pool rejects on the first item that throws while other cores carry on; Stop during a Reencode
run reads as a failure.** `drainWithPool` has no catch (`src/lib/ffmpegPool.ts:68-79`), and its docstring
makes not throwing the caller's contract (`work` `is expected to record the failure against it`,
`src/lib/ffmpegPool.ts:56-58`), so a `work` that throws is outside the pool's stated contract; what is open is what
happens when one does.

- Detached lanes, narrow reach: the sweep's callback catches per square (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:549-568`) and
  the Reencode run has one stretch, hence one core (`src/ui/encodeTab.ts:231`), so only a square re-encoded
  for the A/B window can meet this (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:654-657`), with two or more stretches on three or
  more CPUs and one stretch's encode crashing. The drain then rejects at once, the tab reports the failure
  and unlocks, and the other lanes go on encoding stretches nothing will read; what that does to the shared
  cores is inferred.
- The test titled `carries on after an item throws` never throws: its callback records the item and awaits
  (`tests/unit/ffmpegPool.test.ts:121-129`). Renaming or deleting it changes no behaviour and is listed in
  CL-6.
- Stop pressed while the Reencode tab's sample is encoding terminates the core
  (`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:482-488`); the pending call's rejection leaves `encodeWindows` before its own stop
  check (`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:409`), and `reportRunFailure` recognises only `RunStopped`
  (`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:513-520`). The user sees "Failed: " plus the library's message (with the message the
  repository's fake cores use, `tests/unit/segmentRun.test.ts:63`, that is "Failed: called
  FFmpeg.terminate()") and a console error where "Stopped." was meant; a Stop during the stream-copy cut
  (`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:248`) does the same on both tabs, and the real library's wording is inferred.

Neither open point is a quality change (one changes a visible message, the other the timing and outcome of a
failed re-encode), so the question for a maintainer is whether a rejection that arrives while
`stopRequested()` is set should be reported as a stop, and whether the pool should stop or await its other
lanes when `work` throws; to confirm the Stop report, capture the rejection in
`tests/unit/segmentRun.test.ts:671-689`, pass it to `reportRunFailure` and read the note. Confirmed by two
independent readings; the library's part is inferred, not run.

<a name="nv-3"></a>**NV-3: `cancelRequested` outlives a stopped run, so the next square that needs encoding answers
"Stopped."** Stop sets the flag (`encodeTest.cancelRequested = true;`, `src/ui/segmentRun.ts:484`) and,
apart from a file load or Reset (`src/lib/state.ts:101-109`), only the start of a run clears it
(`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:493`); `endRunUi` leaves it set (`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:504-510`), and a square's
re-encode goes through `prepareRun` without starting a run (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:649-657`).

- After a Stop on either tab, clicking a finished square that holds no output (read back from the cache,
  `src/ui/compareTab.ts:406`, or released for the memory budget) reads the whole source again, a full
  download for a URL, because Stop also emptied the cores (`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:487`), and then throws at
  the first check (`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:224`, or `src/ui/segmentRun.ts:409` without a keyframe table). The
  ring jumps back and the note reads "Stopped.", on every click until some run starts or a file is loaded.
- A Stop pressed while a finished sweep is loading its best square (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:595-598`) is
  swallowed: the note "Stopping…" is overwritten, the bar is filled green (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:607`) and
  the flag stays set, and when the winner is being re-encoded the stop is caught as a display failure and
  logged under "Encoded fine, but could not be shown:" (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:599-605`). This is likely on a
  repeat sweep, where every square comes from the cache and the winner's re-encode is the only encoding in
  the run.
- A Stop pressed before the cut, during the first-use core download (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:536-538`,
  `src/ui/encodeTab.ts:257-259`), is honoured only after the whole source has been read, because the read at
  `src/ui/segmentRun.ts:197` precedes the first check. That `fetchFile` reads or downloads the whole source
  is inferred.

Clearing the flag when a run ends or when a square's re-encode starts, ending a Stop in the best-square
window as a stop, and checking for a stop on entry to `prepareRun` each change what a click or a Stop does,
so which of them to take is the question for a maintainer; to confirm the main case, add a
`tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts` case that fills the cache, sweeps again with one more preset ticked, presses
Stop and clicks a cached square (by reading, the note reads "Stopped." where an A/B load is expected; CL-6
names the missing test of a released square). Confirmed by two independent readings; nothing was run.

<a name="nv-4"></a>**NV-4: A Run press refused because the other tab is encoding leaves that button disabled after the other
run ends.** `src/ui/encodeTab.ts:227-236` and `src/ui/compareTab.ts:225-240`. Each tab's click handler
disables its own Run button, calls a run that returns at once while `encodeTest.running` is set
(`src/ui/encodeTab.ts:245-248`, `src/ui/compareTab.ts:320`, `src/ui/compareTab.ts:451`), and then skips the
hand-back for the same reason (`if (encodeTest.running) return;`); the other run's `endRunUi` re-enables only
its own button (`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:507`), and switching tabs rebuilds nothing (`src/ui/tabs.ts:13-20`).
Sequence: press Run Matrix, switch to Reencode with FFmpeg, press Run Comparison. The note says "An encode is
already running on the Compare Quality tab. Wait for it to finish." and the button greys out; the user who
waits as told finds it still disabled when the sweep has ended, until the panel is rebuilt by a file load or
the Educational switch. The mirror case on the Compare tab writes no note at all; that button has two more
ways back, choosing a square (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:677`) and the end of a single-square retry. Handing a
refused button back is not a quality change, since it alters when a control becomes usable, and the two
tests pin only the held state (`tests/unit/encodeTab.test.ts:494-506`,
`tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts:892-919`). Question for a maintainer: how should a refused press get its
button back when the other run ends (for example by `endRunUi` re-enabling every run button), and should the
Compare-side refusal say why? To confirm: extend `tests/unit/encodeTab.test.ts:494-506` to end the other run
and read the button; by reading, nothing re-enables it. Confirmed by two independent readings.

<a name="nv-5"></a>**NV-5: The "one encoder at a time" rule has gaps.** The comments say there is one encoder
(`src/ui/encodeTab.ts:243-244`, `src/ui/compareTab.ts:772-773`), but what enforces it across the two tabs is
one flag, `encodeTest.running`, read by the sample run (`src/ui/encodeTab.ts:245`) and the sweep
(`src/ui/compareTab.ts:320`, `src/ui/compareTab.ts:451`); by reading, four paths go around it (the first four
bullets) and two neighbouring assumptions about what a run owns do not hold either (the last two).

- The whole-file encode never reads or sets the flag (`src/ui/inBrowserEncode.ts:38-83`) and runs on the
  default core, which is also core 0 of the pool (`src/lib/ffmpegPool.ts:46`). It can overlap any other run,
  the later one rebinding that core's log and progress handlers, and a Stop aimed at the other run
  terminates it, so a long encode is lost.
- A square's re-encode is guarded by `selecting` alone (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:644`) and the grid's busy state
  is fixed when the grid is painted (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:749-750`), so a square can be re-encoded beside
  the other tab's sample run, both writing an output named for core 0 (`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:284`). The
  snapshot errs the other way too: a Reencode run that takes the A/B window from a showing square repaints
  the grid as busy (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:216-219`), and every square stays disabled after that run ends,
  until the grid's next repaint.
- `runMatrix` checks the flag (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:320`), then awaits the sampling and the checksum
  (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:332`) before `encodeCells` raises it (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:495`): milliseconds for a
  local file, up to three one-megabyte reads on the first sweep of a URL (`src/lib/matrixCache.ts:332-333`).
  A sample run started in that gap runs beside the sweep, and when it ends its `endRunUi` clears the shared
  flag and the Stop targets (`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:505-506`) while `matrix.running` is still true, so the
  sweep's Stop then does nothing (`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:483`).
- A file load or Reset calls `resetState()` unconditionally (`src/ui/fileLoading.ts:65`,
  `src/ui/fileLoading.ts:136`), and nothing refuses a load during a run. The old run carries on, and its
  `finally` later clears the running flag, the Stop targets and the retry queue of whatever run is current
  by then (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:616-618`).
- The whole-file encode reads the loaded file again after its awaits: the save name
  (`src/ui/inBrowserEncode.ts:63`) and the original size (`src/ui/inBrowserEncode.ts:73`) come from
  `state.source` once the encode at `src/ui/inBrowserEncode.ts:61` has returned. Load file B while file A
  encodes and A's output is saved under B's name, with B's size recorded against it for Full Analysis
  (`src/ui/cliControls.ts:144-145`); after Reset the save-name line throws on a null source and the finished
  encode is dropped.
- Without a keyframe table the comment says `so it runs on one core alone.` (`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:320-321`),
  but the sweep and the square re-encode hand over every pooled core (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:536-542`,
  `src/ui/compareTab.ts:654-657`) and each core is written the whole video (`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:281`). Only
  the first core drops it (`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:442-445`) and its name carries only the extension
  (`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:130-132`), so a later file with the same extension and again no keyframe table
  would be encoded from the previous video on the other cores (`src/lib/ffmpegEngine.ts:186`): wrong sizes,
  silently, though this review could not name a real file that meets the precondition.

Question for a maintainer: should one shared busy gate be taken by the whole-file encode, the square
re-encode, the sample run and the sweep, what should a file load or a reset do to a live run, and should a
run that cannot cut its stretches keep to one core, as the comment says, or drop the video from every core
it used? The first two answers make runs refuse, queue or cancel where they overlap today and the third
changes which cores run or what they hold afterwards, which is why none of this is a quality change; no
test covers any overlap (the cross-tab tests pin only that a press is ignored while the flag is set,
`tests/unit/encodeTab.test.ts:494-506`, `tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts:892-919`), so confirming it means
one new case per bullet (for the fifth: hold the faked encode in `tests/unit/inBrowserEncode.test.ts`, swap
`state.source` for another name and size, release, and read the download name and `state.reencodeResult`).
Confirmed by two independent readings; what two runs do to each other inside one core is inferred, not run.

<a name="nv-6"></a>**NV-6: Flipping the Educational switch rebuilds every panel, whatever is running or showing.** Teaching
material is decided when an element is built (`src/lib/dom.ts:84`, `src/lib/dom.ts:165-168`), so the switch
redraws every tab, and its only test is whether the app is shown
(`if (els.app.style.display !== "none") renderAll();`, `src/main.ts:87-90`), not whether a run is going or a
result is on screen.

- During a sweep or a sample run the rebuilt panel starts with an enabled Run button, a hidden Stop and a
  hidden bar (`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:98-119`), while the live run goes on writing to the controls it was
  started with, now detached (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:496`, `src/ui/compareTab.ts:618-619`). On the page there
  is no Stop for the rest of the run, the grid stays as painted at the flip (squares frozen on queued or
  encoding, finished ones unclickable even after the run) and the winner, or the sample's comparison, is
  drawn into a detached section; pressing the new Run button meanwhile is the refused press of NV-4, and a
  whole-file encode's box is stranded the same way (`src/ui/inBrowserEncode.ts:25`).
- After a flip with a square showing, the comparison is gone (nothing redraws it) and the A/B window still
  holds the old Compare section as its host (`src/ui/abPanel.ts:36`), so a later Reencode run fires that
  section's handler, which clears `selectedKey` and repaints the detached grid
  (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:216-219`). The visible grid keeps its ring and the "Run This Setting with ffmpeg"
  command for a square that is no longer in the window, until its next repaint (the stale host is the one
  in NV-7).
- The seek card builds an empty results area on every render (`src/ui/seekTab.ts:78-80`) and never reads
  `state.seekResults`, which is written in one place (`src/ui/seekTab.ts:116`) and read in one
  (`src/ui/analysisTab.ts:298`). A finished seeking run therefore vanishes from Inspect while Full Analysis
  still reports it (one in flight goes on writing to the detached card), although the Compare tab redraws
  its grid from state for exactly this case (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:221-223`).
- The demos page sets `#app` to display none while it is up and restores it on close
  (`src/ui/demosPage.ts:316-317`, `src/ui/demosPage.ts:257`), so a flip made while that page covers a loaded
  file, a state reached through browser history, skips the redraw. The tabs then keep their old teach boxes
  until the next flip or load.

Question for a maintainer: should a rebuild be deferred or refused while a run is in flight, and should each
panel redraw its results from state when it is rebuilt? Any answer changes what the page shows after a flip,
so none of this is a quality change; `tests/unit/main.loaded.test.ts:102-108` pins the redraw with the
renderers stubbed and no test flips during a run, so to confirm, start a sweep with a held encode in
`tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts`, call `renderCompareTab` on the same panel again, and read the Stop button
and the squares after release. Confirmed by two independent readings; nothing was run.

<a name="nv-7"></a>**NV-7: A stale "displaced" handler clears the selection that was just made, and A/B playback is never stopped
when its panel goes away.** `src/ui/abPanel.ts:36` and `src/ui/abPanel.ts:305-309`. The A/B window remembers
the section it last drew into and, when asked to draw into another, empties that section and calls its handler
(`if (activeHost !== host) displaceActiveHost();`); nothing resets `activeHost` when a file load or the
Educational switch rebuilds the tabs, so the old, detached Compare section stays registered, with a handler
that clears the shared selection (`encodeTest.matrix.selectedKey = null;`, `src/ui/compareTab.ts:216-219`).
Shortest sequence: sweep file 1, load file 2, press Run Matrix; the sweep ends with the winner in the
Side-by-Side window but no ring and no "Run This Setting with ffmpeg" section, because the old panel's handler
fires on the first draw into the new one, after `selectMatrixCell` has set the selection
(`src/ui/compareTab.ts:643`) and before its last repaint (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:678`). It repeats for every
later file whose load finds the Compare tab's result in the window, so in ordinary use, one sweep per file,
every sweep after the first file ends without the command a sweep is run for, until a square is clicked; after
an Educational flip it is the next square clicked that loses both. The same missing teardown leaves playback
running: `stopActivePlayback` is called only at the start of the next comparison (`src/ui/abPanel.ts:306`), so
pressing Play and then flipping the Educational switch leaves the loop at `src/ui/abPanel.ts:513-536` decoding
both sides at frame rate into detached canvases, with nothing on the page to pause it. No behaviour-preserving
change exists: any fix changes what is ringed, whether the command shows and when decoding stops after a
reload or a flip. Question for a maintainer: clear the A/B window's module state when a panel is rebuilt,
ignore the handlers of detached hosts, or redraw the comparison after a rebuild (which never happens today)?
To confirm: in `tests/unit/abPanel.test.ts` load into a host that has a handler, remove that host, load into a
second host and expect the handler not to be called (by reading, it is called once); the existing tests mock
the A/B window (`tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts:102-118`) or use two live hosts
(`tests/unit/abPanel.test.ts:210-222`). Confirmed by two independent readings; nothing was run.

<a name="nv-8"></a>**NV-8: Squares can be left queued or pending with nothing to run them.** The pass that turns unreached
squares from pending into skipped sits inside the `try`, after the drain
(`if (cell.status === "pending") cell.status = "skipped";`, `src/ui/compareTab.ts:578-580`), so a square
made pending later, or a run that never reaches that line, leaves squares that read "queued", are disabled
(`src/ui/matrixPanel.ts:247-249`), are not counted as unmeasured (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:276-278`) and hold
the ranking back (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:756-757`).

- A failed square clicked while the sweep is loading its best square: `src/ui/compareTab.ts:598` awaits with
  `matrix.running` still true and the queue live until `src/ui/compareTab.ts:616-617`, so `queueRetry` sets
  the square pending and pushes it onto a queue nothing drains (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:441-444`). The window
  lasts a whole re-encode on a repeat sweep, where the winner holds no output; it is the same queue as in
  NV-1, and a fix to the pool does not close it.
- A run that dies before the drain: `retryCells` marks its squares pending before the run starts
  (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:450-457`), and a failed core download, a failed read or fetch of the source, or a
  Stop before the first square (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:536-538`) jumps to the catch at
  `src/ui/compareTab.ts:609-610`, past the pass. "Retry N failed" then turns into "Run Matrix" over squares
  stuck on "queued"; on a first sweep it is only a misleading face.

Both last until a full Run Matrix, which rebuilds every cell (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:327`), or a new file.
Running the pass in `finally` would leave "run it" squares under a "Run N unmeasured" button instead, which
changes labels and what a click does, so the question for a maintainer is whether to do that or to stop
offering the queue once the drain has returned; to confirm, read the cells' statuses at the end of
`tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts:857-874`, which takes the second path without looking at the squares (by
reading, the one square is still pending), and for the first case click the failed square between the press
and the settle in the test at `tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts:797`. Confirmed by two independent readings;
nothing was run.

<a name="nv-9"></a>**NV-9: Draw errors in the A/B window are never shown.** `src/ui/abPanel.ts:445-447` and
`src/ui/abPanel.ts:471-477`. The banner is built with the class `error-msg` and hidden inline; when a draw
rejects, the handler writes "Could not draw the comparison: ..." into it and clears the inline value
(`drawError.style.display = "";`), which hands the element back to its class, whose one rule says
`display: none;` (`src/style.css:505-513`). So the banner has not been visible since the commit that added it
(d951965, #22); this is INFERRED from the cascade, since nothing was rendered, and rests on a search that
found no other rule for the class in `src/`, `index.html` or the shared style sheets imported at
`src/style.css:5-6`. The class's other users write "block" (`src/ui/fileLoading.ts:37`,
`src/ui/seekTab.ts:123`), and the unit test asserts the very value that keeps the banner hidden
(`expect(msg.style.display).toBe("");`, `tests/unit/abPanel.test.ts:436`). Scrubbing bypasses even that
path: the slider's handler calls `void drawAt(relT);` (`src/ui/abPanel.ts:571`) where the view switch, the
segment steps and the first draw go through `drawFrom` (`src/ui/abPanel.ts:424`, `src/ui/abPanel.ts:559`,
`src/ui/abPanel.ts:574`), so a draw that rejects during a drag is an unhandled rejection with nothing
written at all. Either way the user sees the panes stay on the last frame with no explanation, and showing
the message is not a quality change: a banner would appear where none does today, and nothing clears it
once a later frame draws. Question for a maintainer: write "block" and change the expectation at
`tests/unit/abPanel.test.ts:436` (taking the rule off the class instead would show the empty `#errorMsg` box
at page load, `index.html:87`), send the scrub handler through `drawFrom`, and decide whether a good draw
should clear the message? To confirm: in a browser, make a frame read reject and look under the panes.
Confirmed by two independent readings; the rendering, and whether the library's frame read rejects in
practice, are inferred, not run.

<a name="nv-10"></a>**NV-10: The document's Compare Quality table ignores the frame-rate axis, stars an unfinished sweep, and
has a star with no legend.** The Full Analysis document's account of a sweep (`matrixSection` and
`compareSection`, `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:340-425`) is out of step with the Compare Quality page in three
places and unexplained in a fourth.

- **Frame-rate axis.** `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:386` takes four axes
  (`const { qualities, presets, scales, scalers } = matrixAxes(cells);`) and `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:400` looks
  each cell up with `comboKey(quality, preset, scale, scaler)`, but the key has a fifth part, `fps ?? "src"`
  (`src/lib/qualityMatrix.ts:126`), which the page's grid supplies as `block.fps`
  (`src/ui/matrixPanel.ts:189`). For a sweep over Source and 50% the document's table, which the docstring at
  `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:381` calls the grid the winner was picked out of, holds only the source-rate
  squares: the reduced-rate ones are absent, nothing is starred when one of them wins (though a finished sweep
  loads that winner into the A/B section above the table, `src/ui/compareTab.ts:588-598`), and with only
  reduced rates ticked every cell is a dash.
- **Unfinished sweep.** `const best = bestReductionCell(encodeTest.matrix.cells);`
  (`src/ui/analysisTab.ts:385`) ranks whatever has finished, while the page withholds the ranking until every
  square has run (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:756-757`, the fix recorded at `CHANGELOG.md:252`). After Stop, or with
  Full Analysis opened mid-sweep, the page shows no star and the document, with its PDF, HTML and Markdown,
  stars the smallest finished square beside dashes; `tests/unit/analysisTab.test.ts:533-548` pins that star.
- **Frame rate of the compared encode.** Neither the page's A/B summary (`src/ui/abPanel.ts:140-152`, pane
  label at `src/ui/abPanel.ts:342-348`) nor the document's A/B section (`src/ui/analysisTab.ts:344-356`)
  prints the `fps` that `EncodeSettings` carries (`src/lib/types.ts:154-155`). After a reduced-rate square is
  loaded, the document's comparison lists quality, preset and sizes with nothing to say frames were dropped
  (on the page the command under the grid still carries the rate, `src/ui/compareTab.ts:803-815`, and after a
  frame-rate override on the Reencode tab the document's command section shows it).
- **No legend.** Two of the prose blocks that `stripEducationalBlocks` removes
  (`src/ui/analysisTab.ts:452-456`) are result captions, not teaching copy: what the command section is
  (`src/ui/analysisTab.ts:329-334`) and the matrix legend (`src/ui/analysisTab.ts:413-421`, which holds
  `★ marks the largest reduction, which is the comparison above`). The table's cells therefore carry a star
  and a second figure, the encode time, that nothing in the document explains, and deleting the producers
  under DC-1 makes that permanent and removes the only copy of both strings.

None of these is a cleanup: the first adds rows or a column to the document of a sweep with a reduced rate
(a sweep at the source rate comes out unchanged), the second removes a star from exported documents and
changes the expectation at `tests/unit/analysisTab.test.ts:547`, the third adds a row, and the fourth adds
text (a legend that shows the star would also put a second one in the Markdown, against the single-star
expectation at `tests/unit/analysisTab.test.ts:285`). Question for a maintainer: should the document share
the page's enumeration and ranking, name the frame rate where it is not the source's, and keep the two
captions as a non-prose block, or let DC-1 delete them? The old legend could not come back verbatim: it
quotes one stretch's length although a sweep encodes several, and it assumes the starred square is the
comparison above. To confirm: add a case to `tests/unit/analysisTab.test.ts` with two finished cells that
differ only in rate (source and 15 fps), since no existing case has more than one rate, and run
`npx vitest run --config configs/vitest.config.ts tests/unit/analysisTab.test.ts`. Confirmed by two
independent readings.

<a name="nv-11"></a>**NV-11: ?tab=constructor (any `Object.prototype` key) is accepted as a tab.** `readTabFromUrl` resolves a
retired tab name by indexing an object literal (`LEGACY_TAB_IDS`, `src/lib/appUrl.ts:25-29`):
`return (value ? LEGACY_TAB_IDS[value] : null) ?? null;` (`src/lib/appUrl.ts:45`). A name that
Object.prototype also answers to (constructor, toString, valueOf, hasOwnProperty, `__proto__`) therefore
comes back as an inherited function, or for `__proto__` as an object, and `initTabs` treats it as a tab
(`src/ui/tabs.ts:13-20`, `src/ui/tabs.ts:34-40`): every tab button and panel loses its `on` class, so once a
file is loaded the tab bar stands over an empty page, and the address bar is rewritten to the value's string
form (the function's source text, or "[object Object]" for `__proto__`). It takes a hand-written link, has
no security effect and heals on reload, because the rewritten value is no longer an inherited name. An
own-property test (Object.hasOwn) or a Map would make such links read as an unknown name; that changes what
those URLs do and nothing else, which still makes it a behaviour change, and the compiler cannot flag the
lookup: the declared type, `Record<string, TabId>` (`src/lib/appUrl.ts:25`), promises a tab for every string,
and noUncheckedIndexedAccess, which the shared config's `tsconfig.base.json` leaves off, would only add
undefined to that. The same lookup shape exists at `src/lib/codecKb.ts:183` and `src/lib/containerKb.ts:10`,
where the name is one the media library supplies and no file content was found to reach it (see NV-24); the
same idiom would fit there. Question for a maintainer: harden the three lookups together, or leave them? To
confirm: add a case for a tab named constructor that expects null beside the unknown-name case at
`tests/unit/appUrl.test.ts:34` (by reading, the new case would fail today) and run
`npx vitest run --config configs/vitest.config.ts tests/unit/appUrl.test.ts tests/unit/tabs.test.ts`.
Confirmed by two independent readings, one of which modelled the lookup in a standalone snippet outside the
repository.

<a name="nv-12"></a>**NV-12: A failed or overlapping load leaves the page showing one file and holding another.** `loadSource`
(`src/ui/fileLoading.ts:63-124`) resets the shared state before it reads anything
(`src/ui/fileLoading.ts:65`), awaits up to four times with no token, and on failure only shows the error and
reopens the drop zone (`src/ui/fileLoading.ts:119-123`); four sequences leave the screen and the state out of
step.

- **A load, then a load that fails.** `#app` is hidden only by Reset and by the demos page
  (`src/ui/fileLoading.ts:137`, `src/ui/demosPage.ts:317`), never by a failure, so the first file's tabs stay
  on screen under an error about the second, with the first file's decoder already disposed. By most failure
  points the state is partly refilled from the failed file (`src/ui/fileLoading.ts:79-80, 87-88, 96`; only a
  failed URL fetch leaves the source null), so Full Analysis builds a document named for the failed file and
  flipping the Educational switch redraws every tab from that partial state (`src/main.ts:87-90`).
- **Reset or a second load while one is in flight.** A collapsed zone hides Choose File, Browse Demo Files
  and the URL controls (`src/style.css:361-363`), but Reset and drops stay live
  (`src/ui/fileLoading.ts:176-181, 199-204`) and neither cancels the pending load. Reset during a download
  returns to the drop zone and the file then appears anyway; Reset after `src/ui/fileLoading.ts:79` lets the
  load finish with `state.source` null (the bar names the file, Inspect lacks its overview cards, Full
  Analysis is blank); and a file dropped during a slow URL load is replaced when the earlier request finishes
  last, `state.input = input;` (`src/ui/fileLoading.ts:104`) overwriting an Input that is never disposed.
- **A load that finishes under the demos page.** The page saves `#app`'s display when it opens
  (`if (appDisplay === null) appDisplay = els.app.style.display;`, `src/ui/demosPage.ts:316`) and puts it
  back when it closes (`src/ui/demosPage.ts:257`). With a link carrying both ?demos and ?src= the load starts
  at `src/main.ts:92`, the page opens at `src/main.ts:96` and saves "none", the finished load shows the tabs
  under the demo browser (`src/ui/fileLoading.ts:114`), and Back to the file picker restores "none": a
  collapsed bar naming a loaded file with no tabs, until Reset or another load.
- **Reset or a new load during a seeking run.** The run holds no token either
  (`src/ui/seekTab.ts:100-124`), and the loader hides the error banner and then disposes the Input the run is
  decoding from (`state.input?.dispose();`, `src/lib/state.ts:104`). If the pending decode rejects, the run's
  catch puts "Seeking test failed" back on the reset page or above the new file; if it does not,
  `state.seekResults = results;` (`src/ui/seekTab.ts:116`) writes the old file's results into the new file's
  state (which of the two happens depends on mediabunny and was not run).

A fix changes what Reset, a second load and a failed load do, so none of it is a cleanup. Question for a
maintainer: hide `#app` when a load starts or fails, or keep the old file's state until the new one has
parsed; and should loads and the seeking run carry a token checked after each await (the pattern at
`src/ui/samplePicker.ts:87-101`), with a superseded Input disposed and `#app`'s display taken from
`state.source` when the demos page closes? To confirm: none of the 28 cases in
`tests/unit/fileLoading.test.ts` fails a load after a successful one, overlaps two loads or resets mid-load,
and `tests/unit/demosPage.test.ts:372-386, 516-524` pin the snapshot only for a display that does not change
under the page, so add those cases first and run
`npx vitest run --config configs/vitest.config.ts tests/unit/fileLoading.test.ts tests/unit/demosPage.test.ts tests/unit/seekTab.test.ts`;
the seeking case also needs a browser. Confirmed by two independent readings; how long each load stage takes
and what the decoder does after dispose are inferred, not run.

<a name="nv-13"></a>**NV-13: The fallback file input cannot re-pick the same file after Reset.** `pickFile` falls back to the
hidden `#fileInput` (`index.html:115`) when `window.showOpenFilePicker` is missing or fails for any reason
but a cancel (`src/ui/fileLoading.ts:147-164`), and each pick waits on one change event:
`els.fileInput.onchange = () => resolve(els.fileInput.files?.[0] || null);` (`src/ui/fileLoading.ts:161`).
Nothing clears the input's value: its only uses in `src/` are that line, the click on the next line and its
registration at `src/ui/elements.ts:19`. Where the engine fires no change event for an unchanged selection,
choosing the same file again after Reset does nothing, with no message. That browser half is INFERRED: Blink and
WebKit are believed to suppress the event (from memory, not checked) and Firefox was not verified, but the shared `bindDropzone` helper
in `@brain-bbqs/ui` empties the input after every pick, and its type declaration gives the reason as letting
the same file be picked again. The fix is one line (empty the input's value before the click at
`src/ui/fileLoading.ts:162`), yet it changes what a user of those browsers sees, so it is listed here and not
as a cleanup; NV-19 holds the wider choice of adopting the shared helper. Question for a maintainer: clear
the value locally now, or leave it to that decision? To confirm: by hand in Safari, pick a file, press Reset
and pick the same file; the three fallback tests call `onchange` directly
(`tests/unit/fileLoading.test.ts:374, 388, 403`) and would pass either way. Confirmed by two independent
readings of the code; the browser behaviour is inferred, not run.

<a name="nv-14"></a>**NV-14: The seeking test's inputs and lifetime are unchecked.** The seeking test on the Inspect tab
(`src/ui/seekTab.ts:83-129`) takes its sample count and its file on trust, and the GOP card around it does
the same with the keyframe list.

- **(a) Sample Count.** The only check is `const n = parseInt(nInput.value, 10) || DEFAULT_SEEK_SAMPLES;`
  (`src/ui/seekTab.ts:84`); the min and max attributes at `src/ui/seekTab.ts:65-66` bound the spinner, not
  typed text. Typing -5 gives an empty run that prints "NaN s", "NaN ms" and "-Infinity ms" (`seekSummary` has
  no empty guard, `src/lib/seekReport.ts:23-29`); 100000 runs that many decodes one after another with no
  Stop control and then builds a table of as many rows; 1e3 parses as 1.
- **(b) Spreads of per-keyframe arrays.** `gopLengths` has one entry per keyframe
  (`src/lib/mp4boxParser.ts:119-124`) and is spread into Math.min and Math.max at `src/ui/seekTab.ts:27-28`,
  `src/ui/seekTab.ts:138` and `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:282`, so the engine's argument limit decides which files
  load (the spreads at `src/lib/seekReport.ts:27` and `src/ui/seekTab.ts:220-221` are bounded by the typed
  count, not by the file). The limit was measured at about 125,000 arguments in a standalone Node snippet
  outside the repository (V8; other engines not measured), which an all-intra recording reaches at about 70
  minutes of 30 fps video: the throw lands inside the loader's try after `#app` is shown
  (`src/ui/fileLoading.ts:114-123`), so the load is reported as failed with Inspect half built.
- **(c) Error slot and earlier results.** A failed run writes the page's error slot itself
  (`src/ui/seekTab.ts:118-124`) and only the loader ever hides it (`src/ui/fileLoading.ts:64`,
  `src/ui/fileLoading.ts:135`), so a later successful run leaves "Seeking test failed" standing until the next
  load or Reset. `state.seekResults` is written on success only (`src/ui/seekTab.ts:116`) while the card is
  emptied at every start (`src/ui/seekTab.ts:99`), so after a good run and then a failed one the card is empty
  beside an error while Full Analysis still prints the earlier run (`src/ui/analysisTab.ts:298-299`); that
  run is a complete measurement of the file still loaded, so this is a page and a document that disagree, not
  output shown to be wrong.
- **(d) GOP histogram.** `renderGopHistogram` draws one flex child per GOP (`src/ui/seekTab.ts:140-145`) in a
  row with `gap: 2px` and `flex: 1` bars (`src/style.css:1039-1051`, repeated for the document at
  `src/lib/analysisDoc.ts:427-435`), and no rule in `src/style.css` clips or scrolls an ancestor, so N bars
  need 2(N-1) px of gaps before any bar has width. INFERRED from the flexbox rules, not rendered: from about
  430 GOPs in the page's widest card (856px by the stylesheet's arithmetic; about 390 in the document's
  column of some 770px) the bars have no width and the row overflows the card, which a seven-minute recording
  with a keyframe every second reaches, and all-intra footage at 30 fps reaches in about 14 seconds.
- **Lifetime.** Nothing ties a run to the file it started on, and what a Reset or a new load during a run
  then produces was not determined, because it depends on what mediabunny does with a decode after dispose:
  see NV-12.

Each fix is a behaviour change: a clamp changes what an out-of-range entry does (an upper clamp breaks no
test; a lower clamp at 5 breaks the cases that type 2 and 3, `tests/unit/seekTab.test.ts:186, 198, 216, 279`);
folding instead of spreading is identical for every file that loads now and lets a file load that fails
today; hiding the slot when a run starts changes the page; binning or capping the bars breaks
`tests/unit/seekTab.test.ts:430-438`, which pins one bar per interval. Question for a maintainer: which of
these to take, and should the card redraw from `state.seekResults` or the state follow the card? To confirm:
add cases to `tests/unit/seekTab.test.ts` (a count of -5; a failed run after a good one; a `gopLengths` of
200,000 entries) and an empty run to `tests/unit/seekReport.test.ts`, then run
`npx vitest run --config configs/vitest.config.ts tests/unit/seekTab.test.ts tests/unit/seekReport.test.ts tests/unit/analysisTab.test.ts`;
the histogram needs a look in a browser with a long file. Confirmed by two independent readings; (d) and the
browser side of (b) are inferred, and the lifetime case is undetermined.

<a name="nv-15"></a>**NV-15: Every ffmpeg console line takes the popover wrapper's `display: inline-flex`.** `logLine` gives each
console line the classes `l` and its level, info unless told otherwise:
`const line = h("div", "l " + level, msg);` (`src/ui/formControls.ts:154-161`), and every line of ffmpeg
output is logged at that level (`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:246`, `src/ui/segmentRun.ts:295`,
`src/ui/inBrowserEncode.ts:45`). The bare `.info` rule written for the info-icon wrapper
(`src/style.css:609-613`, a span built at `src/lib/dom.ts:84-86`) therefore matches those divs as well and
sets `display: inline-flex`, and the console's own rules set only padding and colour
(`src/style.css:1413-1427`), so nothing overrides it. INFERRED from the cascade, not rendered: consecutive
info lines share a row with nothing between them, most visibly in a wide window, while success, warn and
error lines stay blocks (the B-frames badge, a `span.badge.info`, escapes because `.badge` at
`src/style.css:695-701` comes later at equal specificity). The smallest fix is one declaration,
`display: block` on `.log-console .l`, whose specificity (0,2,0) beats the wrapper's (0,1,0) with no rename
and no test touched; it is still a change to what the console draws, as are the alternatives (scoping the
wrapper rule to `span.info`, which at (0,1,1) would then outrank `.badge` and change that badge's display as
well, or renaming one side, which the tests pin at `tests/unit/dom.test.ts:25`, `tests/unit/dom.test.ts:31`
and `tests/unit/formControls.test.ts:96`). Question for a maintainer: is one line per row the intended look,
and which fix is preferred? To confirm: no integration test, story or snapshot opens the console (a search
for log-console and Output console under `tests/chromatic`, `tests/integration` and `stories` finds nothing),
so run a sample encode in the app and open the Output console, before and after. Confirmed by two independent
readings of the stylesheets; the rendering is inferred, not run.

<a name="nv-16"></a>**NV-16: The Markdown export does not escape what it writes into tables and headings.** The Markdown renderer
(`src/lib/analysisDoc.ts:96-153`) passes the body cells of its tables through `escapeCell`
(`src/lib/analysisDoc.ts:92-94`), which handles the backslash and the pipe and nothing else, and escapes
nothing anywhere else.

- **Line breaks in a cell.** A tag value read from the file reaches a row as `String(v)`
  (`src/ui/analysisTab.ts:237-240`, from `flattenMetadataTags` at `src/ui/inspectTab.ts:56-74`) with its line
  breaks intact, so a value holding one (a comment, a description, lyrics) splits one table row into two
  lines, and an empty line inside it ends the table. It shows in Download Markdown and Copy Markdown only;
  the preview and the HTML file are unaffected.
- **Table headers.** `"| " + block.headers.join(" | ") + " |",` (`src/lib/analysisDoc.ts:113`) writes headers
  unescaped; every header today is an app constant with no pipe or backslash (`src/lib/seekReport.ts:9`,
  `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:422`), so routing them through `escapeCell` would change no output.
- **Title, facts and section titles.** These are interpolated as they stand
  (`src/lib/analysisDoc.ts:140, 146, 149`); of them only the file name is free text from the user's file and
  it is not a table cell, so the impact is low.
- **Other markup.** `escapeCell` neutralises table syntax only, so angle-bracket markup or Markdown syntax in
  a tag value reaches the exported file verbatim, while the HTML export writes the same value as text
  (`src/lib/dom.ts:35`).

Mapping a line break to a space or a break tag, or escaping more than table syntax, changes the exported
Markdown for the files it affects, which is why this is not a cleanup (the header change alone is neutral
today). Question for a maintainer: what should a multi-line value become in a table cell, and should cells
escape more than the pipe and the backslash? To confirm: `tests/unit/analysisDoc.test.ts:157-163` covers the
pipe only, so add a case with a value holding a line break and run
`npx vitest run --config configs/vitest.config.ts tests/unit/analysisDoc.test.ts`. Confirmed by two
independent readings; that mediabunny hands line breaks in tag values through unchanged is inferred, not
run.

<a name="nv-17"></a>**NV-17: The Full Analysis preview frame: 2px short, cannot shrink, and its links navigate the frame.**
`mountPreview` (`src/ui/analysisTab.ts:488-502`) sizes the preview iframe to the document inside it so that,
as the comment at `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:481-485` puts it, the page scrolls once; four things work against
that, read from the code and the standards and none of them rendered here.

- **2px short.** `fit()` sets
  `frame.style.height = Math.max(doc.body.scrollHeight, doc.documentElement.scrollHeight) + "px";`
  (`src/ui/analysisTab.ts:493`), but every element is border-box (`src/style.css:189-191`) and `.doc-preview`
  has `border: 1px solid var(--border);` (`src/style.css:2212`), so the frame's viewport is 2px shorter than
  the document it was measured from. INFERRED: 2px of scroll inside the frame, and a standing scrollbar
  where scrollbars take up space; the root's scrollHeight does not change, so the observer never corrects it.
- **Cannot shrink.** A root element's scrollHeight is never below its viewport height, so the maximum at
  `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:493` never falls below the frame's current inner height. INFERRED: when a narrow
  window is widened in one jump and the document reflows shorter, blank page stays under it until the tab is
  revisited (`src/ui/tabs.ts:17-19`); a slow drag closes the gap only because each callback shaves the 2px
  above.
- **Links.** The app link in the document's header has no target (`src/lib/analysisDoc.ts:189`), the document
  has no base element and the frame no sandbox, so in the preview it loads the app inside the frame; where
  that address is the app's own origin, Save as PDF then prints the nested app
  (`src/ui/analysisTab.ts:536-541`) while Download HTML still writes the document. Whether the Contents
  entries (fragment links, `src/lib/analysisDoc.ts:169`) scroll or navigate the frame was not determined: by
  the HTML Standard's base URL rule for srcdoc documents they are expected to navigate it, which needs a
  browser to settle.
- **Observers.** Each load of the frame runs `previewObserver = new ResizeObserver(fit);`
  (`src/ui/analysisTab.ts:498`) from a listener that is not once-only (`src/ui/analysisTab.ts:489`), without
  disconnecting the observer before it; it takes an in-frame navigation to matter and the leak is negligible.

A fix to the height or the shrink changes what is drawn, and jsdom cannot pin it: it reports 0 for the
frame's offsetHeight and clientHeight, the unit test stubs both document heights
(`tests/unit/analysisTab.test.ts:550-577`) and the integration test only asks for a frame taller than 1000px
(`tests/integration/smoke.spec.ts:152-154`). A fix to the links either adds a click handler in the preview,
leaving the exported file alone, or changes the exported file (a base element, a target on the app link).
Question for a maintainer: take the frame's own border into the measurement (or move the border to a
wrapper), clear the height before measuring, and decide what links in the preview should do? To confirm: add
to `tests/integration/smoke.spec.ts` an assertion that the frame's clientHeight is at least its document's
scrollHeight, and a click on the first Contents entry followed by a check that the document's title
(`.doc-head h1`) is still attached, then run `npm run test:integration`; disconnecting the previous observer
before `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:498` is the one neutral change here. Confirmed by two independent readings;
every rendering outcome is inferred, not run, and the Contents links are undetermined.

<a name="nv-18"></a>**NV-18: The page and the document already disagree on several figures and labels.** The pages and the Full
Analysis document state the same facts from separate hand-written lists (DUP-1); in the places below the two
already differ, so sharing a list there would change one side.

- **Chroma.** The page lists the file's chroma subsampling
  (`if (vt.chroma) g.append(gridItem("Chroma", vt.chroma, { sm: true }));`, `src/ui/inspectTab.ts:157`); the
  document's video rows (`src/ui/analysisTab.ts:139-150`) have no such row, and its only chroma text is a
  prose block that is always stripped (`src/ui/analysisTab.ts:155-157`).
- **Min / Max GOP with no GOPs.** The page prints "0 / 0 frames" (`src/ui/seekTab.ts:27-28, 36`) and the
  document a dash (`src/ui/analysisTab.ts:282`); both are pinned (`tests/unit/seekTab.test.ts:458`,
  `tests/unit/analysisTab.test.ts:643`).
- **Constant-bitrate file with Educational off.** The page's Video Bitrate Over Time card holds only its
  heading, because the declared rate lives inside a teach box the switch hides
  (`src/ui/inspectTab.ts:184-188`, `src/lib/dom.ts:163-168`) and the Video Track card has no bitrate row
  (`src/ui/inspectTab.ts:147-148`), while the document prints a Declared Bitrate row
  (`src/ui/analysisTab.ts:171`). The demo set has a file built for this card
  (`scripts/generate-demos.sh:398-401`); whether it passes the flatness test was not run.
- **No timeline.** With three frames or fewer, or no duration, the timeline is null
  (`src/lib/bitrateTimeline.ts:64-68`) and a file that declares a constant bitrate (ffmpeg-muxed files do, by
  the comment at `tests/unit/inspectTab.test.ts:86`) still takes the constant branch through `!timeline ||`
  (`src/ui/inspectTab.ts:185`, `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:167`). The page's note then says its sample sizes bear
  the declaration out (`src/lib/explainers.ts:528-529`) though no window was measured; the accurate
  too-few-frames card is the next branch.
- **Static atom map.** The document's blocks take their title and aria-label from `rectDetail` with its
  default `zoomable = true` (`src/ui/atomsTab.ts:152, 177, 218`), so every collapsed group in a script-free
  file ends with the hint built by `(zoomable ? " · click to zoom in on them" : "")`
  (`src/ui/atomsTab.ts:227`), seen on hover in the preview and the exported HTML of a fragmented recording.
- **Titles and a note from before a rename.** The document says "Reencode CLI Command"
  (`src/ui/analysisTab.ts:327`) for the page's FFmpeg Command Builder (`src/ui/encodeTab.ts:60`) and
  "In-Browser Reencode Result" (`src/ui/analysisTab.ts:431`) where the page says Transcode for a non-MP4
  source (`src/ui/inBrowserEncode.ts:21, 34-36`); titles differ elsewhere too (MP4 Atom Map,
  `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:251`, against the card's MP4 Box / Atom Structure, `src/ui/atomsTab.ts:80`). The
  sample run's note still reads "Encoding test segment" with a percentage (`src/ui/encodeTab.ts:262, 269`),
  which the whole-file run dropped (`src/ui/inBrowserEncode.ts:46`).
- **Rows on one side only.** The document prints ?×? where the page skips the Video Track card for an unknown
  coded size (`src/ui/analysisTab.ts:141`, `src/ui/inspectTab.ts:136`), drops the GOP section without samples
  (`src/ui/analysisTab.ts:272`), adds a Timestamps Sampled row and a Projected Saving row
  (`src/ui/analysisTab.ts:306, 370-374`; the page gives the saving as a headline at
  `src/ui/savingsPanel.ts:57`) and lacks the page's Sampled row (`src/ui/savingsPanel.ts:82-87`). The
  whole-file Change row carries the factor on the page only (`src/ui/cliControls.ts:153`,
  `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:438`), and a metadata tag is labelled by its name on the page and by its key with
  the name in brackets in the document (`src/ui/inspectTab.ts:121`, `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:239`).
- **Page-only attributes in the export.** `renderStaticAtomMap` reuses `positionBlock`
  (`src/ui/atomsTab.ts:178`), so every exported block carries `data-frac` and `data-chars`
  (`src/ui/atomsTab.ts:150-151`), which only the page's label sizing reads (`src/ui/atomsTab.ts:314`); moving
  them to the page-only path changes the exported bytes and not the rendering.

Each alignment changes a page a user sees or a file a user keeps, and several of the strings are pinned
(`tests/unit/encodeTab.test.ts:410`, `tests/unit/encodeTab.test.ts:415`, `tests/unit/analysisTab.test.ts:160`,
`tests/unit/analysisTab.test.ts:302`, `tests/integration/smoke.spec.ts:143`), so none is a cleanup. Question
for a maintainer: which side is right in each case, and should the shared lists of DUP-1 carry the answer?
To confirm: add cases for a track carrying chroma in the document, the constant-bitrate fixture at
`tests/unit/inspectTab.test.ts:72-83` with the switch off, and a grouped block's title in the static map at
`tests/unit/atomsTab.test.ts:210-216`, then run
`npx vitest run --config configs/vitest.config.ts tests/unit/inspectTab.test.ts tests/unit/seekTab.test.ts tests/unit/atomsTab.test.ts tests/unit/encodeTab.test.ts tests/unit/analysisTab.test.ts`
and `npm run test:integration`. Confirmed by two independent readings.

<a name="nv-19"></a>**NV-19: The drop zone: no window-level guard, every drop cancelled, and a guard for a click handler that does
not exist.** The drop zone is wired by hand (`src/ui/fileLoading.ts:194-204`) while `@brain-bbqs/ui` ships a
`bindDropzone` helper for the same job (its `dist/dropzone.js`); compared behaviour by behaviour, the two
differ in ways a user can see, so swapping one for the other is a product decision.

- **Window guard.** `src/` registers no drag listener on the window or the document, so a file dropped beside
  the zone gets the browser's default handling, while the helper installs a window-level guard unless told
  not to. INFERRED, not run: without a guard the tab navigates to the dropped file, and the helper's guard
  would also cancel text dropped into any input on the page.
- **Every drop cancelled at the zone.** The zone's drop listener calls `e.preventDefault();` before it looks
  for a file (`src/ui/fileLoading.ts:199-204`) and the URL box sits inside the zone (`index.html:100-103`
  within `index.html:92-114`), so a link or text dragged onto the box lights the zone up and inserts nothing
  (INFERRED from the HTML drag-and-drop model, not run). The helper cancels at the zone in the same way.
- **Drag half.** With its guard off and no input passed, the helper differs from the local listeners only in
  also handling dragenter (the highlight arrives slightly earlier) and in calling preventDefault on dragleave
  (no effect on that event); the three drag and drop tests (`tests/unit/fileLoading.test.ts:244, 256, 264`)
  would pass either way.
