- **Picker half.** Given the file input and a pick callback, the helper makes a click anywhere on the zone
  open the picker and empties the input after each pick, which would settle NV-13. Choose File, Load from
  URL, the URL box and its Load button sit inside this zone and do not stop propagation
  (`index.html:96-102`), so a click on any of them would open the picker too: it does not fit the zone as it
  stands.
- **A guard with nothing to guard.** Reset and Browse Demo Files call `e.stopPropagation();` under comments
  saying the zone beneath them opens the file picker (`src/ui/fileLoading.ts:176-181`,
  `src/ui/demosPage.ts:322-326`), and the zone has `cursor: pointer;` (`src/style.css:338`), but `src/` puts no
  click listener on the zone and git history shows one only in two test commits. Today the calls only keep
  those clicks from the document-level listener that closes info popovers (`src/lib/dom.ts:55`), and two
  tests pin them (`tests/unit/fileLoading.test.ts:325-331`, `tests/unit/demosPage.test.ts:347-357`).

Every choice here changes what a click or a drop does, so none is a cleanup. Question for a maintainer: adopt
the helper's drag half, with or without the window guard; cancel only drags that carry files, or let drops on
the URL box through; and either make the zone open the picker, which is what the comments and the cursor
promise and needs the controls inside it reworked first, or drop the two calls, their comments, the pointer
cursor and the two test pins? To confirm: `npm run lint` (in the helper's callback the dropped file is typed
as always present, so a truthiness test on it should trip the no-unnecessary-condition rule; test the list's
length instead), then
`npx vitest run --config configs/vitest.config.ts tests/unit/fileLoading.test.ts tests/unit/demosPage.test.ts`
and `npm run test:integration`; the drop outcomes need a browser, since jsdom has no default drop action.
Confirmed by two independent readings; the browser outcomes are inferred, not run.

<a name="nv-20"></a>**NV-20: Displayed commands name files the run does not use.** `src/lib/cliCommand.ts:130` ends every
argument list with `args.push(outName || encodedFileName("MP4"));` and `src/lib/cliCommand.ts:103` names the
input `inName || "in.mp4"`. Of the three places that display a command for the user to run, only the Reencode
tab passes an output name (`src/ui/cliControls.ts:28`, `encodedFileName(state.format)`); "Run This Setting
with ffmpeg" (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:814`) and the document's "Reencode CLI Command"
(`src/ui/analysisTab.ts:335`) pass none, and none of the three passes an input name. For a source that is not
MP4 (in practice QuickTime, since the loader only accepts what mp4box parses, `src/ui/fileLoading.ts:84-86`)
the Reencode tab's command therefore ends in "video-transcoded.mp4" while the other two end in
"video-reencoded.mp4", although `CHANGELOG.md:105` says the command follows the saved file's naming; and all
three read "-i in.mp4" for a `.mov`, where the whole-file encode itself runs with
`"in" + extOf(state.source.name)` (`src/ui/inBrowserEncode.ts:54`). A fix changes text that is displayed,
copied and exported, so it is not a quality change, and it has to be made at the display sites:
`src/ui/compareTab.ts:385` hashes the argument list, default names included, into the measurement-cache key
(`src/lib/matrixCache.ts:431-432`), so changing the defaults would orphan stored measurements, and
`tests/unit/cliCommand.test.ts:127-128` pins them. Question for a maintainer: should the two other commands
follow the Reencode tab's naming, and should the input placeholder carry the source's extension (the docstring at
`src/lib/cliCommand.ts:89-90` says the displayed command names no particular file on purpose)? To confirm: in the "command for the selected
square" suite (`tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts:653`) set `state.format` to "QuickTime File Format" and expect
"video-transcoded.mp4"; by this reading the command ends in "video-reencoded.mp4". Confirmed by two
independent readings.

<a name="nv-21"></a>**NV-21: Numbers typed into the builder and the sample fields reach the command and the sweep unchecked.**
Four number fields store what is typed without applying the range their `min` and `max` attributes advertise
(`src/ui/formControls.ts:57-58`), the frame-rate axis doubles a sweep when the source rate is unknown, and
three smaller questions about the sweep sit beside them; a clamp, or any of the other fixes, changes what a run
executes or shows, so none is a quality change.

- CRF, keyframe interval and FPS in the builder (`src/ui/encodeTab.ts:155-159`, `src/ui/encodeTab.ts:170-172`,
  `src/ui/encodeTab.ts:190-196`): `cli.crf = parseInt(crfInput.value, 10) || 0;` has no upper bound, so a typed
  99 gives "-crf 99", an FPS of -5 gives "-r -5" and, through `computeGop`, "-g 1"
  (`src/lib/cliCommand.ts:83`, `src/lib/cliCommand.ts:127`), and an interval of 1000 gives "-g 30000" at 30 fps.
  These are the arguments the sample run and the whole-file encode execute (`src/ui/encodeTab.ts:263`,
  `src/ui/inBrowserEncode.ts:58`), not only the ones displayed.
- Duration (`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:73-76`): `encodeTest.duration = parseFloat(duration.value) || 1;` stores a
  typed 50 as 50, so a sweep samples stretches of up to 50 seconds from a field whose `max` is at most 10
  (`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:52-54`, `src/ui/segmentRun.ts:67`). Segments (`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:79`) and Custom %
  (`src/ui/cliControls.ts:94`) clamp in their handlers; Duration is clamped only when the tab is next rendered
  (`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:65`).
- A negative Duration: a typed -3 is stored as -3, `pickSampleWindows` then returns no windows
  (`src/lib/sizeEstimate.ts:257`), and the sweep falls back to one stretch of -3 seconds from
  `encodeTest.startTime` (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:332-335`, `src/ui/compareTab.ts:701-705`), cut with "-t -3"
  (`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:228-245`). That fallback is meant for a file of unknown length
  (`tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts:921-943`); apart from overlapping runs this is the only case in which
  `encodeTest.startTime` can be read with a non-zero value (here at `src/ui/compareTab.ts:334`), which bears
  on CON-2.
- Unknown source rate: with `state.fps` null, `fpsForFraction` returns null for every fraction
  (`src/lib/qualityMatrix.ts:101-104`), so all three Frame rates ticks read "Source"
  (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:694-697`) and ticking two enters every combination twice under one key, because
  `const fpsAxis = fpsValues.length ? fpsValues : [null];` (`src/lib/qualityMatrix.ts:192`) does not
  de-duplicate; each is then encoded twice. It needs a file with no packet rate from mediabunny and no positive
  duration (`src/ui/fileLoading.ts:55-58`, `src/ui/fileLoading.ts:111`), and
  `tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts:339-346` asserts the doubled count on a fixture in that state.
- Measured bytes overwritten: clicking a released or cached square re-encodes it with the builder's current
  values for everything the sweep does not vary (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:657`) and writes the result's size onto
  it (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:662`), so once a builder option such as audio or B-frames has changed, that square
  shows a size at the new settings beside neighbours measured at the old, where the comment at
  `src/ui/abPanel.ts:101-102` says such a square still reports what the sweep measured.
- Eviction: `evictBeyondBudget` has one call, inside the sweep loop (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:570`), so outside a
  sweep nothing is evicted and outputs re-encoded for clicked squares can accumulate past the 192 MiB budget
  (`src/lib/qualityMatrix.ts:115`).
- "select all": it is the only child of a row styled `justify-content: space-between;`
  (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:204-205`, `src/style.css:1896-1902`), so it has sat at the left since #35 removed the
  reuse tick beside it; before #31 it was right-aligned. The rule's comment still describes the tick (CL-2).

Question for a maintainer: should each field clamp to its advertised range in its input handler, should the
reduced-rate ticks be withheld (or the resolved rates de-duplicated) when the source rate is unknown, and are
the last three as intended? To confirm: type 99, -5 and -3 into the fields in `tests/unit/encodeTab.test.ts`
and `tests/unit/segmentRun.test.ts:199-214` and read `cli` and `encodeTest.duration`; with `state.fps` null,
tick two rates, run, and count the distinct keys in `encodeTest.matrix.cells`, which by this reading is half
the number of cells. Confirmed by two independent readings; that a number input hands back an out-of-range
entry unchanged, what ffmpeg does with each value, and where the row places its only child are inferred, not
run.

<a name="nv-22"></a>**NV-22: Progress, notices and the save step during encodes.** Seven things a user sees, or does not see,
around an encode; apart from the two neutral variants noted in the bullets, each fix changes what is shown,
when a dialog opens or what counts as a failed run, so none is a quality change.

- Progress of a pre-cut stretch: `const length = input.trim?.seconds ?? encodeTest.duration;`
  (`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:286`) divides by the Compare tab's Duration field whenever the stretch was cut
  beforehand (`trim` is null, `src/ui/segmentRun.ts:386`), which is the normal case, yet the Reencode tab's
  sample is a fixed 3 seconds (`src/lib/sampleTimeline.ts:13`) and the field defaults to 5
  (`src/lib/state.ts:63`). The bar and the "Encoding test segment" percentage (`src/ui/encodeTab.ts:263-270`)
  therefore stop at 60% and jump to full (30% with the field at 10, full a second early with it at 2), against
  the intent recorded at `CHANGELOG.md:287`.
- Whole-file bar: `finishFill(fill);` (`src/ui/inBrowserEncode.ts:72`) fills the bar under a comment promising
  a full one, and the `finally` block hides it (`src/ui/inBrowserEncode.ts:81`) with only synchronous code
  between, so the full bar is never painted (inferred); on success the sample run and the sweep keep theirs
  (`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:504-510`). Either keep the bar on success, which is visible, or delete the call and
  its comment, which changes nothing a user sees.
- Download notice: the one-time line `Downloading ffmpeg-core (~30 MB, first use only)`
  (`src/lib/ffmpegEngine.ts:69`) goes to a handler that only the default core's `setHandlers` binds
  (`src/lib/ffmpegEngine.ts:173`). The whole-file flow binds it before loading (`src/ui/inBrowserEncode.ts:44`,
  `src/ui/inBrowserEncode.ts:53`), but the sample run (`src/ui/encodeTab.ts:258`), the sweep
  (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:537`) and a click on a cached square (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:654-655`) start the load
  before their first `setHandlers` (`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:246`, `src/ui/segmentRun.ts:294`), so their consoles
  stay empty during the download.
- Square re-encode: clicking a finished square whose output is no longer held encodes it again
  (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:649-657`) with nothing saying so. #35 removed the note that announced it and left a
  comment saying the bar shows that a run is going (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:652-653`), but this path never
  starts the run display and passes an empty progress callback (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:657`), so until the
  comparison loads the only signs are the moved ring and the disabled squares
  (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:750`); CL-1 corrects the comment.
- Save step: `src/ui/inBrowserEncode.ts:61` awaits the whole encode before `src/ui/inBrowserEncode.ts:64` calls
  `pickSaveTarget(saveName)`, by which time the click's user activation has lapsed (inferred), so the picker
  throws and the app falls back to a download with a console warning (`src/lib/save.ts:24-27`); two tester logs
  record that SecurityError (`docs/user_tests/brock.md:111`, `docs/user_tests/erik.md:66`). A dismissed picker
  downloads anyway (`src/ui/inBrowserEncode.ts:65-70`): that is pinned by
  `tests/unit/inBrowserEncode.test.ts:135-140` and is not a defect, though it means nothing uses the
  distinction `pickSaveTarget` draws between a dismissed picker and no picker (`src/lib/save.ts:25`,
  `src/lib/save.ts:29`).
- Exit status: `await ffmpeg.exec(args);` (`src/lib/ffmpegEngine.ts:197`) and the other call, at
  `src/lib/ffmpegEngine.ts:206`, discard what `exec` resolves with, so no caller sees ffmpeg's exit status, and
  the docstring at `src/lib/ffmpegEngine.ts:148-152` says a non-zero exit does not reject. If that is so
  (inferred from the library's API; only the code half is confirmed), `runToFile` records a refused cut as
  present (`src/lib/ffmpegEngine.ts:207`), later runs skip the cut (`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:225`,
  `src/ui/segmentRun.ts:257`) and fail at the read (`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:208`), and an encode that left a
  partial output counts as a success.
- Crash messages: the two messages built by `describeFfmpegFailure` end in the same sentence pair, written
  twice, with a colon at `src/lib/ffmpegEngine.ts:286`
  (`The command itself is sound: run it outside the browser for the exact result.`) and an em-dash in its place
  at `src/lib/ffmpegEngine.ts:294`. One shared constant with the colon changes one punctuation mark in one
  message; sharing only the identical remainder changes nothing.

Question for a maintainer: which of these are intended as they stand, and should the save target be asked for
inside the click, before the encode? To confirm: in `tests/unit/segmentRun.test.ts` set `encodeTest.duration`
to 5 over the 2-second fixtures and expect each stretch to report half-way at one second of output (by this
reading it reports a fifth); let the fake `exec` in `tests/unit/ffmpegEngine.test.ts` resolve 1 and read
`has(name)` after `runToFile` (expected to be true as the code stands); in Chromium, run a whole-file encode
longer than a few seconds and watch for the fallback warning. Confirmed by two independent readings, the exit
status only in part; user activation, painting and the library's exit behaviour are inferred, not run.

<a name="nv-23"></a>**NV-23: Remote sources and track metadata: edge cases read off the code.** Six edge cases in how a remote file
is read and how a file's metadata is loaded and shown; each fix changes what is fetched, named or displayed
for the affected files, so none is a quality change.

- Ranged reads: `readChunk` checks only `resp.ok` before `return await resp.arrayBuffer();`
  (`src/lib/chunkedSource.ts:75-76`), so any 2xx answer to a ranged request is taken as the range, whatever its
  length. A server that advertised ranges but answers 200 with the whole body is harmless for the parser's
  first read at offset 0, but each of `videoChecksum`'s three probes (`src/lib/matrixCache.ts:360-366`) would
  download the whole file; `tests/unit/chunkedSource.test.ts:128-140` itself stubs the ranged read with a 200.
- `Accept-Ranges`: `src/lib/chunkedSource.ts:45` reads the header off the HEAD response, but it is not
  CORS-safelisted, so from another origin it reads null unless the server exposes it (inferred from the Fetch
  standard), and the whole file is then downloaded into a Blob (`src/lib/chunkedSource.ts:59-65`) even from a
  server that supports ranges. This decides whether the ranged branch above runs in production at all; the
  comment at `src/lib/chunkedSource.ts:53-58` declines to probe for ranges on purpose, so any change is a
  policy choice.
- Name from a URL: `const fromPath = url.split("?")[0].split("/").filter(Boolean).pop();`
  (`src/lib/chunkedSource.ts:34`) neither decodes the segment nor drops a fragment, so a link ending
  "my%20clip.mp4" is shown under that name and one ending "clip.mp4#t=10" keeps "#t=10", in the mini bar and in
  the document (`src/ui/fileLoading.ts:118`, `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:105`). Of the two, only the
  percent-encoding reaches saved file names (`src/ui/inBrowserEncode.ts:63`, `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:513`),
  since `baseNameOf` strips from the last dot (`src/lib/save.ts:9-11`).
- Cached import: `mbPromise ??= import("mediabunny");` (`src/lib/mediabunny.ts:12`) keeps a rejected promise
  for the life of the page, so if the preload at `src/main.ts:101-103` fails, every later file load fails with
  the same error until reload (`src/ui/fileLoading.ts:100`). The three other cached promises clear themselves
  on rejection (`src/lib/ffmpegEngine.ts:75-79`, `src/lib/matrixCache.ts:186-191`,
  `src/lib/matrixCache.ts:350-355`); whether a second `import()` would refetch is engine behaviour (inferred).
- Frame rate: `if (d.kind === "video") result.fps = d.packetRate;` (`src/lib/mediabunnyMeta.ts:68`) runs for
  every track, so the last video track's rate wins, while the track object, the Video Track card and mp4box's
  sample table are the first's (`src/lib/mediabunnyMeta.ts:54`, `src/lib/state.ts:112-114`,
  `src/ui/fileLoading.ts:93-94`). For a file with two video tracks at different rates the card prints one rate
  (`src/ui/inspectTab.ts:150`) while GOP seconds, frame distances and the command's "-g" use the other
  (`src/lib/seekReport.ts:35`, `src/ui/seekTab.ts:113`, `src/lib/cliCommand.ts:97-98`).
- Fast start: `detectFaststart` returns null when no top-level `moov` or `mdat` is found
  (`src/lib/mp4boxParser.ts:70`), and its test says that leaves the question open
  (`tests/unit/mp4boxParser.test.ts:243-247`), but both consumers test truthiness (`src/ui/atomsTab.ts:110`,
  `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:262-263`), so "unknown" is shown as the warning badge "Not fast start". After a
  successful parse that means a file with samples and no top-level `mdat`, which is rare.

Question for a maintainer: for each, is today's behaviour the intended one, or should the read require a 206
(or check the length), the name be decoded, a failed import be forgotten, the first video track's rate be used
and a null fast-start get a neutral badge? To confirm: add the cases to `tests/unit/chunkedSource.test.ts`,
`tests/unit/mediabunny.test.ts`, `tests/unit/mediabunnyMeta.test.ts` and `tests/unit/atomsTab.test.ts`, and
load a file from a range-capable host on another origin with the browser's network panel open. Confirmed by
two independent readings; the header filtering, what mp4box does with a whole body at offset 0 and the
engine's handling of a repeated import are inferred, not run.

<a name="nv-24"></a>**NV-24: Knowledge-base accuracy and reach.** Six points about what the knowledge bases say and which of their
entries a loaded file can reach; each is a content decision, so none is proposed as a change.

- u-law and A-law: `describeCodec` files `ulaw` and `alaw` under `PCM (uncompressed)`
  (`src/lib/codecKb.ts:174-182`) with a description that opens
  `Raw, uncompressed audio samples, with no encoding at all. Simple and lossless, but large`
  (`src/lib/explainers.ts:459`). G.711 mu-law and A-law are 8-bit logarithmic companding, which is lossy (this
  reviewer's knowledge of the codec; no source was checked), so "uncompressed", "no encoding" and "lossless" are
  each wrong for them; `tests/unit/codecKb.test.ts:108-111` pins the grouping.
- AV1 chroma: `chromaFromCodecString` reads only the profile digit
  (`const av1 = /^av01\.(\d)/.exec(codecString);`, `src/lib/chromaFormat.ts:135`) and maps 0, 1 and 2 to 4:2:0,
  4:4:4 and 4:2:2 (`src/lib/chromaFormat.ts:136`). Only profile 1 fixes the format: profile 0 may be monochrome
  and profile 2 allows any format at 12 bit (the AV1 specification as recalled, not re-read), so a long-form
  string that itself states monochrome or 4:4:4 is still reported as 4:2:0 or 4:2:2, against the module's rule
  of returning null when the file does not say (`src/lib/chromaFormat.ts:18-20`); the branch is reached only
  when no usable `av1C` record arrives (`src/lib/chromaFormat.ts:150`) and is pinned by
  `tests/unit/chromaFormat.test.ts:111-115`.
- Unreachable records: `state.format` is written only after mp4box has parsed the file
  (`src/ui/fileLoading.ts:84-86`, `src/ui/fileLoading.ts:106`; `src/lib/mp4boxParser.ts:48` throws otherwise),
  and `src/ui/demosPage.ts:33` says as much
  (`mp4box.js reads MP4 and MOV only, so this file lands on the error path.`), so nine of the eleven container
  records (`src/lib/explainers.ts:96-196`) are never looked up, given that mediabunny names such a file MP4 or
  QuickTime (inferred: its format names could not be read). The ID3, Vorbis and RIFF tag tables
  (`src/lib/explainers.ts:307-331`, `src/lib/explainers.ts:334-353`, `src/lib/explainers.ts:356-366`) are
  probably unreachable for the same reason, although the case-insensitive fallback at
  `src/lib/metadataTagKb.ts:71` could still match a free-form MOV key.
- Seven of eleven re-cased: #35 gave the `video` and `audio` fields of MP4 through Ogg sentence case and a full
  stop (`video: "None, this is an audio-only format.",` at `src/lib/explainers.ts:127`) and loosened the test to
  match either (`tests/unit/containerKb.test.ts:34`); FLAC, ADTS, MPEG-TS and HLS keep the earlier style
  (`video: "none, this is an audio-only format",` at `src/lib/explainers.ts:160`, and
  `src/lib/explainers.ts:171-172`, `src/lib/explainers.ts:182-183`, `src/lib/explainers.ts:193-194`). All four
  are among the unreachable nine, so nothing shown today differs.
- Raw tags: `flattenMetadataTags` filters top-level tags to non-empty scalars but merges everything under `raw`
  unfiltered (`Object.assign(flatTags, v);`, `src/ui/inspectTab.ts:61`), and the page and the document
  stringify each value (`src/ui/inspectTab.ts:118`, `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:239`), so a binary or object value
  would print as a comma list of bytes or as "[object Object]". The comment at `src/ui/inspectTab.ts:53-55`
  documents the leniency as deliberate and as the original's; whether mediabunny puts such values there (the
  tag table expects a cover-art key, `src/lib/explainers.ts:285`) is inferred.
- Plain-object lookups: `CODEC_KB[shortCodec]` (`src/lib/codecKb.ts:183`) and `CONTAINER_KB[formatName]`
  (`src/lib/containerKb.ts:10`) index object tables, so an inherited key such as "constructor" would return a
  record of undefined fields from the first and make `containerExplainer` throw at `src/lib/explainers.ts:58`
  for the second. Both keys are mediabunny's own names (inferred to be a closed set), so no file reaches them
  today; if NV-11 is hardened, the same idiom fits here.

Question for a maintainer: for each, correct the copy, keep it as it is, or remove it? To confirm: the
expectations each choice moves are at `tests/unit/codecKb.test.ts:108-111`,
`tests/unit/chromaFormat.test.ts:111-115` and `tests/unit/containerKb.test.ts:24-36`; removing the unreachable
records also moves `tests/unit/explainers.test.ts:58-62`, which uses the WebM record, and removing the three
tag tables moves `tests/unit/metadataTagKb.test.ts:41-50` (edit them, then `npm test`), and loading an MP4 with embedded cover art in a browser settles the raw-tag case. Confirmed by two
independent readings; the codec facts are from memory, and mediabunny's ids, format names and raw values could
not be read, so those parts are inferred, not run.

<a name="nv-25"></a>**NV-25: Number formatting differs at the edges between near-identical formatters.** Several formatters look
like duplicates of one another and print different text at the edges; unifying or correcting any of them
changes output a user sees, some of it pinned by a test, which is why they are listed here and not under
Duplication.

- Three signed percentages for one size change: `fmtSignedChange` (`src/lib/sizeEstimate.ts:337-340`; Estimate
  Detail and the document) prints "-40%", the matrix square's inline copy of the same expression
  (`src/ui/matrixPanel.ts:66`) prints it with the U+2212 minus sign in place of the ASCII hyphen, and
  `fmtSizeChangePct` (`src/lib/format.ts:27-30`; the whole-file result) always keeps a decimal, "-40.0%". At
  exactly no change the first two print "+0.0%" and the third "-0.0%" (`(pct >= 0 ? "-" : "+")`), and each form
  is pinned (`tests/unit/sizeEstimate.test.ts:213`, `tests/unit/matrixPanel.test.ts:124`,
  `tests/unit/format.test.ts:76-78`).
- Summary wording: `src/ui/matrixPanel.ts:273` restates `describeSavings` (`src/lib/sizeEstimate.ts:330-334`)
  without its `about the same size` branch, so below half a percent the sweep summary reads "0.4% smaller"
  where `describeSavings` returns that phrase.
- Precision chosen before rounding: `return (Math.abs(pct) < 10 ? pct.toFixed(1) : pct.toFixed(0)) + "%";`
  (`src/lib/sizeEstimate.ts:323`) prints 9.96% as "10.0%" and 10.04% as "10%"; `fmtChangeFactor` does the same
  at ten times (`src/lib/sizeEstimate.ts:361`).
- Two clocks: `fmtTime` (`src/ui/bitrateChart.ts:61-70`) and `fmtClock` (`src/lib/sampleTimeline.ts:59-65`)
  agree for totals between one minute and one hour and differ at both ends: under a minute 7.5 seconds is "7.5s"
  against "0:08", and past an hour the chart prints "75:00" under an axis titled "Playback time (m:ss)"
  (`src/ui/bitrateChart.ts:160`) where the picker's clock prints "1:15:00".
- Elapsed time: `fmtElapsed` (`src/ui/matrixPanel.ts:38-42`) rounds without carrying
  (`if (ms < 60_000) return Math.round(ms / 1000) + "s";`), so 59.6 seconds prints "60s" and 119.6 seconds
  "1m 60s". The document formats the same figure inline to one decimal of seconds
  (`src/ui/analysisTab.ts:403`), so the two agree only under ten seconds ("95.0s" against "1m 35s").
- The same carry edges in `src/lib/format.ts`: `fmtDur` prints 119.97 seconds as "1m 60.0s" and 3659.6 seconds
  as "1h 0m 60s" (`src/lib/format.ts:7-8`), `fmtBits` prints 999,600 bits per second as "1000 kbps"
  (`src/lib/format.ts:14`), and `fmtRate` prints 29.9999 as "30.000" but 30.002 as "30"
  (`hz % 1 < 0.005 ? Math.round(hz).toString() : hz.toFixed(3)`, `src/lib/format.ts:19`). The chart's own clock
  already carries (`src/ui/bitrateChart.ts:68-69`).
- Chart tooltips: the per-window tooltips (`src/ui/bitrateChart.ts:195-197`) reuse the decimals rule chosen for
  the quarter ticks (`const decimals = Number.isInteger(durationSec / STEPS) ? 0 : 1;`,
  `src/ui/bitrateChart.ts:63`), so a clip of exactly 8 seconds labels a window as running from "1s" to "1s" (4
  of its 12 windows), and one of exactly 4 seconds starts with "0s" to "0s".
- Atom sizes: `fmtSize` (`src/ui/atomsTab.ts:207-212`) compares the shared utils package's `fmtBytes`, which
  prints whole bytes ungrouped, with `const exact = bytes.toLocaleString() + " B";`, so a box of 1000 to 1023
  bytes reads twice over, as in "1000 B (1,000 B)", in a locale that groups four digits, against the
  docstring's `except under 1 KB where they are the same` (`src/ui/atomsTab.ts:207`). It is locale-dependent:
  some locales do not group four digits and print the size once (inferred from the platform's number
  formatting).

Question for a maintainer: one glyph, one precision and one treatment of zero for a signed percentage, and
should a rounded field carry into the next unit everywhere? To confirm: add the values above as cases to
`tests/unit/format.test.ts`, `tests/unit/sizeEstimate.test.ts`, `tests/unit/matrixPanel.test.ts` and
`tests/unit/bitrateChart.test.ts` and run `npm test`. Confirmed by two independent readings, with each example
recomputed outside the repository from the cited expressions; locale behaviour is inferred.

<a name="nv-26"></a>**NV-26: URL handling and the shared shell.** Four observations about the app's address-bar handling and the
shared page shell; each fix changes address-bar text, a scroll position or a button label, so none is a quality
change.

- Bare flag: `writeParam` rebuilds the query through `URLSearchParams`
  (`else url.searchParams.set(name, value);`, `src/lib/appUrl.ts:84-89`), which re-serialises the bare "?demos"
  as "?demos=" (URL standard serialisation; inferred for browsers). Three writers can run while the flag is up:
  the Educational switch (`src/lib/educational.ts:50`), a retired tab name at boot (`src/ui/tabs.ts:38`) and a
  load finishing under the demos page (`src/ui/fileLoading.ts:116`); it is cosmetic, since "?demos=" still reads
  as on (`src/lib/appUrl.ts:116-119`).
- The integration suite passes through that state without seeing it: `tests/integration/smoke.spec.ts:88` boots
  "/?demos&tab=atoms" (through `gotoDemos`, `tests/integration/demoArchive.ts:177-181`), where `initTabs`
  rewrites the retired tab through `writeParam` after `initDemosPage` has run (`src/main.ts:96-97`), and the
  check at `tests/integration/smoke.spec.ts:89` (`await expect(page).toHaveURL(/tab=inspect/);`) matches either
  form. With any fix, assert the exact search string between `gotoDemos` and the demo click.
- What's New: the shared UI package's `initWhatsNew` shows the dialog and then assigns `location.hash` (line 49
  of its `whatsNew.js`), a fragment navigation that fires `popstate` (INFERRED from the HTML Standard as
  recalled; no browser was run). The app's two listeners then act behind the dialog: on the demos page
  `open(false)` has no already-open guard and scrolls to the top (`src/ui/demosPage.ts:311-320`,
  `src/ui/demosPage.ts:328-331`; `close` has a guard at `src/ui/demosPage.ts:252`), and on Full Analysis
  `src/ui/tabs.ts:32` re-shows the tab, which rebuilds the document (`src/ui/tabs.ts:19`).
- Copy Markdown: the shared `copyToClipboard` reads the button's label when the copy completes and puts it back
  1.4 seconds later (lines 58 to 66 of the shared UI package's `dom.js`), so a second press within that time
  (`src/ui/analysisTab.ts:549-551`) captures "Copied!" as the label to restore, and the button stays "Copied!"
  until the tab is rebuilt. The copy itself succeeds; `tests/unit/dom.test.ts:287-299` presses once.

Question for a maintainer: write the flag back bare (or use one serialiser for both writers), guard `open` and
ignore a `popstate` that leaves the tab unchanged (or have the package use `replaceState`), and fix the label
here or upstream? To confirm: in `tests/unit/appUrl.test.ts` set the URL to "?demos", call
`writeEducationalToUrl(false)` and expect "?demos&edu=0" (by this reading it comes out as "?demos=&edu=0"); in
a browser, open What's New after scrolling down the demos page, and press Copy Markdown twice quickly.
Confirmed by two independent readings; the `popstate` premise and the serialisation in a browser are inferred,
not run.

<a name="nv-27"></a>**NV-27: Style rules that reach the wrong element, and labels fitted for a width the page does not keep.** Three places where a generic selector outranks a
specific rule or leaks into an element it was not written for, and a fourth where labels are fitted for one
width and laid out at another; every outcome below is INFERRED from the cascade, since nothing was rendered,
and every fix except deleting the two dead declarations is a visual change.

- `.demo-title` never applies: `.demo-title { margin: 0; font-size: 17px; }` (`src/style.css:2378-2381`) loses
  both declarations to `.section h3` and `.section h3:first-child` (`src/style.css:530-537`) on the only element
  that carries the class, an `h3` inside the `section demo-card` div (`src/ui/demosPage.ts:104`,
  `src/ui/demosPage.ts:147`), so the title renders at 14px with an 8px bottom margin and has done since #28.
  Deleting the two declarations preserves today's rendering (the class stays as a test hook,
  `tests/unit/demosPage.test.ts:80`); raising the selector so that they apply is a visual change.
- Inline "Side-by-Side" heading: on the Reencode tab the comparison's `h3` is the first child of the `ab-inline`
  block inside the sample card (`src/ui/abPanel.ts:302`, `src/ui/abPanel.ts:312`,
  `src/ui/encodeTab.ts:223-225`), so `.section h3:first-child` (`src/style.css:535-537`) removes its top margin
  and it sits directly under the console fold.
- Educational switch: `input[type="checkbox"].switch` sets `height: 1.25rem` and no padding of its own
  (`src/style.css:280-294`), so it takes `padding: 10px` from the generic `input, select` rule
  (`src/style.css:438-448`); under `box-sizing: border-box` (`src/style.css:189-191`) padding and border floor
  it at 22px, not the 20px written.
- Atom-map labels in the document: label sizes are decided once for a 772px column
  (`src/ui/analysisTab.ts:88`, `src/ui/atomsTab.ts:172`) because the document runs no script, but print lays the
  page out at the paper's width (`@page { margin: 14mm; }` with `max-width: none`,
  `src/lib/analysisDoc.ts:460-463`: about 688px on A4 and 710px on Letter), the preview column narrows with its
  window, and blocks clip their labels (`src/lib/analysisDoc.ts:391`). On paper the loss is small, at most a few
  pixels on the longest labels; a narrow preview window is where labels are visibly cut.

Question for a maintainer: delete the two dead `.demo-title` declarations or make them apply, and are the
other three wanted as they render? To confirm: inspect the demo card's title, the Reencode tab's heading after
a sample run and the header switch in a browser's developer tools, and open Full Analysis in a 390px window
and through Save as PDF on a fragmented file; for the two declarations, the Chromatic (Playwright) job, which
runs `npm run test:chromatic` (`.github/workflows/chromatic-playwright.yml:67-84`), shows whether its "Demos
page" snapshots (`tests/chromatic/app.chromatic.test.ts:12-17`) move. Confirmed by two independent readings of
the cascade; inferred, not run.

<a name="nv-28"></a>**NV-28: Labels are not tied to their controls, and the sample picker no longer says where the encode starts.**
Two gaps in the form controls, one of accessibility and one a lost readout; both fixes change behaviour
slightly (a label click would focus its control, the picker would show something new), so neither is a quality
change.

- Labels: `fieldHead` builds `const labelEl = h("label", "field-label", label);` with no `htmlFor` and without
  wrapping the control (`src/ui/formControls.ts:10-16`), `fieldSelect` and `fieldNumber` append the control as a
  sibling (`src/ui/formControls.ts:26-37`, `src/ui/formControls.ts:51-60`), and the seeking test's hand-built
  field does the same (`src/ui/seekTab.ts:61-69`). The twelve selects and number inputs built this way (eleven
  through the two builders, plus the seeking test's) carry no `aria-label` either, so they have no accessible
  name and clicking a label does not focus its control; the fix needs `fieldHead` to be handed the control's id
  (a new parameter, passed by both builders) and the same one line in the seeking test's field.
- Sample picker: the band and its slider text show the unsnapped start
  (`band.setAttribute("aria-valuenow", from.toFixed(1));`, `src/ui/samplePicker.ts:120-127`), while `window()`
  hands the run the keyframe at or before it (`src/ui/samplePicker.ts:80-83`, `src/ui/samplePicker.ts:177`).
  With keyframes ten seconds apart a band drawn at 0:25 to 0:28 encodes 20 s to 23 s, with no overlap
  (`tests/unit/samplePicker.test.ts:122-126` pins that window); the readout line that said so was removed in
  #25, and the docstring at `src/ui/samplePicker.ts:78-79` still claims the track shows it (CL-1).

Question for a maintainer: tie each label to its control, and state or draw the snapped start again? To
confirm: read the accessibility tree of the Reencode tab in a browser (or use a screen reader), and on a file
with sparse keyframes compare the band with the Segment row a finished run shows (`src/ui/abPanel.ts:143`).
Confirmed by two independent readings; no browser or screen reader was used, so the accessible-name outcome is
inferred.

<a name="nv-29"></a>**NV-29: Published demo descriptions point at views the app does not have.** Four description strings in
`scripts/generate-demos.sh` send the user to a frame-type view and to loading a second file into the A/B
window. Two are false: `scripts/generate-demos.sh:393` says `The GOP view shows long B runs` and
`scripts/generate-demos.sh:405` says `Load it in the A/B window against the reference`, but nothing in the app
shows B runs and the A/B window compares an encode with its own source (`src/ui/abPanel.ts:1-2`). Two are
overstated: `scripts/generate-demos.sh:329` (`the frame types in the GOP view`) and
`scripts/generate-demos.sh:388` (`Compare the frame-type pattern`), where the GOP card's only B-frame signal is
a badge that tells "No B-frames" from "Uses B-frames" (`src/ui/seekTab.ts:30-49`). The generator writes each
string into the file's comment tag, its sidecar and the dataset index (`scripts/generate-demos.sh:234-242`),
and the demos page prints it (`src/ui/demosPage.ts:149-150`), so this is published data, not app code: a
rewording reaches users only after the set is regenerated and uploaded again, which is why it is not a quality
change, and whether the archive carries exactly these strings today could not be seen from the repository. A
single reading adds a possible fifth: `scripts/generate-demos.sh:400` says the bitrate-over-time plot flattens
for the CBR file, while the Inspect tab drops that plot when a file declares a constant rate and its samples
agree (`src/ui/inspectTab.ts:185-188`), which depends on the published file. Question for a maintainer: reword
them to what the app shows (the B-frames badge; Compare Quality for the bitrate ceiling), or keep them as a
note of views that are wanted? To confirm: read the `description` entries of the published
`dataset_description.json` against the Inspect tab and the A/B window for those files. Confirmed by two
independent readings, the fifth by one.

### Decisions for maintainers

<a name="nv-30"></a>**NV-30: `tests/` and `stories/` are outside every type check; five places would not compile.** The only
type check is `tsc --noEmit` (`package.json:16`, run in CI by `.github/workflows/lint.yml:32`), and its
project includes `../src` and six config files (`configs/tsconfig.json:6-14`); no included file imports a test
or a story. This is OBSERVED and is the shared configuration's documented design, not an accident of this
repository: the shared config's `eslint.js` (lines 23-24) says "Files outside the tsconfig (the apps' tests/
and stories/) get the non-type-aware rules only", and its Vitest factory sets no type-check option. Read
against the types they use, five places in `tests/` would be rejected. None of them changes what its test
does at run time.

- `tests/fixtures/state.ts:20`: `BASE_CLI: CliState` has no `customScale`, which `src/lib/types.ts:131`
  requires (TS2741, a missing property). This is the drift recorded in CON-9.
- `tests/unit/qualityMatrix.test.ts:124` and `tests/unit/qualityMatrix.test.ts:130`: the literals passed to
  `describeSettings` have no `fps`, which `EncodeSettings` requires (`src/lib/types.ts:155`): TS2345 at each,
  an argument that does not fit its parameter. The literals are older than the field.
- `tests/unit/sizeEstimate.test.ts:401`: `state.source = { kind: "file", name: "clip.mp4", size: 30_000_000 };`
  is not a `ChunkedSource`, which also has `file`, `url`, `supportsRange` and `readChunk`
  (`src/lib/chunkedSource.ts:6-12`, `src/lib/chunkedSource.ts:68`). Six sibling assignments in four other
  test files would compile only because they end in `as never` (for example
  `tests/unit/inspectTab.test.ts:35`), a cast that also hides how incomplete each stand-in is.
- `tests/unit/demosPage.test.ts:171` reads `call[0]` from the call records of a mock whose implementation
  takes no parameters (`tests/unit/demosPage.test.ts:160`), an index into an empty tuple. INFERRED: this
  rests on vitest's `Mock` typing as recalled from versions 1 to 4; version 5's types could not be read.

The first four were also reported by a static pass made for this review with a TypeScript compiler from
outside the repository and no packages installed. With every package import unresolved, such a pass can judge
literals against `src` types and nothing that is typed by vitest, mediabunny, fake-indexeddb or Playwright, so
sites of that kind (for example the zero-argument mock assigned to a typed `Mock` at
`tests/unit/demosPage.test.ts:325`) stay undetermined; nothing was found in `stories/`. A maintainer sees none
of this today: a fixture can disagree with its type (CON-9), and a rename under `src/` that misses a reference
in a test is reported by no check and surfaces, if at all, when that test runs (CL-4). Correcting the five
places is test-only and changes no assertion. Adding the check is not a quality change: CI would fail until
they are corrected and would then enforce something it does not enforce today, and the exclusion belongs to
every app on the shared config. Question for a maintainer: type-check `tests/` and `stories/` in the shared
config, give this app its own tsconfig that includes them, or leave the arrangement as designed? To confirm:
add `"../tests"` and `"../stories"` to the include list in `configs/tsconfig.json`, run `npm run typecheck`,
and expect the five locations, possibly with more from package-typed code. Confirmed by two independent
readings; the fifth place is inferred from vitest's typing.

<a name="nv-31"></a>**NV-31: CI runs Node 20 while four locked packages declare engines that exclude it.** Six workflows pin
`node-version: 20` (`.github/workflows/lint.yml:25`, `.github/workflows/test.yml:24`,
`.github/workflows/deploy.yml:23`, `.github/workflows/preview.yml:21`,
`.github/workflows/chromatic-playwright.yml:48`, `.github/workflows/chromatic-storybook.yml:48`) and nothing
else states a version: `package.json` has no `engines` field and the repository has no version file. Of the
66 distinct Node ranges declared by the lockfile's 449 packages, four exclude every Node 20 release: vitest
5.0.0 (`package-lock.json:6521`, Node "^22.12.0 || ^24.0.0 || >=26.0.0"), `@vitest/istanbul-lib-coverage`
and `@vitest/istanbul-lib-report` 1.0.1 (`package-lock.json:3371`, `package-lock.json:3384`, both `>=22`),
which sit behind the coverage step at `.github/workflows/lint.yml:41`, and chromatic 18.1.0
(`package-lock.json:3723`, `>=22.0.0`), the devDependency that DC-10 proposes to remove; none of the 66
excludes Node 22.21 or 24.11. A maintainer sees nothing today: CI's last run on this commit is green on 20,
so the ranges are not enforced, and the test runner and its coverage reporter run on a Node their authors do
not claim to support (Node 20 also reached its end of life in April 2026; that date is from memory, not
checked for this review). The gap cannot be closed from the other side by lowering the Node types to 20,
because vitest 5.0.0 also peers on `@types/node` at "^22.0.0 || >=24.0.0" (`package-lock.json:6529`; the app
asks for `"@types/node": "^22.9.0",` at `package.json:37`): the runtime has to move, and until it does the
exposure from types a major ahead of it is close to nil, since the only Node API in a type-checked file is
`fileURLToPath` in the two Playwright configs (`configs/playwright.config.ts:3`,
`configs/playwright.chromatic.config.ts:3`). Moving the pins changes the runtime of every job, which is why
this is not a quality change. Question for a maintainer: move the six pins to 22 or 24, in one place if the
version file of DUP-10 is adopted, and add an `engines` field so the next mismatch is stated in the
repository? To confirm: on a branch with the new version, `npm test`, `npm run build` and
`npm run test:integration`, then every workflow on the pull request. Confirmed by two independent readings.

<a name="nv-32"></a>**NV-32: `CHANGELOG.md` against its own rules.** The file is bundled into the app
(`import changelog from "../CHANGELOG.md?raw";` at `src/main.ts:2`, handed to the shared What's New modal at
`src/main.ts:45`), so its gaps are what a user reads. Measured against the project's rules for it
(`CLAUDE.md:6-8`, `CLAUDE.md:14`):

- Five version bumps have no heading: 0.2.1 (#7), 1.3.4 (#39), 1.4.0 (#43), 1.4.2 (#48) and 1.4.3 (#49). The
  headings run from 1.4.4 (`CHANGELOG.md:51`) to 1.4.1 (`CHANGELOG.md:61`) to 1.3.3 (`CHANGELOG.md:68`). The
  one a user would miss is 1.4.0, which removed Google Analytics and the consent banner: the full list the
  modal shows after Show more (it opens on the three newest versions) still carries the entry that added them
  (`CHANGELOG.md:396`) and nothing that says they went.
- One entry is superseded by the next, in the same release and from the same pull request:
  `CHANGELOG.md:115` gives the sweep's total in squares and `CHANGELOG.md:116` in runs, and the app says runs
  (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:81`).
- Of the 128 entries written since the 25-word guide arrived with #18 (178 in all), 50 exceed it, 13 exceed
  30 words and 2 exceed 40 (`CHANGELOG.md:183`, `CHANGELOG.md:185`), counting whitespace-separated words
  without the trailing link. Release 1.3.1 lists 37 entries for one pull request (`CHANGELOG.md:80-123`)
  where `CLAUDE.md:8` asks for related work to be folded into one. The eight newest entries comply.
- Four lines hold eight em-dashes (`CHANGELOG.md:94`, `CHANGELOG.md:105`, `CHANGELOG.md:108`,
  `CHANGELOG.md:214`); CL-9 covers the rest of the repository.

History is history, and no rewrite of old entries is proposed. Every edit to this file changes text the modal
shows, so none of it is a quality change. No test stands in the way: the two that read the file look only at
its version headings and at how many of them the modal shows (`tests/unit/main.smoke.test.ts:15-16`,
`tests/unit/main.smoke.test.ts:119-122`, `tests/unit/main.changelog-link.test.ts:13`). Question for a
maintainer: backfill the five missing headings, or at least 1.4.0 so that What's New says the analytics and
the banner are gone, and delete `CHANGELOG.md:115`? To confirm:
`npx vitest run --config configs/vitest.config.ts tests/unit/main.smoke.test.ts`, then open What's New.
Confirmed by two independent readings; the five missing headings were checked against `package.json` at each
commit in the history.

<a name="nv-33"></a>**NV-33: All three scripts print a hand-counted line range for `-h`, and none matches its header.** Each
script answers `-h` by printing a fixed range of its own header comment:
`sed -n '2,35p' "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}" | sed 's/^# \{0,1\}//'` at `scripts/generate-demos.sh:67`, the same with
`2,21p` at `scripts/upload-demos.sh:36` and with `2,32p` at `scripts/purge-sample-blob.sh:50`. The
generator's header ends one line later, so its help omits the `-o` option (`scripts/generate-demos.sh:36`);
the uploader's help stops in the middle of its last sentence, after "the dandi CLI reads"
(`scripts/upload-demos.sh:21-22`); the purge script's range runs one line past its header, which ends at
`scripts/purge-sample-blob.sh:31`, and only adds a blank line. The generator's range was right when the
script was written and went stale when #28 added a header line; the uploader's has been one line short since
its first commit. None of the three usage lines lists `-h` (`scripts/generate-demos.sh:33`,
`scripts/upload-demos.sh:16`, `scripts/purge-sample-blob.sh:18`), and two sentences inside the printed
ranges are inaccurate in themselves (`scripts/generate-demos.sh:10-12`, `scripts/upload-demos.sh:13-14`;
CL-3 says how). A fix changes what `-h` prints, which is
its whole purpose and the reason it is listed here (two scripts gain a line, the third loses a blank one),
and nothing else: nothing in the repository passes `-h` (the upload workflow passes only `-o demo-out`,
`.github/workflows/upload-demos.yml:32` and `.github/workflows/upload-demos.yml:52`) and each `h)` branch
exits 0 before any tool check or side effect. Question for a maintainer: print from line 2 to the first line
that is not a comment, in all three, so that the range cannot go stale again, list `-h` in the usage
lines, and correct the two inaccurate sentences in the same edit? To confirm: run each script with `-h` and compare the output with its header. Confirmed by two
independent readings of the scripts, which were not run.

<a name="nv-34"></a>**NV-34: `scripts/purge-sample-blob.sh` has not been run.** The script exists to remove the retired sample
video from git history. Its header says the 2.1 MB file was deleted from the working tree by the change that
brought the script, and that the blob stays reachable from every commit since it was added until history is
rewritten (`scripts/purge-sample-blob.sh:3-10`); it warns
`THIS REWRITES EVERY COMMIT FROM THE ONE THAT ADDED THE FILE ONWARDS` (`scripts/purge-sample-blob.sh:12`). At
the reviewed commit the blob is still there: `git rev-list --all --objects | grep mice.mp4` lists
`public/mice.mp4` (2,136,741 bytes), two months after the script arrived with #28. So the script is not dead
code, because the job it was written for is still open and every clone still carries the file; it is also a
one-shot tool that should leave the repository once it has been used, and nothing in `README.md`, `docs/`,
`CHANGELOG.md` or the workflows records whether or when a run is planned. Running it is the opposite of a
behaviour-preserving change: it gives new hashes to every commit after the file was added, invalidates every
clone and open pull request, and needs force-push rights on the default branch (without `-f` the script
stops before the push, `scripts/purge-sample-blob.sh:113-118`, and the hint it prints there is not what a
re-run does: see CL-3). Question for a maintainer: run it once, with
the coordination its header describes, and then delete it (correcting its closing hint meanwhile), or decide against the rewrite and delete
the script now? To confirm the state at any time: `git rev-list --all --objects | grep mice.mp4` prints nothing
after a purge. Confirmed by one reading.

<a name="nv-35"></a>**NV-35: Coverage is 99.9% with no threshold, and some behaviour is executed but pinned by no test.**
`configs/vitest.config.ts:5-8` passes only `rootDir` and `environment` to the shared Vitest factory, which
applies coverage thresholds only when the caller passes them (the shared config's `vitest.js`, lines 44 and
72). CI's last run on this commit reports 99.9% of statements, 98.05% of branches, 99.87% of functions and
99.97% of lines, so a threshold would cost nothing today, and without one a pull request can lower any of
those figures with every check still green. A covered line is not thereby a pinned one. In these places the
suite runs code whose result no assertion reads:

- **Rows of the page and the document.** No test asserts Quietest Window, Peak ÷ Average or File Size on
  either side (`src/ui/inspectTab.ts:95`, `src/ui/inspectTab.ts:201-202`, `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:124`,
  `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:196-197`). Nothing under `tests/` or `stories/` names the first two, and the only
  hit for the third is a different row, Original File Size (`tests/unit/savingsPanel.test.ts:92`), so any of
  those six lines could be deleted with every unit and integration test still passing. The same holds for
  Segment and Quality in the A/B summary (`src/ui/abPanel.ts:143-144`, `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:346-347`). At
  least eleven more rows are asserted on one side only (MIME Type, Encoded Segment Size and Preset in the
  document, for example `tests/unit/analysisTab.test.ts:622`; Overall Bitrate, Display Size, Frames and five
  others on the page, for example `tests/unit/inspectTab.test.ts:486`), and the unit suite has no snapshot
  assertions. This limits how far the suite can vouch for DUP-1.
- **Codec descriptions.** No test reads `description` off a `describeCodec` result, so the thirteen wirings
  of the shape `description: CODEC_DESCRIPTIONS.avc,` (`src/lib/codecKb.ts:53`) and the PCM one
  (`src/lib/codecKb.ts:179`) can be swapped with no unit or integration test failing, as can `fullName` and
  `year`. The nearest assertion is `expect(video.codecInfo).not.toBeNull();`
  (`tests/unit/mediabunnyMeta.test.ts:84`). This bears on CON-7.
- **Tag origins.** Three of the six origin strings (ID3, Vorbis and RIFF, wired at
  `src/lib/metadataTagKb.ts:51-53`) appear in no assertion; the QuickTime, mdta and normalized ones do
  (`tests/unit/metadataTagKb.test.ts:15`, `tests/unit/metadataTagKb.test.ts:31`,
  `tests/unit/inspectTab.test.ts:338`).
- **Reopening the measurement cache.** `src/lib/matrixCache.ts:186-191` forgets a failed open so that the
  next call asks again (`dbPromise = null;`), but the two tests that fail the open fail it on every call
  (`tests/unit/matrixCache.test.ts:368-382`, `tests/unit/matrixCache.test.ts:411-426`) and would pass with
  that line removed. One test that fails the open once and then lets it succeed would pin it.
- **A/B loads.** `tests/unit/abPanel.test.ts` loads the comparison without the `totals` argument in 22 calls
  of its helper (`tests/unit/abPanel.test.ts:82-84`) and 7 direct calls, against 20 calls that pass it; both
  production callers pass it (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:667-670`, `src/ui/encodeTab.ts:273`). Most of that
  file's coverage of `src/ui/abPanel.ts:85` and `src/ui/abPanel.ts:103-104` is therefore of the fallback
  sides, reached through a call shape the app never uses, and nothing asserts the values they produce
  (`encodedSize` is checked once, on the totals path, `tests/unit/abPanel.test.ts:242`). This bears on CON-2.
- **Visual snapshots (one reading).** None draws the Reencode or the Compare Quality panel: the Playwright
  Chromatic test stops at the Inspect tab (`tests/chromatic/app.chromatic.test.ts:19-24`) and the stories
  draw the atom map, the drop zone, the savings panel and the tab bar. The viewport list in the shared test
  package tops out at 1280px wide and no story sets a viewport, so the layout above the 1560px breakpoint
  (`src/style.css:1789`) is drawn by no snapshot either (INFERRED for the stories, whose width Chromatic
  chooses).

Setting thresholds changes what CI enforces, and several findings in this report remove branches that only
tests reach, which moves the percentages slightly; that makes it a decision and not a cleanup. The added
assertions are test-only. Question for a maintainer: pass `thresholds` a little under today's figures (for
example statements 99, branches 97, functions 99, lines 99), and add the assertions above before the
refactors that lean on them (DUP-1, CON-2, CON-7)? To confirm: `npm run test:coverage` prints the figures;
for each gap, delete or swap the cited line and run the named test file, which by this reading still passes.
Confirmed by two independent readings, except the missing threshold and the snapshot coverage, which rest on
one.

<a name="nv-36"></a>**NV-36: CI workflows: three behaviours to confirm on the platform.** Each of these is half OBSERVED in the
repository and half dependent on what GitHub, an action or Chromatic does, which could not be observed for
this review.

- **No workflow names a pull request's `closed` event.** `.github/workflows/preview.yml:3-4` triggers on
  `pull_request` with no `types:`, none of the eight workflows names `closed`, and the deploy job
  deliberately keeps the previews (`clean-exclude: pr-preview/`, `.github/workflows/deploy.yml:40-41`).
  INFERRED: if a bare `pull_request` trigger does not fire on close, and `rossjrw/pr-preview-action@v1`
  (`.github/workflows/preview.yml:31`) removes a preview only on that event, every preview directory under
  `pr-preview/` stays on `gh-pages` for good. Adding `closed` to the trigger would not be enough alone: the
  install and build steps (`.github/workflows/preview.yml:24-28`) would need a guard for that event (this
  last point is from memory of the action's documented usage).
- **The Storybook job pins a runner on which no step starts a browser.**
  `.github/workflows/chromatic-storybook.yml:14-16` pins `ubuntu-24.04`, with a comment that a runner image
  bump moves screenshot pixels with no code change, and `.github/workflows/chromatic-storybook.yml:23-29`
  reports the image. Both are the Playwright workflow's text, line for line
  (`.github/workflows/chromatic-playwright.yml:14-16`, `.github/workflows/chromatic-playwright.yml:23-29`),
  but no step of this job installs or launches a browser (`.github/workflows/chromatic-storybook.yml:45-60`).
  INFERRED: if Chromatic renders a Storybook build in its own browsers, the runner's fonts and rasterizer
  never touch these snapshots and the stated reason does not apply to this job.
- **Storybook's core and framework are on different minors.** The Dependabot update #65 raised `storybook`
  to 10.6.2 (`package.json:44`, `package-lock.json:6005`) and left `@storybook/html-vite`, its builder and
  its HTML renderer at 10.5.6 (`package.json:36`, `package-lock.json:2889`, `package-lock.json:2895-2896`).
  The peer range is satisfied (`package-lock.json:2903`) and the Chromatic (Storybook) workflow succeeded on
  this commit, so nothing fails; whether Storybook expects its packages on one version was not observed.
  `.github/dependabot.yml:3` covers only `github-actions`, so no schedule keeps the npm packages in step. A third copy, 10.2.13, sits under `@chromatic-com/playwright` (`package-lock.json:808`) by that
  package's own choice and is not actionable here.

None of the three is a quality change: the first adds a run on every close that deletes a directory on
`gh-pages`, the second changes a runner label or drops a log line (rewording its two comments alone changes
nothing), and the third is a dependency change. Questions for a maintainer: add a guarded `closed` trigger
and clean the existing directories once? Reword the Storybook job's two comments, or keep pin and step for
symmetry with the Playwright file, which the shared preamble of DUP-10 would want anyway? Align the Storybook
packages at the next dependency pass, and turn on npm version updates in Dependabot? To confirm: after
fetching `gh-pages`, `git ls-tree --name-only origin/gh-pages pr-preview/` lists the previews that remain; a
Chromatic (Storybook) build on a pull request that changes only the runner label shows whether the pin
matters; `npm run build-storybook` and that same workflow cover the third. Confirmed by two independent
readings for the repository half of each; the platform half is inferred, not run.

<a name="nv-37"></a>**NV-37: Consolidations that look attractive and would change behaviour.** Three merges suggest themselves
on a read of the code. Each was traced and none is behaviour-preserving, so they are recorded here to keep
them from being applied as a quality change.

- **One builder and one lifecycle for the run controls.** `engineBox` (`src/ui/formControls.ts:78-96`) and
  `runControls` (`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:98-119`) both assemble a button, `progressBar()`, a `progress-label`
  line and `logConsole()`, and each has its own start, failure and end handling
  (`src/ui/inBrowserEncode.ts:38-43`, `src/ui/inBrowserEncode.ts:74-82`; `src/ui/segmentRun.ts:491-524`).
  They are not the same thing. `engineBox` wraps its nodes in a div with inline margins and adds a result
  block; `runControls` adds Stop and a buttons wrapper. The run lifecycle sets the shared flag
  (`encodeTest.running = true;`, `src/ui/segmentRun.ts:492`) and leaves the bar showing after a success; the
  whole-file encode sets no flag and always hides the bar (`src/ui/inBrowserEncode.ts:81`). A merge would add
  a Stop button to the whole-file encode, change markup and spacing, and put that encode under the running
  flag, which decides which runs exclude each other. The behaviour-preserving part is already in DUP-5 and
  CON-4.
- **One disclosure fold.** There are three `details` builders: `fold()` (`src/lib/dom.ts:124-131`, one
  caller), the output console (`src/ui/formControls.ts:134-142`) and the sweep settings
  (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:109-126`). Their class names are read by code (`src/ui/formControls.ts:147-169`) and
  by tests, and the margins, the summary's padding and font size, the note's alignment, the body wrapper and
  the gear differ from fold to fold. The part they share, caret and hover, is already one rule set
  (`src/style.css:1335-1361`). Building the other two on `fold()` would either flatten those visible
  differences or carry them as options, which removes little.
- **One stylesheet partial for the page and the document.** Serving the 58 shared selectors of DUP-2 from
  one imported file changes source order. `src/style.css:4` notes that imports must come first, so the
  partial's `.badge` rule (`display: inline-block;`, `src/style.css:695-696`) would land ahead of `.info`
  (`display: inline-flex;`, `src/style.css:609-611`) and flip the equal-specificity tie that keeps the
  B-frames badge, which carries both classes (`src/ui/seekTab.ts:39-43`), an inline block. INFERRED from the
  cascade, not rendered.

A maintainer who wants one of these is choosing a redesign with visible effects: markup and spacing, which
runs exclude each other, how a badge lays out. No visual snapshot draws the Reencode or Compare Quality panel
(NV-35), so only the unit tests and a person looking would notice a slip. Question for a maintainer: is any
of the three wanted as a redesign in its own right? If one is attempted, the least that shows a change is
`npm test`, `npm run build` and `npm run test:integration`, plus a visual review of the three tabs. Confirmed
by two independent readings; the stylesheet case is inferred from the cascade.

<a name="nv-38"></a>**NV-38: Nearly every duplicated test helper arrived two days after the fixtures were consolidated.** The
clean-up in #39 (913aa8f, 2026-09-03) added four shared modules, `tests/fixtures/state.ts`,
`tests/fixtures/samples.ts`, `tests/fixtures/sizeEstimate.ts` and `tests/fixtures/demoPaths.ts` (the folder
itself dates from #28), under the commit heading "Shared the fixtures the unit tests were each redeclaring",
and `tests/fixtures/state.ts:1-2` still states that purpose. At that commit the duplication described in
DUP-7 barely existed: `function setUrl(` was defined in one test file, the flush helper in one, and only the
box builder twice. The two pull requests of 2026-09-05 that brought coverage to 99.9%, #44 (8176e6e) and #45
(9475fde), then added 6 and 20 definitions: counting with `git grep` the helper names DUP-7 discusses gives
10 at #39, 16 after #44, 36 after #45 and 36 today (a count of those names, not a census of every helper).
Nothing has been added since, and nothing in `CLAUDE.md`, `README.md` or `docs/` says where shared test data
and helpers live or that one should be looked for before another is written. So DUP-7 can be applied and the
next batch of tests can undo it, as the last one did within two days; a convention or a guard that prevents
this is a rule about how tests are written here (a guard would also change what CI rejects), not a quality
change. Question for a maintainer: does the project want one stated line (for example, that shared unit-test
data and helpers live in `tests/fixtures` and `tests/unit/helpers` and are looked for there before one is
added to a test file), or a lint guard, so that DUP-7 stays fixed? To confirm the history:
`git grep -c "function setUrl(" 913aa8f 8176e6e 9475fde HEAD -- tests` lists one file at the first commit
and four at each of the others. Confirmed by two independent readings.

<a name="nv-39"></a>**NV-39: Renames and rewordings that a user, a snapshot or a test can see.** CL-4 and CL-9 rename and reword
only what nothing outside the code can observe. The rest of the same residue is visible somewhere, so each
piece is a decision:

- **Em-dashes in text a user sees.** Ten strings on thirteen lines: the chroma explainer
  (`src/lib/explainers.ts:483-484`), the Full Analysis introduction (`src/lib/explainers.ts:731-733`), the
  Markdown chart placeholder (`src/lib/analysisDoc.ts:121`), two crash messages
  (`src/lib/ffmpegEngine.ts:285`, `src/lib/ffmpegEngine.ts:294`), the atom readout
  (`src/ui/atomsTab.ts:222`) and four matrix square labels (`src/ui/matrixPanel.ts:80`,
  `src/ui/matrixPanel.ts:100`, `src/ui/matrixPanel.ts:112`, `src/ui/matrixPanel.ts:124`). Four test lines
  carry the same dash and pin the readout and the square labels (`tests/unit/atomsTab.test.ts:104`,
  `tests/unit/atomsTab.test.ts:204`, `tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts:226`,
  `tests/unit/matrixPanel.test.ts:149`), and the placeholder is written into the exported Markdown.
- **Story names, a log line and help text.** Four story names carry an em-dash
  (`stories/AtomMap.stories.ts:44`, `stories/AtomMap.stories.ts:49`, `stories/Savings.stories.ts:56`,
  `stories/Savings.stories.ts:61`). They are the names of Chromatic snapshots, so rewording them is INFERRED
  to open new baselines to accept. One workflow echo carries one too
  (`.github/workflows/version-check.yml:28`, in the branch DC-10 removes), as do five comment lines that the
  scripts print for `-h` (`scripts/generate-demos.sh:4`, `scripts/generate-demos.sh:16`,
  `scripts/generate-demos.sh:27`, `scripts/generate-demos.sh:29`, `scripts/purge-sample-blob.sh:14`; see
  NV-33).
- **Three DOM ids.** `etDuration` and `etSegments` (built from the prefix passed at
  `src/ui/compareTab.ts:92` to `src/ui/segmentRun.ts:67-68`) and `etUpscaleView` (`src/ui/abPanel.ts:408`)
  keep the prefix of Encode Test, a tab label replaced by Compare Quality in #12. No stylesheet, label or
  story refers to them, but tests select by them (for example `tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts:380-381`,
  `tests/unit/abPanel.test.ts:464`), and the assertions that older ids are absent
  (`tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts:306-310`) would become vacuous under a new prefix.
- **Three in-core file names.** `et_in`, `et_snip_` and `et_out_` (`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:131`,
  `src/ui/segmentRun.ts:171`, `src/ui/segmentRun.ts:284`) are printed in the Output console with each
  command (`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:247`, `src/ui/segmentRun.ts:303`) and pinned by 25 test lines: 23 in
  `tests/unit/segmentRun.test.ts:328-616`, plus `tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts:722` and
  `tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts:1056`. Stored measurements are not involved, because cache keys are built
  from the command with its default file names (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:385`).
- **One concept, several words.** Most labels say segment (`src/ui/abPanel.ts:189`, pinned at
  `tests/unit/abPanel.test.ts:299`), three A/B strings and one error say stretch
  (`src/ui/abPanel.ts:256-257`, `src/ui/abPanel.ts:386`, `src/ui/segmentRun.ts:410`), and the Compare tab
  says both sweep (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:115`) and matrix (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:306`,
  `src/ui/compareTab.ts:748`).
- **`is_sync`.** `SampleInfo` keeps mp4box's snake_case key beside `ctsSec` (`src/lib/types.ts:63`). A
  rename is invisible to users but not local: `tests/unit/mp4boxParser.test.ts:270` asserts the key by name
  and fixture literals in `tests/` and `stories/` carry it, none of them type-checked (NV-30). It is moot if
  DC-2 removes the field.

None of this is a quality change: each edit alters text on the page, in an export, in the console, in a log
or in a script's help, a snapshot's name, or what a test selects or asserts by name. Question for a
maintainer: reword the ten strings, the four story names and the help lines in one pass with the four test
lines that pin them, choose one user-facing word each for segment or stretch and for sweep or matrix, and
rename the ids and file names along with the `encodeTest` rename of CL-4, or leave them as they are? To
confirm after any of it: `npm test`, and for the story names the Chromatic (Storybook) build on the pull
request. Confirmed by two independent readings.

### Smaller questions

<a name="nv-40"></a>**NV-40: Smaller questions, most from a single reading.** Short observations that did not get a second,
independent check unless the bullet says so. Acting on any of them changes behaviour, a test's expectation or
a published setting, which is why they are questions and not findings.

- **A failed touch loses the recalled stretches and keeps the measurements (two readings).** `recallWindows`
  hands back its stretches only once `await touch(db, WINDOWS, [record]);` has succeeded
  (`src/lib/matrixCache.ts:284-288`), while `readMeasurements` returns what it found whether or not its touch
  lands (`src/lib/matrixCache.ts:249-253`). On a store that reads but refuses writes (reach INFERRED, and
  narrow), a reloaded page then draws fresh stretches (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:356-360`), every cached
  measurement misses and the sweep is encoded again. `tests/unit/matrixCache.test.ts:398-407` asserts both
  outcomes under a title that names only the first; returning the stretches regardless would flip
  `tests/unit/matrixCache.test.ts:406`. Intended?
- **Two guards that are not there (two readings).** `.github/workflows/version-check.yml:35` tests only that
  the version differs from the base (`if [ "$PR_VERSION" = "$BASE_VERSION" ]; then`), so a lowered version
  passes. `package.json` does not mark the package private and no workflow publishes, so nothing blocks an
  accidental `npm publish`.
- **Four assertions depend on the developer's locale (two readings).** `tests/unit/atomsTab.test.ts:104`,
  `tests/unit/atomsTab.test.ts:110`, `tests/unit/atomsTab.test.ts:204` and
  `tests/unit/inspectTab.test.ts:444` hard-code English digit grouping for strings made by an argument-less
  `toLocaleString()` (`src/ui/atomsTab.ts:210`, `src/ui/atomsTab.ts:222`, `src/ui/inspectTab.ts:221`), so
  `npm test` is INFERRED to fail where the locale groups digits differently (a German locale gives 97.968
  where the first of them expects 97,968; that example rests on one reading). `tests/unit/format.test.ts:104`
  shows the neutral form.
- **Mixed number conventions (one reading).** Rounded byte sizes always use a point as the decimal mark,
  while counts, exact byte counts and offsets follow the user's locale (`src/ui/atomsTab.ts:204`,
  `src/ui/atomsTab.ts:210`, `src/ui/atomsTab.ts:222`, `src/ui/atomsTab.ts:226`, `src/ui/seekTab.ts:33-34`), so
  in German one readout (`src/ui/atomsTab.ts:208-211`) is INFERRED to use the point both ways. The exported
  Full Analysis document keeps whatever locale the exporter had (`src/ui/analysisTab.ts:106`,
  `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:215`, `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:279-280`, `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:306`). Fix one
  locale, or leave it to the browser?
- **A link with tab=report has no alias (one reading).** The value was linkable for three days in August
  2026, from #12 until #17 renamed the tab to analysis. The alias table holds only atoms, seek and reencode
  (`src/lib/appUrl.ts:25-29`), so such a link opens Inspect, although the comment at
  `src/lib/appUrl.ts:21-22` says a sent link opens where its content went.
- **Two lines that are covered and not pinned (one reading each).** `src/ui/compareTab.ts:730` scales each
  stretch by the measured length, but every sweep in `tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts` runs over one stretch
  (the helper's default at `tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts:180`), where a version that gave every stretch the
  measured total would also pass, and be five times out over five. `tests/unit/analysisTab.test.ts:550-577`
  never invokes the observer's callback, so the re-fit on resize at `src/ui/analysisTab.ts:498` could become
  a no-op unnoticed.
- **A hand reset placed after the assertions (one reading).** `tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts:688-689` puts
  `cli.audioMode` and `cli.faststart` back at the end of the test body, so a failure at
  `tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts:685-687` skips the reset and both values leak into every later test of the
  file. CON-9 removes the hazard.
- **Two details of the demos page (one reading each).** `min-width: 176px;` (`src/style.css:2258`) is 6px
  under the 182px that the flex-basis on the line above gives a theme with one tile, so such a card can be
  squeezed narrower than its tile before it wraps (INFERRED from the box model, not rendered). The guard at
  `src/ui/demosPage.ts:257` cannot be false, because `open()` sets the value at `src/ui/demosPage.ts:316`
  before the page can be closed; it is the uncovered branch CI reports for that line, and it still narrows
  `string | null` for the assignment, so deleting it alone would not compile.

Question for a maintainer, for each: intended, or worth an issue? Every bullet can be confirmed from the
lines it cites; none was run.

## Recommended order of changes

Two facts about this repository shape the order. `.github/workflows/version-check.yml:7-11` makes any pull
request that touches `src/**`, `configs/**`, `package.json` or `package-lock.json` bump the version, and
`CLAUDE.md` asks for a changelog entry with each bump, which then appears in the What's New modal. Source
cleanups are therefore cheapest in a few larger pull requests, worded once for users. `tests/`, `stories/`,
`index.html`, `docs/`, `scripts/` and the workflows are outside that gate and can move in small steps.

Run the baseline first and keep its numbers: `npm run typecheck && npm run format:check && npm run lint &&
npm test` should report 53 test files and 959 tests passing, as CI did on this commit.

<!-- prettier-ignore -->
| Step | Findings | Verification for the step | Why here |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1. Comment-only and text-only edits | CL-1, CL-2, CL-3, CL-9 (comments), CL-10, CL-12 (the two `CLAUDE.md` rows) | `npm run format:check && npm run lint && npm test`: counts unchanged | Cannot change behaviour, and removes misdirection before anyone reads the code for the later steps. The parts under `src/` and `configs/` (CL-1, CL-2, the `src/` comments of CL-9 and two rows of CL-3) need one version bump. |
| 2. Tests only | DC-9, DUP-7, DUP-8, CON-9, CON-10, CL-6, CL-7, CL-8 | `npm run format:check && npm run lint && npm test`: the count moves only by what each finding states (DUP-8 and CL-7 delete tests, CL-6 can delete up to three as an alternative, CL-6 and CL-7 add cases, DC-9's alternatives can add one), and each test strengthened under CL-6 or CL-7 is first seen to fail against the change it names | Outside the version gate, and it strengthens the suite every later step relies on. DUP-8's load-failure pair in `tests/unit/ffmpegEngine.test.ts` waits for CON-3 (step 7), which settles both in one pass. |
| 3. Tooling, CI and unreferenced files | DC-8, DC-10, DUP-10, CL-11, CL-12 (the footer attributes and the Playwright install step) | `npm run typecheck && npm run lint && npm run format:check && npm test && npm run build`, then `npm run test:integration` and `npm run test:chromatic`; on the pull request, the five workflows that run for its branch, Version Check where it touches a gated path, and the Chromatic (Playwright) build; the deploy workflow on its first run after the merge (DUP-10) | No source behaviour is involved. DC-8 (files under `src/assets/`) and two rows of DC-10 (`package.json` and `src/vite-env.d.ts`) are inside the version gate, so they share step 4's bump or take their own. |
| 4. Dead code in `src/` | DC-1 to DC-6 | `npm run typecheck && npm run lint && npm test && npm run build && npm run test:integration`: the test count falls by what DC-1 to DC-3 state, since the tests of deleted code go with it | Deleting first shrinks what the later steps have to touch. Decide the last bullet of NV-10 before DC-1. |
| 5. Stylesheets | DC-7, DUP-2, DUP-3 | `npm test` (DUP-2 and the first half of DUP-3 each add one unit test), `npm run build` and `npm run format:check`; then `npm run test:chromatic` and the Chromatic diff, plus a look at what no snapshot draws: the Reencode and Compare panels, the seeking test's fold on Inspect, and the Full Analysis preview with its print preview | DC-7 and the regrouping in DUP-3 need visual verification, which is separate from the unit suite. The two equality tests change no shipped byte and can go with step 2. |
| 6. Local duplication, names and idioms | DUP-4, DUP-5, DUP-11, CL-4, CL-5 | `npm run typecheck && npm run lint && npm run format:check && npm test && npm run build && npm run test:integration` | Mostly small, independent edits; the `encodeTest` rename in CL-4 is wide and takes a commit of its own. `git grep` for each old name afterwards: no static check reads `tests/` (NV-30), so a stale name there shows only when its test runs, and a stale type-only import not at all (CL-4). |
| 7. Structural consolidation | DUP-1, DUP-6, DUP-9, CON-1 to CON-8 | The full local suite, `npm run test:integration` and both Chromatic jobs | The largest diffs, safest once the tests pin more (step 2) and the dead paths are gone (step 4). |

Deliberately not scheduled:

- Everything under Needs verification. Each item changes behaviour or needs a decision. NV-1 to NV-9 are
  defects in run state and deserve a decision before step 6, because DUP-5 (one Run handler, step 6), CON-1
  (`matrix.running`) and CON-4 (both step 7) touch the same code and are the natural place for the fixes.
- The optional rows: the explicit `complexity` and `environment` pins in CL-11, the Playwright configs in
  DUP-10, the favicon in DUP-11, and the rows their findings call low value or lowest priority.
- A rule to keep each theme fixed is suggested where one exists: type-checking `tests/` (NV-30) would have
  caught the drifted mirror behind CON-9 and the uncast literal in CL-8, and would report the stale fixture
  keys and names that several findings say pass silently today (it would not see the dead setup of DC-9,
  which compiles); a coverage threshold (NV-35) holds the number while dead branches are removed; an equality
  test holds the two dark palettes (DUP-3) and the shared selectors (DUP-2); a Node version file removes six
  pins (DUP-10).

## Not changed (deliberately)

Things that look like findings and are not. Each was traced like a finding and kept, for the reason given.

- **Exports that only tests import.** 31 of the 319 exports under `src/` have no importer outside `tests/`: 26
  are still used inside their own module on a path production reaches (for example `renderDemoBrowser`,
  exported at `src/ui/demosPage.ts:98` and called at `src/ui/demosPage.ts:269`) and were left exported as
  test seams when #39 dropped the export from every symbol that no other module, test or story imported; three
  more, `ensureFfmpegInput`, `deleteFfmpegFile` and `runFfmpegArgs`, are the forwarders CON-3 inlines; the
  other two are `resetFfmpeg` (DC-5) and `htmlToPlainText` (DC-1). `FileLoader.loadFile`
  (`src/ui/fileLoading.ts:215`) is the same kind of seam: no production caller today (DUP-5 would give it
  one), and the awaitable way in for the unit tests, since the picker and drop handlers discard the promise
  (`src/ui/fileLoading.ts:173`, `src/ui/fileLoading.ts:203`).
- **`src/main.ts` empties and assembles the Inspect panel itself** (`src/main.ts:31-38`), where the other
  three panels are emptied by their own renderers. The arrangement is deliberate and documented at both ends
  (`src/main.ts:26-30`, `src/ui/inspectTab.ts:233-235`); a wrapper module would move the five calls, not
  remove a concept.
- **Two lines in `src/main.ts` that look redundant.** `src/main.ts:41` renders Full Analysis on every load
  although the tab re-renders on each visit (`src/ui/tabs.ts:17-19`): it is what fills the panel when a link
  with both `tab=analysis` and `src` finishes loading with that panel already showing.
  `void matrixCache.clear().finally(` at `src/main.ts:55` needs no catch, because `clear()` goes through a
  queue that swallows every error and so cannot reject (`src/lib/matrixCache.ts:196-205`,
  `src/lib/matrixCache.ts:307-314`).
- **`drainWithPool` is not the shared `runQueue`** (`src/lib/ffmpegPool.ts:60-65`). It hands each lane its own
  ffmpeg worker and takes a stop check; the shared queue has neither.
- **The Educational switch is not the shared `createFlagStore`** (`src/lib/educational.ts:16-23`). It defaults
  to on and stores "true" or "false", where the shared store defaults to off and stores "1" or nothing, so a
