  swap would misread choices already stored. The try/catch around storage is a separate matter, in DUP-4.
- **`RunStopped` is not the shared `InterruptedError`** (`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:464-470`). The shared helpers
  work from an `AbortSignal`, which the app does not use, and the message "Stopped" is pinned by tests
  (`tests/unit/segmentRun.test.ts:438`).
- **The `!state.source` guards in the Inspect renderers** (`src/ui/inspectTab.ts:238`,
  `src/ui/inspectTab.ts:247`) can fire in the app. One route: with a file loaded, a failed load brings the
  drop zone back with the panels still shown (the catch at `src/ui/fileLoading.ts:119-123` never hides them);
  a URL load that then fails before the source is set (`src/ui/fileLoading.ts:65`,
  `src/ui/fileLoading.ts:76-79`) leaves them shown with no source; and the next Educational flip re-renders
  into both guards (`src/main.ts:89`).
- **Two guards that cannot fire from `src/main.ts` in a browser.** `typeof ResizeObserver === "undefined"`
  (`src/ui/atomsTab.ts:321`) is what lets the jsdom unit tests draw the map without a stub, and the
  empty-headings return in the contents nav (`src/ui/inspectToc.ts:22`) is pinned by
  `tests/unit/inspectToc.test.ts:65-71`.
- **`showMiniLoaded` is called twice in one load** (`src/ui/fileLoading.ts:81`, `src/ui/fileLoading.ts:118`).
  The first call puts the size beside the stage text while the file is parsed; the second restores the name
  when the load is done.
- **`DemoLoader` beside `FileLoader`** (`src/ui/demosPage.ts:35-38`) is a deliberate narrowing to the one
  method the demos page needs, which is also what lets its test pass an object with `loadUrl` alone
  (`tests/unit/demosPage.test.ts:325`).
- **`src/ui/zoomPan.ts` registers window listeners at import** (`src/ui/zoomPan.ts:25-39`). By design: one
  pair serves every comparison, where a pair per render would accumulate (`src/ui/zoomPan.ts:5-6`).
- **The tag filter drops object values**, so a normalized date that arrived as a `Date` would not be listed
  (`src/ui/inspectTab.ts:66`). That matches the filter's stated scalar-only rule (`src/ui/inspectTab.ts:53-55`)
  and the knowledge base, which has no entry for a normalized date, and the date still shows under its raw
  key. That the media library hands the date over as a `Date` is inferred.
- **The exact-key guard in the tag index never skips** (`src/lib/metadataTagKb.ts:60`): the 90 seeds hold no
  repeated key, which makes it the uncovered branch CI reports for that file. It keeps exact lookup
  first-wins like the upper-case guard two lines below it (`src/lib/metadataTagKb.ts:62`), which does skip,
  and without it a future duplicate would become last-wins with no test to notice.
- **`demo-open` and `demos-error` have no CSS** (`src/ui/demosPage.ts:163`, `src/ui/demosPage.ts:224`). They
  are hooks that the unit and Playwright tests select (`tests/integration/demoArchive.ts:187`,
  `tests/integration/demos.spec.ts:139`), so they are not among the unused classes of DC-6.
- **The document stylesheet repeats the page's class names and atom palette**
  (`src/lib/analysisDoc.ts:203-208`, `src/lib/analysisDoc.ts:382-385`). An exported file has no app around it
  to take a theme from, and the comment says so; DUP-2 proposes a test that holds the two equal, not a merge.
- **`src/lib/settings.ts` holds one constant** (`src/lib/settings.ts:2`). `configs/vite.config.ts:5` imports
  it under Node, so it has to stay free of DOM and app imports.
- **Two things in `src/lib/qualityMatrix.ts` that look like copies or redundancy.** `MATRIX_SCALES` and
  `MATRIX_SCALERS` (`src/lib/qualityMatrix.ts:70`, `src/lib/qualityMatrix.ts:73`) are spreads of the
  dropdown's lists, not hand-kept copies. In `fpsForFraction` (`src/lib/qualityMatrix.ts:102`) the first
  test narrows null and undefined and the second rejects negatives.
- **Two things in `src/lib/cliCommand.ts`.** The `filters` array holds at most one entry
  (`src/lib/cliCommand.ts:122-125`) and is there so that the command carries a single `-vf`, as the comment
  above it explains. The trailing flush in `formatCliCommand` (`src/lib/cliCommand.ts:146`) fires only for an
  empty argument list, which `tests/unit/cliCommand.test.ts:219-221` pins.
- **`src/ui/matrixPanel.ts` builds its own table** (`src/ui/matrixPanel.ts:162-163`) and does not use
  `dataTable`. Its cells are buttons under a two-row header, and `dataTable` takes text cells only
  (`src/lib/dom.ts:134`).
- **`savingsBar` turns a percentage into a string, a number and a string again**
  (`src/ui/savingsPanel.ts:32-35`). The band's left edge and width are derived from the already rounded
  percentages; a standalone model of the arithmetic found that working from unrounded shares gives a
  different width in 1350 of 5599 sampled cases.
- **Two things in `src/ui/abPanel.ts`.** The reel helpers read `encodeTest.abSegments` live while drawing
  uses the list captured at load (`src/ui/abPanel.ts:160-184`), which is what
  `tests/unit/abPanel.test.ts:440-451` pins for a panel whose file was reset under it. The ruler and the
  segment buttons are built and not attached for a single stretch, as their docstrings say
  (`src/ui/abPanel.ts:209-211`, `src/ui/abPanel.ts:253-254`).
- **`matrixSettingsOpen` lives at module scope** (`src/ui/compareTab.ts:63-67`), not in shared state, so that
  the settings fold stays open across the panel rebuild the Educational switch triggers;
  `tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts:493-502` pins it.
- **`hashName` sits beside the cache's FNV hash** (`src/ui/segmentRun.ts:175-179`). It is a different
  algorithm with a different output, and its result is part of the snippet names printed in the console, so
  sharing one hash would change what a user sees.
- **`lastUsed` on both stored records and `key` on the stored windows are never read by name**
  (`src/lib/matrixCache.ts:88`, `src/lib/matrixCache.ts:92`, `src/lib/matrixCache.ts:95`). IndexedDB reads
  them: `key` is the key path and `lastUsed` the index that eviction walks (`src/lib/matrixCache.ts:180-181`).
- **The 1560px breakpoint is written three times** (`src/style.css:1789`, `src/style.css:2184`,
  `src/style.css:2235`). A media query cannot read a custom property, and each use sits under a comment that
  ties it to the fixed watermarks.
- **The app keeps its own colour palette** (`src/style.css:1-6`) and does not import the shared tokens.
  Deliberate: the shared tokens file says in its header that this app keeps its own darker dark palette and
  that the shell stylesheet reads only tokens that palette defines.
- **Shell markup is pasted into `index.html`** (the watermark at `index.html:15-24`, the What's New dialog at
  `index.html:257-264`, and the theme toggle and footer likewise). The shared UI package's README says each
  piece exists twice by design, as a fragment to paste and as a DOM builder, and the two fragments compared
  for this review (the watermark and the dialog) match the package's.
- **Three tests in `tests/unit/dom.test.ts` call only the shared UI package** (`tests/unit/dom.test.ts:14-18`,
  `tests/unit/dom.test.ts:199-207`, `tests/unit/dom.test.ts:287-299`). The comment at
  `tests/unit/dom.test.ts:12-13` states the intent, and the third is the only pin of the Copied! flash that
  the app's Copy Markdown button shows; at most, the default-size assertion at `tests/unit/dom.test.ts:205`
  could move onto the real reset button, which passes 15 (`src/ui/fileLoading.ts:169`).
- **Two literals that tests spell out where an import looks possible.** The integration mock writes the
  archive URL in full (`tests/integration/demoArchive.ts:14`): the literal is what pins which archive and
  dandiset the app talks to, since an imported constant would follow a mistake in the source, and the
  source's constant ends in a slash (`src/lib/demoArchive.ts:112`), so a straight import would build a route
  pattern that matches nothing. The store and index names in `tests/unit/matrixCache.test.ts:119-120` are
  written out because the test seeds the database before the cache opens, and the names are private to the
  module (`src/lib/matrixCache.ts:40-42`).
- **Assertions that removed ids are absent** (`tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts:306-310`,
  `tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts:379`). Nothing in `src/` emits those ids any more, but each sits under a
  comment and they are regression guards for controls that were taken out on purpose.
- **A comment says one core beside a mock of two CPUs** (`tests/unit/compareTab.test.ts:279-280`). It is
  right: the pool leaves one CPU to the page (`src/lib/ffmpegPool.ts:37-40`), so two CPUs give one core.
- **`tests/unit/helpers/mainHarness.ts` is a thin wrapper** (`tests/unit/helpers/mainHarness.ts:1-6`). The
  import of `src/main` has to stay in the app's own code so that Vitest transforms it and a test file's
  mocks apply, as its comment says and the shared test package's own comment confirms.
- **`LICENSE` and `LICENSES/MIT.txt` are the same text.** The second is where the REUSE layout keeps a
  licence (`REUSE.toml:7`); the first is the file GitHub's licence detection reads (inferred).
- **The archive's coordinates are written in the shell scripts and in TypeScript**
  (`scripts/generate-demos.sh:49-50`, `scripts/upload-demos.sh:26-27`, `src/lib/demoArchive.ts:20-22`). A
  shell script cannot import TypeScript, and the source's comment names the script it has to agree with.

## Method & limits

**Nothing from the repository was executed.** The review procedure asks for the maintainer's consent before
running a repository's own tooling. That question was put and had not been answered when this report was
written, so the review is read-only analysis: no `npm ci`, no type check, lint, test, build, `knip`, `jscpd` or
plugin-based linter run, and no script under `scripts/`. Every "would still pass" and every statement about what
a browser draws is a reading of the code, marked INFERRED where it rests on browser or library behaviour.
Findings whose safety would normally be confirmed by a run say so in their confidence line.

**Baseline, taken from CI instead of a local run.** The Lint workflow's last run on this commit (run
40513805310, job 122222323198, 2026-10-27, head `8ecf060`) passed its type check, format check and lint steps and
reported `Test Files 53 passed (53)` and `Tests 959 passed (959)`, with coverage of 99.9% statements, 98.05%
branches, 99.87% functions and 99.97% lines. The other workflows on the same commit were green: Test
(integration) 40513805132, Deploy 40513805271, Chromatic (Playwright) 40513805207 and Chromatic (Storybook) 40513805155. The verification commands named under each finding should reproduce those numbers before a change
and after it; a finding that deletes or adds tests names them.

**Tools used.** All were the review's own and none loads the repository's configuration or plugins:

- a TypeScript language-service script (TypeScript 6.0.2 from the review environment, with its own compiler
  options) for the import graph, references to every export, unused locals, parameters and imports, and
  read and write counts of type members;
- a token-based clone finder, a stylesheet class and id usage script, and string and class listers (Python and
  Node, standard library only);
- `git log -S`, `git blame` and `git show` over the full history (69 commits) to date each item;
- the published tarballs of `@brain-bbqs/config` 0.2.1, `@brain-bbqs/ui` 0.3.0, `@brain-bbqs/utils` 0.2.0,
  `@brain-bbqs/test-utils` 0.3.0 and `@brain-bbqs/ember-client` 0.2.0, read as data (checksums matched
  `package-lock.json`), since `node_modules` was not installed.

The mechanical sweep came back clean on the points a tool can decide: no orphan source file, no export without a
reference outside its own file other than framework entry points, no unused local, parameter or import, every
one of the 242 classes in `src/style.css` named somewhere in the source, all 29 ids in `index.html` used, no
reversed literal assertion, no lint, type or coverage suppression, no `TODO` or `FIXME`. `jscpd` and `knip` were
not run; the clone finder and the reference script stand in for them, and are cruder.

**What was read, and how.** Every file under `src/` (55 TypeScript files, 11,149 lines), `index.html` and all 53
unit test files were read in full at least once; `src/style.css` (2,508 lines) and the document stylesheet were
read in full in a separate pass against the DOM the code builds; the fixtures, integration and Chromatic tests,
stories, scripts, workflows, configuration files, `CLAUDE.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `README.md` and
`docs/user_tests/template.md` were read in full. The three filled-in records under `docs/user_tests/` were
searched, not read as a whole. The check of each test's title against its body covered the 12 largest unit test
files (393 `it()` blocks) test by test; five more rows, in the samplePicker, ffmpegPool, format and containerKb
tests, were met while reading for other findings; the other files were read for duplication and dead setup but
not test by test, so CL-6 and CL-7 are a floor, not a census. `mediabunny`, `mp4box` and `@ffmpeg/*` were not available to read, so
statements about their behaviour are marked INFERRED.

**How claims were checked.** Each candidate was traced a second time, independently and with the aim of
refuting it, before it was written down: 328 candidates (595 separate claims, counting their parts), of which
176 stood as stated, 151 were corrected or narrowed (the corrections are what this report states) and one was
refuted. Eight were then dropped as true but not worth a change, and the candidates rejected as intentional or
load-bearing are listed under Not changed. Twelve run-state and encode items (behind NV-1 to NV-3, NV-5, NV-7,
NV-9 and NV-20 to NV-22) had a third reading, which agreed with the second on every one (the items themselves
state the two readings every item had); the third reading planned for the page-side items was not run. Every section was then drafted from that evidence and read once
more against the code by a separate reviewer before it went in. Eleven items are this reviewer's own and did not
go through the refutation pass; they were re-checked against the tree on 2026-11-18 and read again when their
sections were drafted and reviewed: the unreferenced icons (DC-8), the type reference in DC-10, the favicon
(DUP-11), the CI blocks and Playwright configs (DUP-10), the dark palette (DUP-3), the story drift (DUP-9), four
comments in CL-2 and CL-3, the em-dash counts (CL-9), the purge script (NV-34) and the coverage threshold
(NV-35). NV-40 lists observations that arose late and says for each how many readings it has had. A few items
were also checked with small stand-alone models written for this review from the cited lines (among them the
pool's loop, the tab lookup, the number formatters, the savings bar's rounding, the engine's argument limit and
the size-estimate arithmetic); each such item says so, and a model is not a run of the repository's code.

**What could not be verified.** Anything that needs a browser (layout, cascade outcomes, user activation, file
input behaviour), anything that needs the platform (GitHub Actions triggers, Chromatic, Dependabot), the content
of the published demo set, and anything a compiler would decide about `tests/` (the review's own static pass ran
with third-party modules unresolved, so it can show an error that involves only the repository's own types and
cannot rule one in or out where a `vitest`, `mediabunny` or Playwright type is involved).

**Layout.** Each table is preceded by a prettier-ignore comment, which stops the formatter from padding its
columns: padded, this file was about 600 KB, and GitHub showed it as plain text instead of rendering it. Each
Needs verification item carries a named anchor (nv-1 to nv-40) so that the contents list can link to it.

**Dates and place.** The review was carried out between 2026-11-15 and 2026-11-22 against `8ecf060`, which was
the head of `main` throughout. This report is meant to be kept at
`docs/code-reviews/2026-11-22-code-quality-review.md`; a later review should start by re-verifying each item
here against the code at that time.
